# AGENTS.md

Instructions for AI coding agents working in this repo. Cross-tool standard ([agents.md](https://agents.md/)) — read by most coding agents (Cursor, Codex, Copilot, Claude Code, …). Tools that only read `CLAUDE.md`: create a local, gitignored `CLAUDE.md` containing `See @AGENTS.md`. Also read `AGENTS.local.md` if present — it holds instructions specific to the current host or developer.

## Project

Bull Bitcoin Mobile: self-custodial Bitcoin + Liquid + Lightning wallet. Flutter/Dart, BLoC, Drift/SQLite, GoRouter, get_it DI. See [README.md](README.md) for product details.

**Repo doc map:** [README.md](README.md) = product · [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md) = layer model & design rules · **AGENTS.md** (this file) = toolchain, conventions, commit/test rules · [FEATURES.md](FEATURES.md) = cross-feature dependency graph.

**Composition root** (where to wire a new feature): `lib/main.dart` → `lib/bootstrap.dart` (`startApp()` → startup wizard → `Bull.init()` → `runApp`) + `lib/init.dart` (service initialization) + `lib/app.dart` (root widget) → `lib/locator.dart` (get_it; `AppLocator.setup` registers core, then every `<Feature>Locator.setup`) → `lib/router.dart` (GoRouter; `AppRouter.router` spreads each `<Feature>Router`'s routes). A new feature is added in those last two files.

## Toolchain — non-negotiable

- **Always `fvm flutter` / `fvm dart`.** Never bare `flutter`/`dart`. The pinned SDK lives in [`.fvmrc`](.fvmrc); using a global SDK silently breaks builds.
- **Use the makefile.** Don't reinvent commands:
  - `make deps` — `fvm flutter pub get --enforce-lockfile`
  - `make analyze` — `fvm flutter analyze --fatal-warnings --fatal-infos` (matches CI; same check the pre-commit hook runs)
  - `make checks` — local Dart/Flutter checks: analyze + `make bull-ui-check` + `make custody-check` + `make fix-check` + `make format-check` + unit tests. Node-based custody import checks run only in CI
  - `make bootstrap` — melos workspace bootstrap (wraps `fvm dart run melos bootstrap`)
  - `make build-runner` — codegen (freezed, json_serializable, drift, flutter_gen)
  - `make translations` — `fvm flutter gen-l10n`
  - `make setup` — full first-time setup (clean + deps + build-runner + translations + hooks + ios pods on macOS)
  - `make unit-test` / `make integration-test` / `make test`
  - `make unit-test-shard SHARD=<name>` — one shard of the unit tests, as a job of the CI's `tests` matrix runs it (shards in [tools/test_shards.sh](tools/test_shards.sh): `app-1` and `app-2` (the app's test files in two parts), `p2p-1` to `p2p-3` (features/p2p's in three), `packages` (every other member)); `make unit-test-shards-check` fails unless the shards together run exactly what `make unit-test` runs
  - `make p2p-screen-captures` — the P2P screen captures that `make unit-test` skips, into `P2P_CAPTURE_DIR` (must be set)
  - `make drift-migrations` — after changing Drift schema
  - `make android` / `make android release` — reproducible build
  - `make android beta` — beta tester channel APK (`.beta` applicationId, installs alongside production)
  - `make verify` — verify a built APK matches a published release
  - `make build-runner-watch` — codegen in watch mode during dev
- **Analyze the whole project, never specific files.** `fvm flutter analyze` (no path arg). CI runs `--fatal-warnings --fatal-infos` ([analyze_and_test.yml](.github/workflows/analyze_and_test.yml)) — match it locally.
- **`fvm dart fix --dry-run` must print `Nothing to fix!`** before commit. If not, run `fvm dart fix --apply` and stage the result.
- **Pre-commit hook is the floor.** It runs analyze + dart fix dry-run ([`.git_hooks/pre-commit`](.git_hooks/pre-commit)). **Never `--no-verify`.** If it fails, fix the cause.

## Database schema migration support

- Every database schema version shipped in a published release is immutable and must remain a supported upgrade source through the migration chain; `main` generally archives that published truth.
- Before the next release, `develop` keeps exactly one pending schema revision: the latest published schema plus 1. Aggregate all unreleased schema changes into that same next migration and snapshot; never bump once per feature or commit.
- Intermediate schema states that existed only in `develop` are not compatibility targets. Development installs may require a reset or reinstall; do not preserve intermediate snapshots solely for them. This never exempts a step from the idempotency rule below.
- **Only the foreground app migrates, and every migration step must still be idempotent.** WorkManager background tasks open the database on their own drift connection. drift does not serialize migrations across connections and writes `user_version` after each step, so a background task migrating alongside the app re-ran steps over a database that was already further along and left users updating from 6.12.x with a permanent startup crash (#3045). Background tasks therefore check `SqliteDatabase.isAtCurrentSchema()` and skip until the app has migrated; any new code that opens `SqliteDatabase` outside the foreground composition root must do the same. `_reportingMigration` runs each step in a transaction, so a step that fails part-way leaves nothing behind, but `user_version` is still written after the step commits and a step can be re-run in full. `onCreate` drops and recreates tables left by a first launch that never finished, and refuses if any table it does not seed holds a row. A second run of a step must leave the schema and the user's data unchanged: check before changing the schema (the `MigrationGuards` extension in `migration_guards.dart`, or catch only `duplicate column` / `already exists` and rethrow anything else), and run a data change only in the branch that makes the schema change it belongs to, or, for a seed, only while its table is empty. These guards are production correctness, never cleanup. `test/migrations_test/bull_database/migration_rerun_test.dart` re-runs every step over a current database holding user data, `migration_crash_recovery_test.dart` kills the upgrade (and a fresh install's first launch) after every commit and relaunches, and `migration_race_test.dart` races two connections from every schema fixture; all must stay green. A new step is re-run by the first automatically: seed any data it touches there, and add its schema fixture to the other two.
- Before release, verify the path from the latest published `main` schema fixture to the final pending schema. Once shipped, that migration and snapshot become immutable.

## Monorepo / melos

The repo is migrating incrementally to a [melos](https://melos.invertase.dev/) pub-workspace. The Flutter app remains at the repo root through `useRootAsPackage: true`. Current `workspace:` members (checked 2026-11-08, see ARCHITECTURE.md "Monorepo Migration" for what each one owns): `secrets`, `bull_ui`, `bull_ui_catalogue`, `primitives`, `bull_payjoin`, `bull_tor`, `screen_privacy`, `bull_logger`, `bull_pricer`, `bull_logs`. The makefile remains the canonical entry point across the workspace.

- **Run melos through the makefile** (`make bootstrap`), which wraps `fvm dart run melos` so the pinned SDK ([`.fvmrc`](.fvmrc)) is used. Never type bare `melos` (wrong SDK). For melos subcommands without a make target yet, use `fvm dart run melos <cmd>` — and add a make wrapper if it becomes routine.
- **The makefile stays canonical** for daily commands. melos does not replace it: `make deps` is still `fvm flutter pub get --enforce-lockfile`, and the reproducible build chain ([Containerfile.app](Containerfile.app), [build-android.yml](.github/workflows/build-android.yml)) does not run melos. melos is a `dev_dependency` only — never compiled into the app, so the reproducible APK is unaffected.
- **`packages/` and `features/` are reserved homes** for the migration (exception to rule #14 below): shared-foundation/infrastructure code lives in `packages/` (`lib/core`'s infra-only spirit per rule #7 applies there too); Flutter feature packages mounted by the root shell live in `features/`. Packages stay pure Dart by default; a Flutter dependency is allowed only when the package's capability inherently requires it (plugin/platform lifecycle, a sealed UI component, or implementing `bull_ui`) — never to permit feature presentation in an infrastructure package. Each extracted package gets `resolution: workspace` plus an entry in the root `workspace:` key (pattern established by melos PR #927). See ARCHITECTURE.md "Monorepo Migration" for the full rules.
- **Workspace verification is measured, not assumed.** Whole-project `fvm flutter analyze` covers the root and all members, and `make bootstrap` succeeds with `enforceLockfile: true` (verified 2026-07-30). Pub workspaces use the single root `pubspec.lock`; do not add member lockfiles. `make unit-test` runs `melos exec` over every declared member with a `test/` directory, the app included: Flutter members through `fvm flutter test`, pure-Dart members through `fvm dart test`. It calls `melos exec` directly through `fvm dart run`, because `melos run` would re-invoke a bare `melos` from `PATH`. CI runs the same set in parallel shards (#212): `tools/test_shards.sh` names the shards (a whole member, part k of n of a member's test files, or the default `*` that takes every member no shard names, so a new member or a new test file always lands in a shard), and its `check`, run by the `checks` job, fails if a member or a test file of a split member is in no shard or in two.

## Architecture — enforce, don't drift

Read [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md) ("How the codebase actually looks today" + "The architecture we tend toward") and [FEATURES.md](FEATURES.md) before adding or moving code. **The repo is in active migration.** For the directory census (feature/domain/facade counts, non-normative, dated) see ARCHITECTURE.md "How the codebase actually looks today" — don't duplicate those numbers here, they drift. The target below is what new code must follow. Existing code may violate these rules — do not replicate violations, but don't refactor unrelated legacy in the same PR either.

Some rules below are canonical (textbook Clean/Hex Arch), others are deliberate project conventions. Both are binding here, but knowing which is which helps when an external source contradicts a rule — the project rules win inside this repo, but cite the canonical source if the conversation moves to *why*.

Hard rules:

1. **Feature isolation — facade is our chosen pattern.** Modular Flutter has several valid options (facade, abstract use-case interfaces, event bus, mediator); this codebase picks facade. **Target location**: `<feature>/public/<feature>_facade.dart`. **Current legacy** (e.g. [`labels_facade.dart`](lib/features/labels/labels_facade.dart)): facade at feature root — new facades use the target location, don't move legacy ones. Never import another feature's internals (`domain/`, `data/`, `presentation/`) — the `public/` facade is the only importable surface. **The facade *is* the interface** — a concrete class, not an `abstract interface class`; its method surface + `export` block are the contract (add an abstract interface only for DIP-to-break-a-cycle or test mocking). **It returns only the feature's published types** (the entity itself when clean and stable, else a dedicated public DTO + mapper as `labels` does) — **never** a `data/models/` model, an `application/`-internal type, or another feature's entity; the return type must be in the `export` block. Consumers wrap a facade call in **their own** use-case (rule #4).
2. **Layer direction — one chain, always.** `ui → presentation (bloc/cubit) → usecase (domain) → repository (domain interface → data impl) → datasource (data)`. **A bloc never imports a repository or a datasource** — it always goes through a use-case, the testable seam where data-layer errors are mapped to feature errors. **Use-cases are always present but stay thin: orchestration only.** Put each kind of logic in its home — **invariants in domain entities/value objects; data-shaping (mapping, aggregation, caching) in the repository; orchestration in the use-case**. Pushing data-shaping or invariants into a use-case is the "fat use-case" smell — move it down. The inward-only dependency rule is [hexagonal-canonical](https://alistair.cockburn.us/hexagonal-architecture); the MVVM-spine + repository is the official [Flutter guide](https://docs.flutter.dev/app-architecture/recommendations).
3. **Watchers go through use cases**, not repositories. `watcher → use case → repository`.
4. **BLoCs are thin — in our architecture.** No orchestration, no business decisions, no complex transforms; those live in use cases. ([bloclibrary.dev](https://bloclibrary.dev/architecture/) treats the bloc layer itself as the business-logic layer above repositories — our stance is a deliberate Clean-Arch refinement, not bloclibrary canon.) **Project convention**: never call another feature's facade directly from a BLoC — wrap it in your own feature's use case, and the BLoC calls that wrapper. This is the cure for "business logic in presentation", ARCHITECTURE.md's most common pitfall.
5. **Local widget state stays in `StatefulWidget`.** BLoC/Cubit is for business state only — what other layers care about. Form-field focus, animation controllers, expanded/collapsed panels, scroll positions: keep them in widget state. Mixing the two makes BLoC tests flaky and bloats state classes.
6. **Repository: abstract interface in `domain/`, impl in `data/` — don't over-abstract the rest.** Declare the contract as `abstract interface class <Noun>Repository` in `domain/repositories/<noun>_repository.dart`; put the implementation in `data/<noun>_repository_impl.dart` next to its datasources — no second `repositories/` folder. A datasource wraps one external system and is a **private member** of its repository. One repository is enough for trivial CRUD; add a separate datasource only for real transformation or multiple backends. **Boundary rule (never bends):** a repository's public signatures use only domain types (entities, value objects, `Result<Entity, …>`); wire models, library types, JSON `Map`s and DTOs never cross the boundary. *(Legacy leak, don't replicate: `WalletAddressRepository` takes a `WalletModel` param.)* **Entity and model are always two separate types** (entity in `domain/entities/`, model in `data/models/`, mapper between) — never collapse them. The entity is **self-validating** and carries no serialization; the model is **pure data**. Cap is two types per record — see [Flutter recommendations](https://docs.flutter.dev/app-architecture/recommendations) on the five-types-per-record pattern being verbosity for large apps only. Value objects are for correctness-critical primitives (amount/sats, address, fee rate), not every field.
7. **`/lib/core` is legacy and disappears; the shell is composition only.** Add nothing to `lib/core`, neither logic nor wiring. A change that touches a `lib/core` domain extracts that part into its owning package or feature, so its `lib/core` diff is net negative; never relocate it to root `lib/` just to shrink that diff. What remains there until extraction is infrastructure only, with no feature/business logic. **Dated exception (2026-11):** the wallet readers stay in `lib/core/wallet` until the capability split; during the single-writer cutover only deletions and delegation to the single-writer sync package (`packages/transaction_sync`, introduced by the wallet sync PRs) are allowed there, no new logic or wiring. The shell (`lib/*.dart`) holds entry points, ordered bootstrap and lifecycle calls, app-level navigation, capability selection and calls to each module's published setup API. It implements nothing: no storage queries, mapping, grouping, wallet/signing/sync policy, recoverable error translation, presentation or strings; each package or feature constructs its own internals. A port does not imply a shell adapter: implement it in the module that may depend on both sides. A root adapter is exceptional: it documents the concrete cycle it breaks and its removal condition, and only forwards public capabilities. Root files that predate this rule and construct a module's internals (`lib/payjoin_setup.dart`, `lib/recoverable_payjoin.dart`, `lib/payjoin_runtime_adapters.dart`, `lib/report_logger_adapter.dart`) are tracked debt, moved in their own PRs. See ARCHITECTURE.md "Monorepo Migration".
8. **Shared value objects belong in `packages/primitives`**, the canonical package. During migration, compatibility types and exports may remain in `lib/core/primitives/`; see [FEATURES.md](FEATURES.md). Before adding one, search both locations and `lib/` for a same-intent type; reuse it rather than creating a duplicate.
9. **Prefer rich domain models** — methods that enforce invariants, not anemic DTOs mirroring DB rows. ([Fowler's anemic-domain anti-pattern](https://www.martinfowler.com/bliki/AnemicDomainModel.html) is widely cited but not universal; this is our preference, aligned with ARCHITECTURE.md's "Anemic Domain Models" pitfall.)
10. **No raw colors.** Always pull from the theme. If the user describes a color in plain language, pick the closest theme color that works in both light and dark mode. Don't edit theme files unprompted.
11. **Failures — one sealed family per feature, `*Failure` not `*Error`.** Three words, kept apart: **`Exception`** = thrown infra (data layer, caught at the boundary); **`Error`** = a `dart:core` programmer bug, never caught (one declared exception: the price read of the order page, #249 below); **`Failure`** = a modeled, recoverable **value** in `domain/`. Define a sealed `<Feature>Failure extends Failure` in `domain/<feature>_failure.dart`, **Flutter-free**; cross-cutting modes (network, storage-locked, not-found, timeout, auth, device, insufficient-funds) come from the shared `sealed CoreFailure` in `lib/core/failures/` (alongside the `Failure` base; `lib/core/errors/` is the legacy graveyard). Map foreign errors at the boundary; never leak another layer's or feature's type. **Translation is a presentation extension, not a method on the failure:** `presentation/<feature>_failure_l10n.dart` exposes `toTranslated(BuildContext)` — the only place `context.loc`/`flutter` touches a failure (keeps `domain/`+`data/` Flutter-free); the `sealed` switch makes a missing user message a compile error. **The end user never sees a dev string:** the catch-all variant returns a **generic** localized message (`oopsSomethingWentWrong`), never the raw `logMessage` — `unexpected: (m) => m` leaks dev detail. **Propagation:** repositories return `Result<T, F extends Failure>` (`Ok`/`Err`, `@useResult`; helpers `fold`/`map`/`mapErr`); `throw` only for `dart:core` bugs ([Flutter Result](https://docs.flutter.dev/app-architecture/design-patterns/result)). The **repository** is the one `try/catch` boundary (or the feature **use-case** when wrapping a shared core repo that still throws); use-cases forward/compose `Result`s; the bloc `switch`es and holds the typed `<Feature>Failure` in state; the UI renders `failure.toTranslated(context)`. **Migration (#1895) is sanctioned and staged** — bring a feature fully in line when you touch it, one feature per PR. **Legacy (don't replicate):** `BullException`; hardcoded English; `<Feature>Error` naming; `toTranslated` on the error; per-layer error-file splits; `fund_exchange`'s singular prefix.
12. **Acyclic feature graph.** Check [FEATURES.md](FEATURES.md) before adding a dependency.
13. **Keep the dependency graph live.** Any PR that adds a feature, removes a feature, or changes which other features it consumes via `public/` facades **must** update the mermaid graph in [FEATURES.md](FEATURES.md) in the same commit. The graph is documentation only if it matches the code — if you change one without the other, both become useless.
14. **Folders justify their existence — files don't justify folders.** A tiny piece of code is one file with a role suffix, not a folder of one file:
    - One use case → `<feature>/domain/<verb>_<noun>_usecase.dart`. Don't create `domain/usecases/` for a single file.
    - One entity → `<feature>/domain/<noun>.dart`. Don't create `domain/entities/` for a single file.
    - One datasource → `<feature>/data/<noun>_datasource.dart`. Don't create `data/datasources/` for a single file.

    Create the folder the moment a **second** file of that kind appears (or you know it's imminent in the same PR). Don't pre-create empty folders or use `.gitkeep`. Suffix carries the role; path carries the layer.

    **Exception (melos migration):** `packages/` and `features/` are intentionally pre-created with `.gitkeep` as reserved workspace homes for the in-progress monorepo migration — do not remove them or treat them as a rule-#14 violation. See the Monorepo / melos section.

15. **Enforce with the compiler, not hope.** Repository contracts → `abstract interface class` (forbids `extends`, forces `implements`); lock finer capabilities with `@Deprecated.implement()` / `.instantiate()` / `.extend()`. Failure families and multi-case states → `sealed`; a missing `switch` case is a compile error. `Result`-returning repo methods → annotate `@useResult`. Cross-feature and `data/`-from-`presentation/` bans → a rule in the first-party analyzer plugin system (Dart 3.10+); under melos, package boundaries + the `implementation_imports` lint make them hard errors. All available in the current toolchain (Flutter 3.41/3.44 bundle Dart 3.11/3.12) — no point-release dependency.

When a request would break these rules, explain why and propose a compliant alternative. Don't silently comply.

When you **work in** a feature, file, or folder that already violates these rules (legacy, a drifted hexagonal module, a leaked boundary), don't silently work around it and don't quietly copy it. **Flag it and offer a fix:** name the specific rule it breaks and propose a **scoped refactoring** to bring it into line — as its **own** atomic commit/PR, separate from the task at hand. Then let the developer decide: take it now, defer it, or skip it. Never fold an opportunistic refactor into an unrelated change (that breaks atomic commits). This is the "leave it cleaner than you found it" rule from [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md)'s intro, kept compatible with atomic commits — surface the opportunity, don't force it.

## Null safety

Never introduce the postfix non-null assertion operator (`value!`). A missing value must be handled explicitly: promote a checked local variable, use pattern matching, return a typed failure, or render the loading, unavailable, or error state. Do not replace an assertion with an unchecked cast or a fabricated default that hides missing data. When touching an existing assertion, check the lifecycle and asynchronous paths that can invalidate its assumption; cover a recoverable missing value with a regression test. This rule concerns non-null assertions, not boolean negation (`!condition`) or inequality (`!=`).

## Naming conventions

Codified from a sweep of the actual codebase. ARCHITECTURE.md is silent on most of these; the rules below reflect what the dominant code already does. Where two conventions exist, the recommendation is the more common one — migrate toward it, don't replicate the minority.

**Folders** (inside a feature):

| Folder | Convention | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `domain/entities/` | plural | rich models + value objects |
| `domain/repositories/` | plural | the single `repositories/` folder — **abstract** interfaces (contracts) |
| `domain/usecases/` | plural, no underscore | the canonical use-case home (~80% already here) |
| `data/datasources/` | plural, no underscore | not `data_sources/`; one external system each |
| `data/models/` | plural | wire/persistence model — **1 per entity, always separate from the entity** |
| `data/mappers/` | plural | model ↔ entity; a file only once it earns one (rule #14) |
| `data/` (repo impl) | — | `<noun>_repository_impl.dart` lives directly here |
| `presentation/` | — | bloc/cubit + state + event |
| `ui/screens/`, `ui/widgets/` | plural | |
| `public/` | — | facade lives directly inside |
| `watchers/` | plural, optional | drives use-cases, never repositories |

> Deprecated — converge away (see ARCHITECTURE.md "Convergence"): `application/`, `adapters/`, `interface_adapters/`, `frameworks/`.

**Files** — `snake_case`, singular suffix:

| Kind | Pattern | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Use case | `<verb>_<noun>_usecase.dart` | `broadcast_bitcoin_transaction_usecase.dart` |
| Repository interface (abstract) | `<noun>_repository.dart` in `domain/repositories/` | `bitcoin_wallet_repository.dart` |
| Repository impl | `<noun>_repository_impl.dart` **or** `<tech>_<noun>_repository.dart`, in `data/` | `bitcoin_price_repository_impl.dart`, `drift_electrum_server_repository.dart` |
| Datasource | `<noun>_datasource.dart` **or** `<tech>_<noun>_datasource.dart`, in `data/datasources/` | `electrum_remote_datasource.dart`, `bdk_wallet_datasource.dart` |
| Entity / value object | `<noun>.dart` | `wallet.dart`, `auto_swap.dart` |
| Model (wire/persistence) | `<noun>_model.dart` in `data/models/` | `wallet_utxo_model.dart` |
| Mapper | `<noun>_mapper.dart` in `data/mappers/` | `wallet_utxo_mapper.dart` |
| Bloc | `<feature>_bloc.dart` | `send_bloc.dart` |
| Cubit | `<feature>_cubit.dart` | `settings_cubit.dart` |
| State | `<feature>_state.dart` | `send_state.dart` |
| Event | `<feature>_event.dart` | `send_event.dart` |
| Facade | `<feature>_facade.dart` | `labels_facade.dart` |
| Locator | `<feature>_locator.dart` | `wallet_locator.dart` |
| Failure (family) | `<feature>_failure.dart` (domain) + `<feature>_failure_l10n.dart` (presentation) | per rule #11 |

**Classes** — `PascalCase`, matching local quirks:

- **Use cases**: `<Verb><Noun>Usecase` — **lowercase 'case'**, never `UseCase`. Codebase-wide convention (`GetSettingsUsecase`, `UpdateTorSettingsUsecase`, `BroadcastBitcoinTransactionUsecase`). Single public entry method **`execute(...)`** (206 of 209 use-cases as of 2026-06, non-normative; never `call`).
- **Datasources**: `<Name>Datasource` — **lowercase 's'**, never `DataSource`. Codebase-wide (`BdkWalletDatasource`, `BoltzDatasource`).
- **Repositories**: abstract interface = `<Name>Repository` (`abstract interface class`, in `domain/repositories/`); concrete impl = `<Name>RepositoryImpl` or `<Tech><Name>Repository` (in `data/`). Pick the technology prefix when the backing tech is meaningful; use `Impl` when the technology label would be awkward.
- **Entities & value objects**: bare noun, **no `Entity` suffix** (`Wallet`, `WalletUtxo`, `TransactionOutput`). Legacy outlier `LedgerDeviceEntity` — don't replicate.
- **Models (wire/persistence)**: `<Noun>Model` (`WalletUtxoModel`) — in `data/models/`, freezed; serialization only, never crosses the repository boundary (rule #6).
- **Mappers**: `<Noun>Mapper` (`WalletUtxoMapper`) — in `data/mappers/`; translates model ↔ entity.
- **Non-repository domain interfaces**: `<Name>Port` (`BlockchainPort`, `ElectrumConnectivityPort`) — a capability abstraction that is not a repository; lives in `domain/`. Repository interfaces use `<Name>Repository`, never `Port`. (The `Port` *suffix* stays for these; only the hexagonal *folders* `application/`/`adapters/`/`frameworks/` are deprecated.)
- **Bloc/Cubit**: `<Feature>Bloc` / `<Feature>Cubit` (`SendBloc`, `SettingsCubit`).
- **State / Event**: `<Feature>State` / `<Feature>Event`, sealed with freezed when there are multiple cases.
- **Facade**: `<Feature>Facade` (`LabelsFacade`).
- **Watcher** (when introduced): `<Subject>Watcher`.
- **Failure**: one sealed `<Feature>Failure` family per feature in `domain/<feature>_failure.dart` (base `Failure`); never `<Feature>Error` — `Error` is reserved for `dart:core` bugs. Translation extension `<Feature>FailureL10n` in `presentation/`. See rule #11.

When the same role exists with two names in the codebase, use the dominant one and flag the outlier as legacy. Don't rename outliers in unrelated PRs — that breaks atomic commits.

### Member ordering

Inside a class, declare members in this order, with a blank line between each group:

1. **Instance fields** — the object's state. Reading these first tells you *what the thing is*.
2. **Constructor(s)** — how it's built, on top of the fields you just read.
3. **Methods** — its behaviour, including `@override`s.

```dart
class Amount {
  final BigInt sats;

  Amount(this.sats) {
    if (sats < BigInt.zero) throw ArgumentError('Amount cannot be negative');
  }

  bool get isDust => sats < BigInt.from(546);
}
```

This is fields-first on purpose: our domain, value, and error types are defined by their state, so surfacing it first reads most naturally. It is a soft convention — the analyzer does not enforce member order, so keep it consistent by hand and in review.

**Do not enable the `sort_constructors_first` lint.** It enforces the *opposite* order (constructor before fields); turning it on would fight this convention and churn every class. Widgets, whose constructor is effectively their public API, may keep the constructor first if that reads better there — don't reformat existing widgets to satisfy this rule.

## UI Kit — reuse first, build the kit as you go

The codebase is migrating toward a real UI Kit. The skeleton already lives in [`lib/core/widgets/`](lib/core/widgets/) (`buttons/`, `cards/`, `inputs/`, `lists/`, `dropdown/`, `bottom_sheet/`, `text/`, `selectors/`, …) and theme tokens in [`lib/core/themes/`](lib/core/themes/). Today the same widgets get re-implemented per feature (`_SaveButton`, `_ConfirmButton`, `_BottomButtons` …) — stop adding to that pile.

The go-forward home for the kit is the **`bull_ui` design-system package** (`packages/bull_ui`; see ARCHITECTURE.md "Design-system package"). Provenance: **`BB*` = legacy `lib/core/widgets`, `Bull*` = `bull_ui`**. A feature whose UI is built on the kit imports only `package:bull_ui/bull_ui.dart` for widgets — never `package:flutter/material.dart`/`cupertino.dart`/`widgets.dart`. Migration is incremental, so both still exist; prefer a `Bull*` widget when one exists, otherwise follow the workflow below against `lib/core/widgets/`.

Workflow when you need a widget:

1. **Search `lib/core/widgets/` first.** If something close exists, use it.
2. **If close but missing a variant**, extend the core widget (new prop, new constructor) rather than fork. One source of truth, no copy-paste.
3. **If genuinely new and reused by ≥ 2 features**, put it in `lib/core/widgets/<category>/` from the start — that *is* growing the UI Kit.
4. **If used by exactly one feature**, it lives in `<feature>/ui/widgets/` — but write it composable enough to be promoted later (no hardcoded colors, no hardcoded text, take callbacks not bloc refs).
5. **Widgets never live under `adapters/`, `frameworks/`, `domain/`, or `application/`.** UI goes in `ui/` or `lib/core/widgets/`. Full stop.
6. **No hardcoded user-facing strings.** Always `context.loc.<key>` — the `BuildContext` extension (`build_context_x.dart`) that wraps `AppLocalizations.of(context)`; it is the dominant convention (≈2564 uses vs 3 raw `AppLocalizations.of`, as of 2026-06, non-normative). Manage keys in [`localization/`](localization/) with [`tools/arb.dart`](tools/README.md) (`fvm dart run tools/arb.dart help`) — don't hand-edit the `.arb` files — then run `make translations`. A duplicated literal across screens means a missing l10n key.
7. **Theme tokens only** — colors, spacing, typography pulled from the theme. See rule #10 above.

When you spot a duplicate of an existing core widget in feature code, flag it in the PR description as a follow-up cleanup. Don't silently leave it. Don't fix unrelated duplicates in the same PR either — that breaks atomic commits.

## Commits — atomic + Conventional

Format: `type(scope): description`

- **type**: `feat | fix | refactor | chore | docs | test | ci | build | perf | style`
- **scope**: feature folder name (`wallet`, `send`, `payjoin`, `swap`, `core`, `ci`, `hooks`, …) — lowercase, single noun.
- **description**: imperative, lowercase, no trailing period, ≤72 chars total subject line. Human-friendly and compact — describe the *why* if non-obvious; the *what* is in the diff.

Rules:

- **Atomic.** One logical change per commit. A feature + its refactor = two commits. A fix + cleanup = two commits. Independently revertable.
- **No `Co-Authored-By` trailer.**
- **No `--no-verify`.** Pre-commit must pass on its own.
- Reference the recent history (`dc3642801`, `14cce2ad6`, `1fecb0bb8`) for style.

## Tests

- Use cases and entities with business rules **must** have unit tests.
- **Layout:** tests live in `test/` mirroring `lib/` (`test/features/<feature>/…`, core under `test/core_test/`); files end `_test.dart`.
- **Tooling:** `bloc_test` for blocs/cubits, `mocktail`/`mockito` for collaborators; prefer **fakes** for repositories/datasources — the abstract interfaces exist precisely so you can swap a real implementation for a test double.
- `make unit-test` for the test you wrote. Don't claim done until it's green.
- Integration tests live in `integration_test/` and need device/.env fixtures.

## Dependencies (pubspec)

- **GitHub refs only, never `path:` deps.** All cross-repo packages are pinned via `git: { url, ref }` ([example](pubspec.yaml#L11-L14)).
- Adding a dep? Bump `pubspec.yaml`, run `make deps`, commit `pubspec.lock` in the same commit.

## Verify before suggesting

**Your training data is stale; the ecosystem isn't.** Flutter, Dart, BDK, Boltz, payjoin, freezed, drift, go_router, bloc — all churn fast. Pasting an API or library version from memory wastes the user's time.

Before recommending a library, API surface, syntax, flag, version, or pattern:

1. **Anchor on the current date.** Run `date -u +%Y-%m-%d` — your knowledge cutoff isn't today.
2. **Cross-check at least one trustworthy source**, freshness-matched to that date: official docs (pub.dev, docs.flutter.dev, dart.dev), the project's own GitHub (`gh release list`, `gh issue list`), StackOverflow answers from the last ~2 years, or Reddit (`r/FlutterDev`, `r/dartlang`).
3. **Cite the source** in chat or in the PR description: title + URL + a date stamp. "Per pub.dev/packages/foo (2026-04-15) …".
4. **If you can't verify, say so explicitly** — "I think X but couldn't confirm" beats stating a wrong API confidently.

This applies equally to architectural suggestions, tooling, CI tricks, command flags, and security recommendations. Confidence ≠ correctness.

## CLI and tooling habits

- **Prefer `rtk <cmd>`** if `command -v rtk` succeeds — token-optimized proxy. Falls back transparently if not installed.
- **Use `gh`** for GitHub (PRs, issues, runs, releases). If `gh` is unavailable, use `curl` or Fetch against the REST API or fetch the page — never invent URLs.
- **Learn unknown CLIs with `--help` first.** Don't guess flags. `<cmd> --help`, `<cmd> subcommand --help`, then act.
- **Get the current date with `date -u +%Y-%m-%d`** before judging anything as "recent" or "outdated" — see the verification section above.

## Logging and error reporting

Use `package:bull_logger/bull_logger.dart` and its `log` API. **Our levels deliberately differ from the usual logging convention: `INFO` is debug-console chatter, not a persisted diagnostic.** All levels print to the console only in `kDebugMode`; the on-disk TSV files are the logs users can share with support.

| API | On-disk support log | Reporter route |
|---|---|---|
| `log.info` | No | None |
| `log.fine`, `log.finer`, `log.finest` | Yes | None |
| `log.config` | Yes | None |
| `log.warning` | Yes | None |
| `log.severe` | Yes, flushed | Error capture |
| `log.shout` | Yes, flushed | Exception capture, or an info-level message when no exception/trace pair is supplied |

- **Choose a destination deliberately.** Use `info` for repetitive development traces (individual polls, relay attempts, fee previews). Use `fine` for milestones an exported log must reconstruct, `config` for configuration, and `warning` for recoverable failures. Record a persistent failure and its recovery as bounded state transitions rather than persisting every failed poll. Keep messages short and structured: mnemonic-like word sequences can be redacted by the local sanitizer.
- **Sentry is a separate, conditional route.** On the normal path, `severe` and `shout` invoke the optional reporter; other logger methods do not. In the app, capture requires the release SDK to be enabled and user consent. Background tasks do not call `Report.init`, so they currently keep their diagnostics on disk. Reentrant calls inside the log listener and internal logger failures do not invoke the reporter; their local diagnostics are best effort. Automatic SDK events have their own consent-gated paths; do not bypass the app's reporting boundary with feature-level SDK calls.
- **Local redaction is not Sentry redaction.** `Logger._sanitize` applies to disk and console output, not to the objects passed to the reporter. `Report.beforeSend` removes exception values, breadcrumb messages/data and requests, and attempts to clear frame variables; it does **not** remove every string. In particular, `bull.dev_message` and `shout` message events can retain the supplied message. Use fixed labels or explicitly allowlisted structured metadata. Never interpolate payment URIs, addresses, txids, amounts, credentials or user input into reporting messages. An `error:` argument can also expose its text in local logs: reduce sensitive foreign causes to safe type/status metadata before logging, and never rely on a scrubber to make secrets safe.
- **Payjoin uses the same vocabulary.** `AppPayjoinLogAdapter` maps `info`, `fine`, `warning` and `severe` directly to the homonymous wallet methods, without code-based remapping or a package-only debug level. Routine traces use `info`; persisted milestones use `fine`. Both require an explicit code. Remove context-free routine calls rather than emitting indistinguishable lifecycle noise. `PayjoinLogger` accepts structured fields only, without free-text message arguments; the host renders `PayjoinLogCode.description`. Structured session references retain 16 hex characters; the adapter displays their first 7 characters after the role, without a `ref=` label. Preserve fixed codes, roles and safe session references; never forward a raw foreign exception message.
- **Verify routing when changing logging.** Extend the contract tests in `packages/bull_logger/test/logger_reporter_test.dart` and the affected adapter tests. Check both what reaches the shared log and what invokes the reporter; a console line alone does not prove that support or Sentry will receive it.

Sources of truth: [`logger.dart`](packages/bull_logger/lib/src/logger.dart), initialization in [`logging_setup.dart`](packages/bull_logger/lib/src/logging_setup.dart), [`report.dart`](lib/core/utils/report.dart), the app's [`ReportLoggerAdapter`](lib/report_logger_adapter.dart), foreground wiring in [`init.dart`](lib/init.dart), background bootstrap in [`handler.dart`](lib/core/background_tasks/handler.dart), and [`AppPayjoinLogAdapter`](lib/payjoin_runtime_adapters.dart).

## Security (self-custodial wallet — treat as load-bearing)

This app holds users' keys. A leak is not a bug, it's a loss of funds. Hold these as hard as the architecture rules:

- **Only `packages/secrets` touches the keystore that holds the seeds.** No new import of `flutter_secure_storage` outside it (the app's own secure store in `lib/core/storage` is the one allowlisted exception), no import of `package:secrets/src/`, and never an `// ignore:` of `invalid_use_of_internal_member`. `make custody-ci-check` fails on any of them in CI; local `make custody-check` runs the Dart key-material gate. Rules: `tools/pr_governance/custody.js`.
- **Never log secrets.** Mnemonics, seeds, xprivs, PINs, raw key material never reach logs, Sentry, or analytics. Scrub before reporting; assume anything logged is exfiltrated.
- **Secrets are ephemeral.** Read from `flutter_secure_storage` at point of use; don't cache key material in long-lived bloc/singleton state. Treat a revealed value as short-lived.
- **Sealed UI for display.** Show a secret through a widget that reads it internally and never returns it (the `MnemonicView` pattern — see ARCHITECTURE.md "Sealed UI as a security tool"); never add a getter that hands the raw value to a caller.
- **Block capture on secret screens** — `no_screenshot` plus exclusion from the semantics/accessibility tree.
- **Validate at the domain boundary.** Addresses, amounts, descriptors are value objects that reject invalid input at construction (rule #9) — never trust a raw string deeper in.
- When a change touches key material, signing, or backup/recovery, **say so explicitly in the PR** so it gets the right review.

## Don'ts (the short list)

- Don't run `flutter` / `dart` without `fvm`.
- Don't run `flutter analyze <path>` — whole project only.
- Don't bypass `.git_hooks/pre-commit`.
- Don't import across feature internals — facade only.
- Don't put business logic in BLoCs or `/lib/core`.
- Don't log, cache, or expose secrets (mnemonic / seed / xpriv / PIN) — see Security.
- Don't return a `data/` model from a repository, or a non-published type from a facade — boundaries carry domain types only.
- Don't reinvent a widget that already exists in `lib/core/widgets/`.
- Don't hardcode user-facing strings — use `context.loc.<key>`.
- Don't hard-wrap prose mid-sentence in markdown, comments, PR descriptions, or commit bodies — write each sentence on one continuous line and let the editor soft-wrap. Manual line breaks belong only between paragraphs or list items.
- Don't add `path:` deps to pubspec.
- Don't amend or force-push without explicit ask.
- Don't add `Co-Authored-By`.
- Don't ship code you couldn't verify (`make unit-test` red = not done).
- Don't recommend an API, version, or pattern from memory — verify against pub.dev / GitHub / docs first.

## When in doubt

Plan first, code second. Read the existing facade of the feature you're touching. If the architecture file says one thing and the code says another, the file wins — flag the legacy code as a follow-up rather than copying it.

## Troc (fork)

The app's displayed name is « Bull Market » since #105, with the application id `io.github.skyman21m.bullmarket` and the repository `Skyman21m/bull-market`; « Troc » remains in internal names until their files are next touched.

This repository is Bull Market (formerly Troc), a fork of Bull Bitcoin Mobile. Fork-specific facts live in this section so upstream edits to the rest of the file merge without conflict.

- The Android `applicationId` is `io.github.skyman21m.bullmarket` (`io.github.skyman21m.troc` before #105), so every variant installs alongside the official Bull Bitcoin app. The Gradle `namespace` stays `com.bullbitcoin.mobile` on purpose. `test/core_test/android_identity_test.dart` pins both, plus the launcher labels.
- The launcher icons are generated by `tools/troc_launcher_icon.py`, not by `flutter_launcher_icons`. Running `dart run flutter_launcher_icons` would restore Bull's icon from `pubspec.yaml`. After changing the drawing, run `python3 tools/troc_launcher_icon.py` (needs Pillow) and commit every file it writes; `--check` tells whether the committed files are what it generates. `test/core_test/launcher_icon_test.dart` pins the adaptive icon's layers (monochrome included) and the files of every density.
- The workspace has two more members than upstream. `packages/robosats` is the pure-Dart RoboSats protocol package: robot identity, robot OpenPGP key, and the coordinator client with the embedded federation list (the public reads, and the robot of this device on a coordinator), behind a transport port. `features/p2p` (package `p2p`) implements that port, the path of every request to the coordinators, over the embedded Tor (the map tiles of #246, the one connection the feature opens itself outside Tor, are not that port; the wallet's price and swap service also leave outside Tor, through the wallet: « The Map view of the book (#30) » below) (`TorCoordinatorTransport`: one Tor session per coordinator for the anonymous reads and one per coordinator and robot for the requests of a robot, `http` to `.onion` hosts only, no `User-Agent`, see its doc comment and `docs/spikes/tor-transport.md`) and is registered by the shell through `P2pFeature.setup`.
- The P2P screens live in `features/p2p` (`lib/src/ui`: Tor wait, order book, its depth chart and its map, order details with « Take Order », the trade screen, coordinator dialog, « Search order », and the Garage). « Offers », « Create » (the create form since #182, disabled only where the feature holds no robot) and « Garage » sit at the top of the tab, in place of the official client's bottom bar, « Create » becoming « Order » while the network has an active trade or a pending make (« Create order » is then at the top of the list it opens) (« The trade screen (#88) » below); the Garage needs no network and is there while Tor connects. « Search order » opens an order of the public book from its URL without asking any coordinator; for an order the books received do not hold, which the official client reads with the user's robot, it waits while the book of the coordinator the URL names is still awaited and opens the order if that book brings it, says « Coordinator offline » (the feature's text for a coordinator that does not answer) when that book could not be had, and otherwise the official client's « You do not have a Garage Key » when no Garage Key is stored for the network, its « This order is not available » when one cannot be read, a failure not being an absence, or when the URL names no coordinator of the federation or no order number, and, with a Garage Key stored, the page of the order, which the robot that would take it reads there (`OrderSearchMissUnlisted`, #245: a password-protected order, which no book lists, or a public one the book in memory does not hold yet, « Taking an order reached by its URL » below) (`OrderSearchMissDialog`, `BookCubit.settleSearchMiss`, #121). Only for those last three, and only then, the book asks the vault whether a key is stored (`HasGarageKeyUsecase`, whose `exists` reads the slot of the network, hands no key out and uses no robot), never for an order found and never once the dialog is closed or the book closed; the dialog waits meanwhile, a few seconds for an empty slot, as tall as « This order is not available » so that it does not jump, and the answer is a live region for a screen reader; nothing is sent to any coordinator, and without the vault's handle no key can be stored. Deviations from the reference, said in the pull request of #121: « This order is not available » is the coordinator's answer (error 1044) in the official client and is said here without asking any coordinator; « Coordinator offline » where the official client would show the failure of its read of the order; the wait before the answer; « Acknowledged » to close. Since #245 an order outside the public book is opened with the robot that would take it, as the reference's order page reads it with the user's robot. The depth chart (`DepthChart` in the domain, drawn by `DepthChartView` with a `CustomPainter`, no charting dependency) is built from the book alone, with no request of its own: it centres on the orders it draws with one vote per coordinator (each coordinator's median premium or price after the currency and host filters, then the middle vote, and of two middle votes the one nearer zero, never their mean), and prices its price axis with the market price the orders' own prices imply, also one vote per coordinator (of two middle votes the lower), the chosen host first, where the official client reads `/api/info/` and `/api/limits/`. So a coordinator that floods the book does not move the window of the others, within limits: a majority of flooding coordinators wins; with an even number of coordinators voting, the two middle votes are not averaged and the one nearer zero is kept (the lower on a tie), so a tie cannot move the window off a vote near zero, but an honest book whose median is several points from zero can lose to a single order nearer zero on the other side; choosing the host is the recourse, and brings the chart back to that host's orders. The map view is « The Map view of the book » below. A coordinator is drawn with its own image, as the official client draws it, never with the provisional robot avatar of a maker: `CoordinatorAvatar` shows, in a disc, the avatar of `frontend/static/federation/avatars/<alias>.webp` (200 by 200, the file the official Android app loads), bundled in `features/p2p/assets/coordinators/` and decoded at the size drawn, and `MakerAvatar` badges each maker's robot of a federated coordinator with it in the book and in the chart's tooltip, the badge carrying the coordinator's name for a screen reader in the book. The images are assets only, named by the constants of `coordinator_images.dart` keyed by the aliases of the embedded list, never read from the network or from what a coordinator sends. A coordinator of the list without an image, or whose image cannot be loaded or decoded, has a mark of its own instead (`CoordinatorMark`: the capitals of its long alias in a framed rounded square, coloured from the theme by its short alias). The images have no licence of their own: they are each operator's artwork, sent for the federation and committed by the maintainers under the RoboSats repository's AGPL notice. The owner decided on 2026-11-12 to bundle them (the project is free software and nothing is at stake), and any of them is removed on request of its operator or of RoboSats (`NOTICE.md`). That decision covers these images only: the fork does not reuse Bull Bitcoin's logo; its displayed name, « Bull Market », is the owner's decision of #105, for a proof of concept meant to be shown to the upstream team, and is to be revisited if that team asks. `coordinator_images.dart` records the commit and Git blob id of each image, `features/p2p/test/coordinator_images_test.dart` pins them, and `test/features/p2p/p2p_coordinator_images_test.dart` loads each through the app's own asset bundle and checks its blob (#57). Text that a coordinator or a maker chose is shown as plain text, never as markup, and never logged: the package cleans and bounds what a coordinator sends (`displayText`, which it exports), and the screens pass the names of the coordinators and everything the coordinator dialog shows, its entry in the federation list included, through `CoordinatorText` (the same cleaning, with the code points drawn as nothing removed too, the zero width joiners and the variation selectors 15 and 16 kept in prose, and a bound). Links and contacts are shown, never opened, so nothing leaves Tor; one is offered for copy only when, so cleaned and without any of those code points, it is printable ASCII of at most 1,000 characters with no space at either end (`CoordinatorText.copyable`), so that what is copied is exactly what is read, and `packages/robosats/test/coordinator/embedded_data_test.dart` pins that every contact and onion address of the federation list is. The text of an order (its payment methods) is the package's cleaning only. The shell only mounts `P2pFeature.route` as a third tab of the bottom bar, between Wallet and Exchange, behind the gate of Silent Payments (`P2pFeature.isVisibleTo`: superuser and developer mode). The P2P routes are always registered: with the gate closed the tab is hidden, every `/p2p…` location redirects to the wallet (`P2pFeature.redirect`), and no P2P widget, cubit or Tor start is built. The tabs of the bar are the pure functions of `lib/shell_tabs.dart`. The book follows the wallet's network (testnet → RoboSats testnet).
- The Map view of the book (#30): `MapChartView` (`lib/src/ui/map_chart_view.dart`), the official client's map chart (`components/Charts/MapChart`, `components/Map/index.tsx`) drawn by the app with a `CustomPainter`: no map dependency, no location permission, nothing of the user's own position, and no request until « Show tiles » (#246, design note `docs/design/phase-3-map-tiles.md`): the switch at the right of the title, off on every book's map built and in every new create form (the meeting point's map keeps its switch and acknowledgment while its form is open, as the reference's `F2fMapDialog` stays mounted in `MakerForm.tsx:651-665`; `MapTilesChoice`), opens the reference's dialog word for word the first time (« Download high resolution map? », « Close », « Acknowledged », `MapTilesDialog`), and only then does `OsmMapTileAdapter` (`lib/src/data/osm_map_tile_adapter.dart`), the one connection the feature opens itself outside Tor (the owner's decision of 2026-11-23, as the reference's Android app; the wallet's price and swap service also leave outside Tor, through the wallet), fetch `https://tile.openstreetmap.org/{z}/{x}/{y}.png` for the tiles in view (`MapTile.covering`: whole zooms 0 to 15, Leaflet's rounding), drawn in place of the countries, which stay in the square of a tile that has not come (`MapChartPainter.paintGround`). Its rules: a `dart:io` client of its own per session, created at the first tile and closed when the switch goes off or the map closes; HTTPS only, the certificate checked, no redirect, at most 6 requests at once, bounded waits; an answer of at most 512 KiB read as a tile only as a PNG of 256 × 256 checked before decoding, `x-blocked` and « Too Many Requests » (429) read as a refusal (« Map tiles unavailable », and nothing more asked until the switch is turned off and on); a failed tile asked again only when the set of tiles in view changes; the tiles asked given up while the map is covered by another page; exactly the headers `User-Agent` and `X-Requested-With` at `TumbleLark` (an anagram of « Bull Market », the owner's choice), `If-None-Match`, `Accept-Encoding: identity` and `Host`; the tiles kept in memory for the session only, 256 tiles and 16 MiB at most, nothing on disk; a direct connection, never an environment's proxy; the meeting point's map protected from screen capture (`PrivacyScreen`, through a `MapTileGuard`) while its tiles show, and only then, following the wallet's « Screen Privacy » setting; a fixed log label, never a tile's address or numbers; the credit « © OpenStreetMap contributors » on the map while tiles show. `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` pins that no other file of the feature opens a connection (`dart:io`'s clients, sockets, `InternetAddress` and `Process`, `dio`, Tor, and Flutter's `NetworkImage`, `Image.network`, `FadeInImage`'s network constructors and `NetworkAssetBundle`; a plugin reached through a platform channel is not seen), `map_tiles_test.dart` the client's rules, `map_tiles_route_test.dart` and `maker_password_location_route_test.dart` the switch and the dialog on both maps.
  It sits below the row of the coordinators as the chart does, and shows every order of the book received that has a position, whatever the filter of the list, as the official client's map shows its whole book (`MapChart`, `orders={federation.book}`); an order at 0°, 0° is drawn there, since the reference tests only that both values are present.
  The world is `features/p2p/assets/map/world_110m.bin`, Natural Earth's 1:110m countries (public domain) reduced by `tools/troc_world_map.py`, which refuses any source but the one it pins and documents the format; `WorldMap.parse` reads it and fails closed (any byte out of the format, and the orders are drawn on an empty map), and an asset the bundle cannot give is the same empty map, with no error thrown.
  To regenerate it, run `python3 tools/troc_world_map.py <ne_110m_admin_0_countries.geojson>` and commit the asset with its new SHA-256 in `WorldMapSource` and `features/p2p/test/world_map_test.dart`; `--check` tells whether the committed asset is what the script writes, and that test runs it when `TROC_NATURAL_EARTH` names the source file.
  Web Mercator as Leaflet (`WebMercator`: latitudes clamped at ±85.0511°, no wrap at the antimeridian), zooms 0 to 15, opening at zoom 1 on 0°, 0°; the pins are drawn by the app, the outline of the drop and the smile its own, the proportions of the visor and of the eyes taken from RoboSats' `MapPin` (`components/Map/index.tsx`, AGPL-3.0, recorded in `NOTICE.md`), primary for a seller and secondary for a buyer; `MapMarks` groups them as Leaflet.markercluster with the reference's settings (80 points, up to level 13), once per book, level by level from the deepest, never while the map moves.
  A tap on a pin opens its order, a tap on a group zooms in as `zoomToBounds` does or, when zooming cannot part it, lists its orders; a long press or a pointer that hovers shows `OrderTooltip`; `MapLayout` draws only the marks in view and, past the levels with groups, one pin per point, so a thousand orders at one point cost one pin.
  A drag that starts on the map moves the map (its slop, 3 points, is below the scroll view's), so the row above it is where the book scrolls and is pulled to refresh.
  A screen reader reads a summary, which it can scroll to move the map by half a view, then each pin and group in view (200 at most, those nearest the centre), each node keyed by its order so that the focus stays on it while the map moves, which it can activate as a tap does; nothing animates.
  Deviations from the reference, each in the pull request of #30: « Show tiles » left out for privacy until #246 brought it, outside Tor, with its own deviations listed in its pull request; for the licence, the world map from Natural Earth (the licence and provenance of the reference's map file are not stated); product choices of Troc, said as such: the world kept in view, the zoom not snapped to whole levels after a pinch (the groups following the nearest level), a group that zooming cannot part listed rather than spread out, no double tap or wheel zoom, no coverage polygon on hover, « No order with a face to face location » over a map with no pin, the chart's empty and loading states where the reference shows an endless spinner while its book is empty (`MapChart/index.tsx:87-97`), the pin's tip exactly on the position (the reference's `iconAnchor` puts it 2 pixels off), the pin's outline and eyes in the theme's fixed dark and light colours, the groups in the theme's success, tertiary and warning colours, and the screen-reader texts and actions.
- The filter sentence of the book (#101): `BookFilter` (`lib/src/domain/book_filter.dart`) holds one order type and three sets, currencies, payment methods and hosts, an empty set meaning any; its texts are `BookFilterText` (`lib/src/presentation/book_filter_text.dart`) and its sheets `FilterSheet` (`lib/src/ui/widgets/filter_sheet.dart`).
  The rules are the official client's (`utils/filterOrders.ts` at commit ff1998c): an order is kept when its currency is one of those chosen, its host one of those chosen, and its payment method text contains at least one of the method names chosen (`filterByPayment`: JavaScript's `includes`, so case included and anywhere in the text, « SEPA » keeping « Instant SEPA » and « Revolut » not keeping « revolut »), swaps never in the list; the depth chart follows the currencies and hosts only, its price axis in the one currency chosen and otherwise in US dollars, as the reference prices « ANY », and the map follows no filter. The payment-method sheet offers every method of the official client's fixed list of fiat methods (`PaymentMethods.fiat`, its `fiatMethods`, `AutocompletePayments.tsx:301`), whatever the book and the other choices (`BookFilter.offeredPaymentMethods`): it was first narrowed to the methods of the orders the other choices keep, which the owner reported on 2026-11-14 as methods gone missing once a side or a currency is chosen (#112); then the whole list in its order was too long to scroll (#118). Its order, computed once as it opens so that no row moves while the user ticks (`BookFilter.paymentMethodSheet`): the methods chosen, in the order chosen, so that they are in view when it opens again (the reference scrolls its list to the first chosen option); then the methods some order would be kept with, each with its number of orders (« Instant SEPA · 12 orders », a chosen one too, « no orders » included), the most orders first, equal counts in the list's order; then, under « No orders right now », every other method in the list's order, dimmed but selectable. A count is the number of orders the filter would keep with that method alone, the side, currencies and hosts as they are, by the filter's own rule (`paymentMethodCounts`, `matchesPaymentMethods`), never by `PaymentMethods.parse`, so that a count never promises an order the filter will not show; the counts are those of the books received when the sheet opened, a partial load included (reopening it counts again); while no coordinator's book is known (none answered yet, or every one offline: `BookState.hasBookReceived`, which a refresh keeping the previous books on screen does not clear), the book is unknown, not empty, so the sheet lists the official list in its order, the chosen methods first, with no count, no heading and nothing dimmed, and stays so if the books arrive while it is open. Its search field is the reference's (`useAutocomplete` with MUI's default `createFilterOptions`: the name contains the text anywhere, case and accents ignored, the text not trimmed; `matchesOptionSearch`, whose accent folding covers Latin-1, Latin Extended-A and the Romanian « ș » and « ț » since the feature takes no normalization dependency, the rest of Latin Extended-B and Vietnamese, which NFD would fold, being a known limit); the sheet keeps its height while a text is typed, so that only the rows change, as in the reference; it narrows every group, hides « Any payment method » while a text is typed, and forgets the text when the sheet closes; its hint is the feature's « Search », where the reference's field has none.
  The order type stays a single choice, as in the reference: with buy and sell only, both is any, and the order of the book, the empty book's message and « I want to » follow one side.
  Choosing filters asks no coordinator anything: every coordinator is still asked for its book and shown in the row above it, as in the reference, where choosing a host requests nothing either (`refreshBookHosts` only subscribes again to every host's book on Nostr when « ANY » is chosen back, and the book here is read over HTTP).
  Deviations from the reference, each with its reason: several payment methods brings the app to the reference's own behaviour (its filter takes several, `BookControl.tsx` with `AutocompletePayments` `multiple`; the app took one); several currencies and several hosts are the owner's request of 2026-11-13, the reference taking one of each; each selector names its subject while nothing is chosen (« Any type », « Any currency », « Any payment method », « Any host »; « Tout type », « Toute devise », « Tout mode de paiement », « Tout hôte », the reference's French words) where the reference shows « ANY » on all four, the owner's request of 2026-11-13 for clarity, and so the English sentence starts with « I want » while no type is chosen (« I want to [Any type] » is not English) and the empty book says « any currency » where the reference says « ANY »; with several choices a selector shows the first one chosen and how many more (« EUR +2 »), as the reference shows its hidden payment tags, and a screen reader hears the subject and every choice (« Currency: EUR, USD or CHF »); a sheet that takes several choices applies each tap at once, as the reference applies each change, but stays open for the next one where the reference's list closes after each, which choosing several would make tedious, and closing it keeps what was chosen; the payment-method sheet's order, its counts and its heading are the owner's request of 2026-11-14 (#118), the reference listing its fixed list in its own order, and in its search: the hint « Search » is an addition (the reference's field has none), « No payment method found » is an addition (the reference's list simply disappears), the button that empties the field is an addition (the reference has none), the text typed stays after a choice where the reference empties it (its list closes at each choice; this sheet stays open), and « Any payment method » is hidden while a text is typed (it is not a method to search).
  `FilterSheet` (several choices) and `FilterPickerSheet` (the order type, one choice, closed by it) draw as `BullPickerSheet`, but their title scrolls with their rows: on a phone of 320 points with the text three times as large, `BullPickerSheet`'s fixed title fills the whole sheet in French and leaves its rows no room, which the type's list had before #101; from two and a half times the text size their close button goes above the title, which then has the whole width, and their rows narrow their margins, so that « Sélectionner » fits on a line of a 320-point phone (a single word wider than the room left beside a coordinator's image, such as « Gumpellite » at three times the size, still breaks inside); their list adds the system's bottom inset to its padding. They live in the feature rather than in `bull_ui`, which comes from upstream; fixing `BullPickerSheet` there and promoting them is a follow-up.
- The Garage (`lib/src/ui/garage/`, #29) is the official client's Garage Key mode, on the device but for the robot panel of its profile (« The robot panel of the Garage » below), the only screen that sends a robot to a coordinator, and nothing to Nostr; it starts in Garage Key mode, with no mode switch and no legacy token mode. Its cubit (`GarageCubit`, one per network, keyed by the wallet's network like the book) reads the vault only when the user opens the Garage, and the Garage is offered only when `P2pFeature.setup` received the vault's handle. With no key: the welcome screen, the wizard (« Generate Garage Key » calls `create`, then the key is read back and shown at once for backup with the official client's alert, since it is never part of the wallet backup; a key is pasted before one is generated, never over a stored one; « Generate new » with a key stored asks « Delete Garage Key? » first, and the PIN when the delete needs it (below), then deletes and draws), and « Robot recovery », where a key is pasted. One rule for a key shown without the PIN: only right after `create`, while the user stays in the Garage with the app in the foreground the whole time from the tap on « Generate Garage Key » or « Generate new »; once the Garage is left or the app hidden or paused, the vault still drawing included, the key is shown only behind the PIN (`GarageView` listens to the app's lifecycle for the whole visit, the wait included, and tells the cubit, which remembers a hide until the next tap); a pasted key is never shown without the PIN. Until the user has seen it and pressed « Continue », or deleted it, `UnacknowledgedGarageKeys` (in memory, written only by `GarageCubit`, whose library keeps its set private) makes each visit show the backup alert with the key behind the PIN, a key drawn while the user left included; it reveals nothing itself, and after the app dies the key is the profile's. A closed cubit never reads the vault. With a key: the profile, whose key stays hidden until the user asks and passes the PIN the wallet asks before it shows its seeds (`AppUnlockFacade.buildReauthenticationGate`, lent by the shell as `P2pFeature.route(reauthenticate:)`, pushed on the root navigator, revealing only on its first callback while its route is on top; without it the key is never shown there) and is hidden again when the app goes to the background (the wallet's seed view does not do this), the robot navigator (robots derived on the device, from robot 0 at each visit: the index is not stored yet), and « Delete All », which deletes the key of this network only after the official confirmation. A read the vault cannot settle shows why, « Retry », and, unless the keystore is only locked, the delete with the same warning. Deleting a key, by « Delete All », « Generate new » or that way out, asks after « Delete Garage Key? », and before anything is deleted, the same re-authentication as showing it, once a robot of the key may live on a coordinator: when a robot index of the key is bound, on either network, or the key is not recorded as drawn here for the network of its slot (imported, acknowledged, drawn before the records, or whose record failed); and always for a slot that cannot be read (a keystore that misbehaved may give its key back later, intact and possibly with robots on coordinators, and nothing tells which key it holds), which covers a key that gives no robot, and so no fingerprint either. The official client asks only the confirmation: deleting the key loses the robots a coordinator knows, and what they hold there, unless the user kept a copy, so Troc asks the PIN of whoever holds the unlocked phone. `NeedsPinToDeleteGarageKeyUsecase` reads it from `p2p.sqlite` (one read-only query of the repository, `exposureOf`) when the user deletes, not when the key was read, says yes without a read for a slot that cannot be read, and says yes when the database cannot be read; `GarageCubit` asks the re-authentication the screen lends it and deletes only once it passed, and a re-authentication left, or none lent, deletes nothing and leaves the screen as it was (nothing but a hide of the app changes the state while it is asked, a second tap and « Retry » meanwhile are ignored, so that the slot it decided on is the one it deletes, and a cubit closed meanwhile deletes nothing). Without the shell's re-authentication such a delete is disabled, as « Show Garage Key » is; the state's `deletionNeedsPin` only greys it, the decision being the read at the delete. An `Error` of the database read is a bug (rule 11) and is not caught: a delete then throws and deletes nothing, and a read of the Garage stays on its wait. A key drawn here, recorded so, with no robot bound, is deleted on the confirmation alone, as in the official client, and a wallet with no PIN passes the re-authentication at once, as for its seeds. Right after a draw whose history could not be recorded, the robot step of the wizard and the profile say so, and that its robots will ask for the user's confirmation before they reach a coordinator, which the acknowledgement of « The robot panel of the Garage » below does (`p2pGarageKeyHistoryNotRecorded`, `GarageWithKey.historyNotRecorded`), until the key is deleted; at a later visit nothing tells such a key from one drawn before the records. The whole Garage is protected from capture with `screen_privacy` and `BullScreenPrivacyGate`, which draws nothing until the protection is on and only a message and « Retry » where the platform refuses it. `GarageKeyView` (`lib/src/ui/widgets/garage_key_view.dart`) is the only widget that turns a key into text: one `Text` kept out of the semantics tree, and the clipboard only through `GarageKeyClipboard`, one per feature, which empties the clipboard 30 seconds later if it still holds that key (compared by reading and parsing it, never by keeping its text; emptied anyway when it cannot be read; emptied at once when the app's lifecycle detaches, best effort, since swiping the app away usually kills the process first); `test/garage_key_view_surface_test.dart` pins its members, the two places `revealBech32` is called, and the file's imports and declarations, so that `Text` and `clipboard.copy` are Flutter's widget and the feature's helper. The copied key is not flagged sensitive: Flutter's `Clipboard.setData` cannot set `EXTRA_IS_SENSITIVE`, so from Android 13 the system shows a preview of the key at each copy, and a keyboard with a clipboard history may keep a copy the 30 seconds do not remove (the wallet's BIP85 copy has the same gap); a small platform channel is to fix it before any release (issue #56). The field where a key is pasted (`GarageKeyField`) has no selection and no text menu, as the wallet's recovery phrase fields (no Copy, Cut, Share or « Translate » that would take the key out of reach of the clearing or to another app), so a « Paste » button reads the clipboard once on the user's tap, takes the focus away from the field so that a pasted key is never sent to the keyboard (and writes nothing if the field took the focus back meanwhile), and « Clear » empties it; no selection can be made from the app's own interface or a hardware keyboard, but the system keyboard's own editing tools can still select, cut and copy a typed key while the field has focus (Android's input connection does it without asking Flutter, `InputConnectionAdaptor` of the engine), and the keyboard sees what is typed: a read-only field with an in-app keyboard, as the wallet's, and the sensitive clipboard flag are issue #56; it tells the keyboard not to learn, suggest, correct or autofill (`autofillHints: null`), cleans the text as the official client does (whitespace removed, lower case), shows its validator's reasons, and keeps the text out of the semantics tree as the wallet's recovery phrase fields do, its label, error and buttons staying in it; `GarageKey.parse` checks it before anything is stored, and the text stays in the field's controller. Links of the wizard (support group, documentation) are shown for copy, never opened. Cubit states hold a `NetworkGarageKey` and the public face of a robot (number, nickname, hash id), never a key's text or a token; `test/garage_key_output_test.dart` pins that the files that handle a key neither import nor name anything that prints or logs, and the cubit and screen tests capture prints and the logger's file over every path.
- Tor in the P2P tab: each entry in the tab builds a new book, which calls `ensureReady` on the embedded Tor, waits for it to be ready, then asks every coordinator again. P2P always uses the embedded Tor, even when the wallet is set to an external Tor proxy. Tor and the sessions to the coordinators stay up after leaving the tab. « Retry » is offered only when Tor failed to start, and it restarts the embedded Tor that the whole wallet shares. The wait shows the three steps of the official Android client and a bar that runs, and two things the official client does not show, both asked for by the owner (#68). First, the share of its start that Arti reports (`TorConnecting.progress` of `bull_tor`, which the feature carries as `P2pTorReport` without any change to `bull_tor`, and which `TorWait.percentAfter` turns into the percentage shown): none before Tor reports a share, whole percent rounded down, at most 99 % until Tor is ready (`bull_tor` reports a full share during the 5 seconds it holds readiness), never lower during one attempt, from nothing again at each new attempt (`bull_tor` reports a stop or a connecting state without a share before the Snowflake fallback and « Retry »), and none once Tor is ready or has failed; the bar is filled to it and keeps running over the fill, so it never stands still before Tor is ready, except with reduced motion, where the run stops once a percentage is shown and the fill and the number carry the progress (before any share it runs, as decided in #62); the room of the percentage is kept for the whole wait, so nothing moves when it comes. Second, under the bar, twelve phrases of Troc's own, approved by the owner, one every 2 seconds (`TorWaitPhrases`, keys `p2pTorWaitPhrase1` to `12`), in an order `P2pShuffle` draws at each wait; `P2pShuffle` is injected like `P2pClock` (random in the app, unshuffled in the test seam), so no widget draws at random. Keep that widget light: one timer and one animation controller (the gear and the fade), both ended in its `dispose`, a change of phrase rebuilding only itself, the room of the largest phrase kept so that nothing moves, no turning gear and no fade under reduced motion, and the phrases out of the semantics tree so that a screen reader does not read a new one every 2 seconds.
- The book of the P2P tab asks again by itself a coordinator whose request got no answer before it shows it offline, which the official client does not do (its `loadBook` asks each coordinator once, `frontend/src/models/Federation.model.ts` at commit ff1998c; a request in flight is joined, which Troc does too): the owner asked for it (#73), since the first requests after a cold start of Tor are the ones that fail (`docs/spikes/`). The rule is `BookRetry`: only a failure that says nothing was answered (`CoordinatorUnreachable`, `CoordinatorTimedOut`, `CoordinatorTransportBusy`) is asked again, and a coordinator that answered something unusable, or something that is not HTTP (`CoordinatorTransportFailed`, which the transport does not retry either, its other causes not passing), is offline at once; a coordinator that has not given its book yet in this book gets two more attempts, about 5 then 15 seconds after the failed ones, and one that has gets one more after about 5 seconds, each wait spread by up to 20 % at random. Each attempt is a whole request of the transport (up to 132 seconds and three Tor sessions), so the attempts of a coordinator that times out every time last about seven minutes; it is shown awaited 80 seconds at most from the moment it is asked (`BookRetry.awaitedAtMost`, the project's decision of #73: the third attempt of a fast failure starts 46 to 54 seconds in, with the spread, and the slowest cold first contact measured took 23.2 seconds once Tor was ready, so 80 seconds covers the measured cases and stays bearable to look at), then shown offline while its attempts go on silently within the rule, a late answer still lighting it and adding its offers. `LoadFederationBooksUsecase` runs the rule, one instance per book, which remembers the coordinators that gave their book to it; the book requests in flight are kept by `BookRequestsInFlight`, a lazy singleton of the feature keyed by network and coordinator, so that no book, of this visit to the tab or of a later one, sends a second book request to a coordinator while one runs: it joins it, as the official client's coordinators keep their `_bookPromise` for the whole app (a book that is left cancels only its own attempts; a request whose books are all gone runs to its end, within the transport's 132 seconds, and is forgotten then, and a request that has ended is never handed to a book that asks after it); its timers and draws come from `P2pWaits`, injected like `P2pClock` (real timers and a random draw in the locator, the test zone's timers and no spread in the test seam), so no use case or cubit starts a timer or draws by itself. « Retry » (the offline ones) and a pull to refresh (all) give those they ask all their attempts again and a new 80 seconds, joining a request still in flight; a refresh ends the waits, leaving the tab ends them all, bounds included, and a Tor that reports failed under the book ends them and shows offline at once those waiting to be asked again. The indicator of a pull, and what « Retry » awaits, closes once every coordinator asked has answered or is shown offline, and in any case 10 seconds after (`BookRetry.refreshAtMost`): the row carries the rest.
