Above the offers and above the depth chart, `CoordinatorsRow` (`lib/src/ui/widgets/coordinators_row.dart`) shows the image of each coordinator of the network in the order of the federation, lit once its book has arrived, dimmed with a clock while it is awaited, grey with an offline mark after its last attempt, and a line under it: « N of M coordinators answered » while any is awaited and once all are, « N coordinators offline » with « Retry » once none is. It is a deviation from the official client, which shows only a bar over its book while any coordinator loads, asked for by the owner (#73, option A): it stays once the offers are shown, whatever the « hosted by » filter, and a tap on an image opens the coordinator's dialog and does nothing else. It replaced the orange banner that listed each offline coordinator with its reason. A coordinator that answered something unusable (a body too large, an unexpected status, a body that is not JSON or not a book, an answer that is not HTTP) is shown offline in the row like one that did not answer, its image grey with the offline mark and its node saying « offline »: a rule decided in #73, since for the user it has no offers to show; the coordinator dialog, which reads `/api/info/` itself, shows its details when that answers, and the Select Host sheet of the Garage notes « Coordinator offline » of a coordinator the book could not reach or whose answer was too large, not of one that answered something else. Keep it light and still: one animation controller, which pulses the awaited images only while one is awaited and never with reduced motion (the clock is the sign either way); the line keeps the room of its largest form with figures of one width, so nothing moves when it changes; each image is one semantics node, its name and its state, with no live region; images of 32 points in targets of 48 fit six at 320 points, and more wrap to a second line.
- The P2P texts are the feature's own ARB files (`features/p2p/lib/l10n/`, class `P2pLocalizations`, registered in `lib/app.dart`), like `features/logs`; `tools/arb.dart` only manages `localization/`. Keys and English/French texts come from the phase-1 text catalogue of the project; the other locales have empty ARB files and fall back to English. After editing them, run `fvm flutter gen-l10n` inside `features/p2p` and commit `lib/generated/l10n`.
- `test/features/p2p/p2p_screens_capture_test.dart` renders the P2P screens to PNG with the app's dark theme, fonts and the recorded books of `packages/robosats/test/fixtures/coordinator/`; it is skipped unless `P2P_CAPTURE_DIR` names an output folder. CI runs it on every application pull request in the `captures` job (`make p2p-screen-captures`, #258) and keeps the PNG files a few days as an artifact of the run; the `CI` gate requires it, so a capture that stops rendering fails the pull request. The captures look for buttons as a user would, on screen: one below the screen is scrolled into view first (`scrollUntilVisible`), never tapped offstage. `package:p2p/testing.dart` is its seam (fake transport, Tor states or reports with the share of Tor's start, clock, the order of the phrases of the Tor wait, and the Garage over a vault the test made); the Garage is captured with the public example key of the RoboSats tests only. The same library is the seam of the live integration tests: `liveCoordinatorTransportForTesting` hands out the real Tor transport, and it exports the robot types the app-facing library keeps to itself. It is for tests only: `test/features/p2p/p2p_testing_seam_test.dart` fails if a file under `lib/`, `features/*/lib/` or `packages/*/lib/` imports it.
- `packages/robosats` derives RoboSats key material outside `packages/secrets`: Garage Keys, robot tokens, Nostr keys and robot OpenPGP keys. These are RoboSats identities, never wallet seeds, and `packages/robosats` never derives anything from a wallet seed: the only Garage Key derived from a wallet's words is computed inside `packages/secrets` (`secret.derive.garageKey`, #81). Its BIP32 private derivation is hand-written in `lib/src/bip32.dart`, is not exported, and is tested against the BIP32 specification vectors. The custody gate (`packages/secrets/tool/key_material_gate.dart`) matches named library symbols, so it does not see this code: any change there needs the same scrutiny as a change in `packages/secrets`. Storing a Garage Key at rest is decided (#28): `packages/secrets` keeps one Garage Key per network through `Secrets.garageKeys()` (slots `mainnet` and `testnet`, stored under `com.bullbitcoin.secrets/garage/p2p/<slot>`), 32 random bytes drawn inside the package, never derived from a seed, never stored with one, and never part of a wallet backup: the user keeps a copy separately. Since #81 the package also derives a Garage Key from a secret's words, `secret.derive.garageKey(network:, index:)` (`Secret.garageKey`, `Deriver.garage`): the first 32 bytes of the BIP85 entropy at `m/83696968'/82796679'/0'/{network}'/{index}'` (network `0'` mainnet, `1'` testnet, index `0'` for the first key), computed on each call and stored nowhere, refused for a secret with a passphrase or without words; the application number `82796679'` and its purpose `0'` are reserved, and the path, the purpose and the truncation are frozen once a version that derives a key has shipped (`packages/secrets/doc/design.md`, § The derived Garage Key). No production code calls it yet: the custody gate refuses `Secret.garageKey` and `SecretDerivation.garageKey` everywhere. A stored key is never replaced (`create` and `import` need an empty slot, settled with the full read budget under the lock), and `delete` is the only way to empty a slot. Only the shell may ask `Secrets` for that handle, to pass it to `features/p2p` alone: the custody gate refuses `Secrets.garageKeys` outside the class `AppLocator` of `lib/locator.dart`. That file also composes the WorkManager isolate (`lib/core/background_tasks/handler.dart` calls `AppLocator.setup`), whose lock does not reach the foreground's, so the handle is obtained in the foreground composition only: `AppLocator.setup` has a required `withGarageKeys` flag, which `lib/init.dart` sets to true and the background handler to false, and `AppLocator.setupP2p` asks for the handle only when it is true and passes it to `P2pFeature.setup(locator, garageKeys:, databaseDirectory:)` with the directory of the feature's database (« One robot, one coordinator » below); with `null` for both, nothing that reads or writes a Garage Key, a binding or that database is registered in the feature. `test/locator_test.dart` runs both values, and `test/core_test/background_tasks/background_tasks_keystore_test.dart` reads what each composition passes, since `AppLocator.setup` cannot run in a unit test. No background task may create or import a Garage Key. The custody gate refuses `GarageKeyMaterial.bytes`, `GarageKey.revealBytes` and `GarageKey.revealBech32` outside two named files of `features/p2p`: the repository `lib/src/data/garage_key_repository_impl.dart` (class `GarageKeyRepositoryImpl`, the bytes and `revealBytes`) and the display widget `lib/src/ui/widgets/garage_key_view.dart` (class `GarageKeyView`, `revealBech32`, in its own members only, so a stateless widget whose copy button is a closure of its build). `GarageKeyRepositoryImpl` maps the RoboSats network to its own slot (testnet to `testnet`, mainnet to `mainnet`); an imported key arrives parsed by `GarageKey.parse`, and the copy of its bytes is zeroed when the vault returns. Its surface lets out whether a key is stored, robots derived from it, the key a draw has just made (`create`, built from the bytes the vault drew, for the record of its history; the draw use case hands it to no one), and, for the Garage only, the key of a network (`read`), never its bytes or its text: a key leaves it only as a `NetworkGarageKey`, tagged with the network of the slot it came from, and a robot only as a `NetworkRobot`, which only a `NetworkGarageKey` makes, with its own network, so that a robot of the testnet key cannot be handed to a mainnet call (`FetchRobotUsecase` reads the network from the robot); `test/network_garage_key_test.dart` pins, with the analyzer's resolution (so through a `typedef`, a dot shorthand or a prefix too), that only the repository constructs a `NetworkGarageKey`; the language cannot forbid it, since the constructor is public. The gate sees where the bytes are read, not where they go, so `features/p2p/test/garage_key_surface_test.dart` pins, from the syntax tree, the full signatures of its public methods to those of `GarageKeyRepository`, its single field and constructor parameter, the absence of `part` files, and the absence of any field in a P2P failure but the reason of an invalid key. `features/p2p` enables `avoid_print`. The use cases that generate, import, find, use and delete a key are internal to the feature, and their failures are the sealed `P2pFailure` family (`lib/src/domain/p2p_failure.dart`), types without a message. Callers follow the rules of the vault (`packages/secrets/README.md`): create or import only on an explicit action of the user, never at startup, in a constructor or in the background, and only after a read found no key; a stored key is never replaced (`GarageKeyAlreadyStoredFailure` is reported, never worked around); a key that cannot be read is left alone until the user deletes it. Only `lib/public/p2p_facade.dart`, `lib/src/p2p_locator.dart`, that repository and the test seam `lib/testing.dart` (which registers the Garage over a handle a test made) import `package:secrets`, each with a `show` list that `features/p2p/test/secrets_imports_test.dart` pins, reading the directives from the syntax tree.
- The custody gate since #47 (rules and limits in `packages/secrets/tool/README.md`) is a fence on named symbols, signatures and places, not a data-flow proof. It scans every Dart file under `lib/`, `packages/*/lib/` and `features/*/lib/` but `packages/secrets`, generated files included, and fails on a symbolic link there, on a package or analysis configuration under those roots, on a root without a Dart file, and on an import, export or part that names a file of the repository it does not scan. `Secrets.garageKeys` is allowed once, in the static `AppLocator.setupP2p` of `lib/locator.dart`, as `withGarageKeys ? <vault>.garageKeys() : null` passed to `P2pFeature.setup`; `startApp` of `lib/bootstrap.dart` is called only by `main` of `lib/main.dart`, which no code names, and `Bull.init` only by `startApp`; `AppLocator.setup` gets a boolean literal for `withGarageKeys`, `true` only as a statement of `Bull.init`; `setupP2p` is called only as a statement of `setup`, with its flag; any other reference to these functions fails, and no `package:get_it` call has `GarageKeys` in its type arguments or in a type literal it is passed. The derived Garage Key (`Secret.garageKey`, `SecretDerivation.garageKey`, #81) is refused in every production file, with no exception yet. No production library but the vault's own re-exports a declaration of `package:secrets`, in `features/*/lib` and `packages/*/lib` only the P2P facade, locator, repository and test seam refer to the vault, each to the declarations of the table `_vaultUsers`, and no other file there invokes a member of a value typed with a vault declaration; no production file imports `package:p2p/testing.dart`. `GarageKeyRepositoryImpl` and `GarageKeyView` declare exactly the public members, signatures and supertypes of the table `_garageKeyReaders`, written for the surface of #54 (with `create` returning the key it drew since #60); their supertypes from the workspace declare abstract members only; their files declare only what the table lists, with no part and no dynamic access; no extension on them exists and their private members are named only inside them; the P2P failures they name carry no field, mixed in or inherited, but `GarageKeyInvalidFailure.reason`. The table pins the signatures the analyzer prints and the internal libraries of their types, so an upgrade of Flutter, of the analyzer or of `primitives`, or an upstream merge, can fail the gate, closed: the README says how to update it. A change to those surfaces, to the files that use the vault or to the composition changes the gate's tables in the same pull request. It does not see what the values a reader returns let out (the `GarageKey` inside a `NetworkGarageKey` derives every robot token; `GarageKeyView.build` hands the text to `GarageKeyClipboard`, which it does not check), where a value goes inside a reader's members, or `packages/robosats`' hand-written derivation. The source check of `background_tasks_keystore_test.dart` and the feature's tests `secrets_imports_test.dart` and `garage_key_surface_test.dart` stay.
- A robot on a coordinator (`CoordinatorClient.fetchRobot` and `fetchKnownRobot`, `FetchRobotUsecase`, `docs/spikes/robot-coordinator.md`): the answer of a coordinator is accepted only when its hash id and nickname are the ones this device derives from the token, compared before any key of the answer is read, and its OpenPGP key pair is either the pair this very first contact sent or a pair the robot's token proves (`UnprovenCoordinatorRobot.prove`: the private key unlocks with the token and holds the secret values of the public key; a coordinator receives the SHA-256 of the token, never the token, and the S2K that locks the private key is salted and iterated over the token itself, so only a holder of the token can have made that pair). Anything else is a typed failure (`RobotHashIdMismatch`, `RobotNicknameMismatch`, `RobotPgpKeyNotProven`), never a silent change of key as in the official client, which adopts whatever pair a coordinator returns. So the pair is not stored: a read with the short header sends no pair and always proves the pair it gets back, after a restart or for a robot of an imported key alike. The proof holds only because each secret part is locked by an iterated and salted S2K over the token (`ProtectedSecret.provesPassphraseWith`, which the parser requires and the proof checks again): a simple S2K over SHA-256 would derive exactly the token hash every coordinator holds. « Proven » means made by a holder of the token, nothing more: a coordinator can return any genuine pair of the robot it ever received (the use case makes a new one for each first contact the user asks for, and sends that same pair again when it retries that request after a busy answer), a different one at each read, so a proven pair is not necessarily the one the coordinator stored first nor the one it will check signatures with; and `CoordinatorRobot.pgpKeyProven` is the coordinator's word, like `found` (it makes it true by returning the very pair sent in other packet framing), so no screen may rely on it alone to say that a robot existed before. A proven pair is re-encoded from the packets the proof checked (primary key, certified user ID and its certification, the selected subkey and its binding, each signature without its unhashed area): whatever else a coordinator put in the blocks is dropped, so what a robot holds is bounded by the key its owner made. A proof hashes 32 MiB of SHA-256 for a key of the official client or of Troc, and at most `PgpLimits.proofHashedBytes` (62 MiB, both secret parts and every S2K pass together), a pair that would hash more, one GnuPG protected at its largest count included, being refused before anything is hashed; it wipes the passphrase bytes and the secret values it unlocked, and the robot keeps only the locked pair. `FetchRobotUsecase` makes the pair of each first contact itself, for that robot, after the binding and before the request, so that no caller can send a pair this device did not just make for that robot (a pair that comes back as sent is taken without a proof): a first contact is a `RobotFirstContact` (`RobotContact.firstContact`), which keeps the pair made at its first request and sends it again at each request made with it, so that a request sent again never hands the coordinator a second pair, and a read back is a `RobotReadBack`; it makes and proves pairs only through `RobotKeyComputePort`, whose implementation `IsolateRobotKeyCompute` runs each in `Isolate.run`, from static methods so that the isolate gets nothing but its one argument. What crosses to the isolate: to make a pair, the robot's whole `RobotIdentity` (its token, both forms of its token hash and its two Nostr secret keys); to prove one, the `UnprovenCoordinatorRobot`, which holds the token, the two blocks of the answer and public facts only. That isolate shares the heap of the app's isolates, so it keeps the screens fluid, it does not keep the token or the unlocked values apart in memory, and copies stay unwiped until collected: the token itself (a `String`, which cannot be wiped), its bytes in the list `s2kIteratedSalted` builds the salt and the token from and in the hash's own buffers, the big-endian copy of the scalar and the list made to reverse it, the key objects `pinenacl` builds, and the copies of the identity a message makes. The answer itself is parsed on the calling isolate before the proof, three times: a key block with more than `PgpLimits.keySignatures` (8) signature packets, where a robot key has 2, is refused before any is verified, so an answer costs at most 24 Ed25519 verifications there (a hostile block of 32 packets cost about 80, about 300 ms under JIT, before that bound, #69). `features/p2p/test/robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` pins that only that file proves a robot or makes a pair, that nothing in the feature unlocks one or refers to the package's internals of the proof (`RobotPgpKey.proveMadeBy`, `CoordinatorRobotAnswer.parse`, `UnprovenCoordinatorRobot.parse`), and that no file of `lib/` silences a diagnostic but those the generators write (lints and unused imports) and the one `experimental_member_use` of the table migration of `p2p_database.dart` (« One robot, one coordinator » below); it resolves every Dart file of `lib/`, the translations included. Troc's hand-written code (`features/p2p`, `packages/robosats` and Troc's own files of the shell), tests included, has no `// ignore` and no `// ignore_for_file`: the single exception is that `// ignore: experimental_member_use` on the line of `TableMigration(schema.garageKeyHistories)` in `features/p2p/lib/src/data/p2p_database.dart`, which drift marks experimental and the wallet's own migrations silence the same way, and which that test pins to its line; generated files keep what their generators write, and an `ignore` of `invalid_use_of_internal_member` is refused anywhere by `make custody-ci-check`. Every request made for a robot carries its `RobotSessionKey`, and the transport never lets it share a Tor session or a connection with another robot or with an anonymous read, and refuses a credential that names no robot: keep it so in any new authenticated call, and extend that guard, which looks at the `Authorization` header only, when a credential travels elsewhere (the chat websocket puts the token hash in a query parameter). Only `NostrKeyDerivation.currentFrontend` is sent for a real robot. A key object holds canonical armor only, so nothing but base64 goes from a key into a header. The robot use cases stay inside `features/p2p`: `package:p2p/p2p.dart` has no robot method and exports no type that makes, holds or reveals a robot (`GarageKey`, `RobotIdentity`, `RobotPgpKey`…), so the shell cannot handle RoboSats key material out of reach of the custody rules; a test of the feature pins it. Creating a robot is always explicit: only a `RobotFirstContact` sends a key pair. The live test `integration_test/p2p_robot_tor_test.dart` creates throwaway robots on testnet coordinators only; never point an authenticated test at mainnet, and never write a Garage Key, a token, an `Authorization` header or text a coordinator chose to a log, a fixture or a report.
- Robot requests (#29): who may call what, and how often.
  Only `PutRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase` (the user's request to put a robot on the coordinator the user chose) and `ReadRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase` (the read back from the coordinator the robot's binding names) call `FetchRobotUsecase`, and only through `RobotRequestLimiter`; only `PutRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase` makes a first contact (`RobotContact.firstContact`); `ReleaseRobotUsecase` is the only caller of `CloseRobotSessionsUsecase`, which is the only caller of `CoordinatorSessionsPort.closeRobotSessions`: `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` pins all four.
  `ReadRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase` takes the coordinator from the binding (`standingOf`), never from a screen, and sends nothing for a robot without a binding (`RobotNotBoundFailure`), bound on the other network (`RobotBoundToAnotherCoordinatorFailure`) or whose coordinator the federation list of the app no longer has on that network (`RobotCoordinatorChangedFailure`).
  `PutRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase` reads the binding first: a robot bound to another coordinator, or to one that changed, is sent nothing; a robot already bound to that coordinator is read back first, with the short header, and gets a first contact only when the coordinator answers `RobotUnknown`, so a coordinator that stored a first contact whose answer was lost never receives a second pair for it; a robot without a binding goes straight to its first contact.
  `RobotRequestLimiter`, one for the feature (a lazy singleton, in memory, for the run of the app), keeps at most 2 robot requests in flight per coordinator and network, whatever their robot, and the others wait in the order asked; the trade use cases of #87 (their reads of an order, the take, the cancel) go through it too.
  Each call opens its requests for its robot (`open`) and closes them when it ends; a screen that releases what it opened gives its calls a `RobotCallOwner` (the Garage's panel of a robot, one per panel cubit, handed to `PutRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase.execute` and `ReadRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase.execute` as `owner`), and `release(robot, owner:)` drops that owner's requests still waiting, stops that owner's calls opened so far (`RobotReleasedFailure`) and ends their waits at once, a request already in flight running to its end, its answer returned to its call and dropped by the screen that is gone. Since the security review of #251 (PR 274, C1) a release never stops a call of another owner or of none: every call of a trade (its watch and step 0's read, its chat, its actions, the take, the make, the keyring's read) is opened without an owner and goes on, and still counts for `isInUse`; before, the panel closing when the Garage moved stopped the page of that robot's trade shown above it, which said « Cannot reach … ».
  Both robot use cases open their call before they read the robot's binding, so that a release during that read stops the call before anything is bound or sent (security review of step 5).
  A busy answer of the transport (`CoordinatorTransportBusy`) is counted for its coordinator before its place goes to the next request, then sent again, up to 4 attempts per request, after waits of 2, 4 and 8 s each spread by ±20 % at random, holding no place; more than 12 busy answers from one coordinator within 10 minutes make its requests fail at once with `RobotCoordinatorBusyFailure` until enough of them are older; a busy answer to a call released meanwhile counts too.
  The limiter is given a time that only moves forward (the locator's `Stopwatch`), a delay and a randomness, never `Random()` or the wall clock, so that a clock set forward or back neither opens nor closes a coordinator and tests control every wait.
  No other failure is sent again, a timeout or a transport failure included: the request may have reached the coordinator, and the next `PutRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase` of the robot, bound by then, reads it back before any new first contact.
  A nickname taken (error 7004), which the client of the package has read back once with the short header, is read back once more 10 s later, its pair proven: found, the robot is taken; still unknown, it is `RobotNicknameTaken`, the index stays bound to that coordinator and nothing moves to the next robot.
  Each coordinator and network has a cap of 3 such refusals per run: a first contact holds a slot of it from before its request until its outcome is known, so first contacts running at once never pass it together, and a nickname taken that the read 10 s later does not resolve (still unknown, unanswered, or never sent because the robot was released) uses the slot up; with no slot left, a first contact fails at once with `RobotCoordinatorTooManyRefusalsFailure`, before anything is written or sent for it (`refusesFirstContacts` tells a screen beforehand); the cap is in memory, and a persisted one comes with phase 2's automatic choice of the next robot.
  `ReleaseRobotUsecase` waits for the requests of the robot in flight, those of calls an earlier release stopped included, 10 s at most, then closes its sessions, and again once a late request has ended, unless a call of the robot still runs (one opened since, or a trade's): it then leaves the sessions to it and closes them, without the caller waiting, once the limiter says the robot is idle (`RobotRequestLimiter.idle`: no call open, no request of a released call in flight), never under a request to a coordinator in flight. The Nostr copies of a chat message are not counted: they go through the robot's Tor sessions outside the limiter and are not waited for, so when the Garage has left the robot of a trade, a copy sent just before the user leaves that trade's page may fail as the sessions close; the message itself still reaches the coordinator, and nothing is shown. It was already so before #274, whose release closed the sessions at once (security review of PR 274, R5; #275).
  An answer is parsed on the calling isolate before any proof, at most 24 Ed25519 verifications (above); a `PutRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase` parses at most two answers (the first read of a bound robot that finds it, or the answer to its first contact or to the client's own read after a 7004, and the read 10 s later), 48 verifications, a `ReadRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase` one, 24; a busy or failed request carries no answer to parse.
  The robot use cases accept both networks (owner's decision of 2026-11-12: creating and reading a robot moves no money), and what they show is a `RobotOnCoordinatorFace` (order ids, rewards, the status of the Nostr key the coordinator holds, whether the first contact a put sent was answered with another key pair, proven, so that the robot was there before; a robot that the put's first read finds, with no first contact sent, does not count), with no token, key or fingerprint.
  Every failure they add is a type without a field, and `p2p_failure_l10n.dart` gives each its text in the robot panel (`toRobotText`, which names the coordinator).
- The robot panel of the Garage (#29), under the robot navigator of the profile: « Coordinator that knows your robot: », the reference's « Coordinators that know your robot: » in the singular, since a robot lives on one coordinator here.
  `RobotCoordinatorCubit` (one per robot shown, a `BlocProvider` keyed by the key and the index, closed when the user moves to another robot or leaves the Garage) holds the `NetworkGarageKey` of the Garage as `GarageCubit` does, and derives the robot from it only when it calls a use case, refusing an index the key does not give back as it is.
  It is the only caller of `PutRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase` but the take of #87 (which puts the fresh robot of a trade on the order's coordinator), `ReadRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase`, `ReleaseRobotUsecase` and `AcknowledgeGarageKeyHistoryUsecase`, and only the panel calls its `putOn`, `retry` and `acknowledge`; it, its states and every widget of `lib/src/ui/` reach a robot request only through them, never `FetchRobotUsecase`, the coordinator client, the limiter, the bindings, the database, a credential or a raw client: `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` pins it.
  Its states hold faces only: the coordinator as the federation list gives it, and what the use cases return (`RobotOnCoordinatorFace`), never a token, a key, a fingerprint or `pgpKeyProven`; « already existed » is the use case's `alreadyExisted`, said whenever a first contact the panel asked for, after « Select Host » or « Retry », came back with another pair, and never for a robot the put found by its first read.
  It reads where the robot stands on the device (`ReadRobotStandingUsecase`) at each display, follows Tor (`WatchP2pTorStateUsecase`, which starts nothing: the book of the tab starts Tor), and reads a robot bound on its network back from the coordinator of its binding as soon as Tor is ready, as the reference reads its robots at each display.
  The coordinator of a binding is resolved by `ListRobotHostsUsecase` (`RobotHosts.boundTo`, through `RobotBinding.admits`): a coordinator that left the list or changed its address or keys shows « changed » and nothing is sent; a robot bound on the other network says so, and nothing is sent.
  A first contact happens only on `putOn`, for a robot without a binding, whose key needs no acknowledgement, once Tor is ready, which the screen calls after « Select Host » (the coordinators of the list with an onion address on the robot's network, in federation order, « Coordinator offline » when the book of the tab could not reach them, a coordinator closed by the limiter's refusal cap disabled with its text, the existing coordinator dialog behind an info button) and « Continue » on « Send {nickname} to {coordinator}? »; a bound robot is never given a coordinator by the screen.
  Choosing a coordinator in the Garage is optional: nothing in the panel says a robot must be sent before trading (phase 2 binds a free robot when an order is taken).
  After a failed first contact the cubit reads the standing again and shows the failure where the robot now stands; « Retry » of a bound robot reads it back, except after « does not know this robot » (`RobotUnknown`) or a key refused as too recent, where it puts the robot on the coordinator of its binding again, which `PutRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase` does by reading it back first and making a first contact only when the coordinator does not know it.
  When the database cannot be read right after a failed first contact, whose request may have left, the panel keeps what that request got and says that the records cannot be read (`p2pRobotRecordsUnreadable`), never « Nothing was sent. »; « Retry » reads the database again and keeps the failure until it can.
  A put the database refuses (`P2pStorageFailure`) may follow a request of the same call, the read back of a robot bound earlier or the first contact whose nickname was taken and read again 10 s later: such a refusal, shown on a bound robot (`RobotFailed.kind` says the call was a put) or with the records unreadable, says only that the records cannot be read; on a robot still without a coordinator nothing was bound, so nothing left, and a read back the database refuses sent nothing: both say « Nothing was sent. » (second security pass on step 5).
  The acknowledgement of a key of unknown history (#29, step 6): for a robot without a binding whose key is neither recorded as drawn here nor acknowledged on the robot's network (`RobotStanding.needsAcknowledgement`: pasted, drawn before the records, or whose record failed), « Select Host », enabled once Tor is ready as for any key, first opens « Robots from elsewhere » (`RobotHistoryDialog`: the key was pasted in or its draw could not be recorded, its robots may already exist on coordinators since the official client sends each robot it shows to every coordinator, a robot lives on one coordinator here so each goes where the user used it, and a new key starts clean; factual, for a key from the user's own backup as for any other).
  Only « I understand » goes on: the panel calls the cubit's `acknowledge`, which calls `AcknowledgeGarageKeyHistoryUsecase` and reads the standing again, and the sheet opens only when the state then lets a coordinator be chosen (`RobotWithoutCoordinator.mayChooseHost`); « Cancel », a system back or a tap outside records nothing and opens nothing; a second tap while the dialog closes records nothing more and pops nothing under it (the navigator absorbs the pointers of the frame after a pop, and a closing route ignores them, so the dialog needs no guard of its own); the tail of a double tap 16 to 120 ms apart may close the « Select Host » sheet that has just opened, as any tap outside it does, rather than reach the Garage under it: tests pin both.
  It holds for the key on that network: the other robots of the key go straight to the sheet, the same key in the other slot asks again there, and an import of the key makes it `imported` again, so it is asked again; a record that fails shows the storage text on the robot (`RobotWithoutCoordinator.acknowledgementFailure`), opens nothing, sends nothing, and « Select Host » asks again.
  `putOn` refuses by itself a robot whose key needs it, before any use case, and the bind of `FetchRobotUsecase` refuses it again (`RobotHistoryUnknownFailure`).
  Nothing else records it. `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` restricts who names the members: only that use case refers to `recordAcknowledgedKey`, only the cubit calls the use case, and only the panel calls `acknowledge`, `putOn` and `retry`, from its own widgets only (no extension, top-level function or variable of its file, and no extension of a watched class anywhere in `lib/`); the public members of `RobotCoordinatorCubit` are pinned exactly, so that no new one wraps a use case. The behaviour tests guard when the members are called (only on « I understand »; no test seam records it, and the captures go through the dialog).
  What the panel opens (« Robots from elsewhere », « Select Host », the confirmation, and the coordinator dialog behind an info button of « Select Host », since the second security pass on #77) closes with it when it goes away, the robot, the key or the network changing under it or the Garage left (`ClosesWithPanel`: its own route is removed at once, whatever sits above it, and the future its opener awaits completes as a dismissal), so that none stays above a Garage it no longer belongs to; a sheet that the coordinator dialog opens to show one of its links for copy does not close with it (the coordinator's public text, read-only); and a closed cubit records and sends nothing, each of its public methods returning at once (security review of step 6).
  A robot bound after it carries the provenance `acknowledged`, the key's history on the robot's own network only (« One robot, one coordinator » below); nothing else changes for such robots in this ticket: phase 2 decides what they may do with an order.
  The progress bar of a request runs with reduced motion too, as the waits of the tab do before they know a share; after 30 s the panel says it is still waiting.
  Deviations from the reference, for the rule « one robot, one coordinator »: no automatic first contact, one coordinator per robot, only the bound coordinator read back, a returned key pair taken only when proven, nothing of the coordinator's answer stored, no Nostr recovery event, the wizard local (the panel is on the profile only), orders shown as plain text (no order page yet), no « Claim » of rewards, the settings of the robot (Telegram, webhook, stealth invoices) not shown, the confirmation before the first contact, which the reference does not ask, and « Robots from elsewhere » before a robot of a key of unknown history goes anywhere, which the reference has no need of since it sends every robot everywhere.
- **The design notes of the phases.** Phase 2, taking an order (#12): `docs/design/phase-2-taking-an-order.md`. Phase 3, making an order (#13): `docs/design/phase-3-making-an-order.md`, written before its code: its proposals and the open decisions of its § 7 become rules of this file only with the pull requests that build them.
- An order as a robot sees it, the take and the cancel (#84, phase 2): `CoordinatorClient.fetchOrder`, `takeOrder` and `cancelOrder` of `packages/robosats` (its README, section « an order, the take and the cancel ») read `GET /api/order/` as a `CoordinatorOrder` and send a taker's take and cancel, with the robot's short header and session. The trade use cases of #87 call them: each takes a `RobotIdentity`, so `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` allows `fetchOrder` in `lib/src/domain/usecases/trade/trade_reader.dart`, `takeOrder` in `take_order_usecase.dart` and `cancelOrder` in `cancel_trade_usecase.dart` only (« The trade use cases (#87) » below). A `POST` is sent once: neither the client nor the transport (`tor_coordinator_transport_test.dart`, « never sends a POST again … ») sends it again, a timeout included, since the coordinator may have acted; the next read tells. A take answered `200` is a take only when the answer shows the robot as the pre-taker with its bond (`OrderTakeStarted`); `OrderAlreadyTakenByThisRobot` is a trade still running, and `OrderNotTaken` the order as it is, which the reference coordinator answers to a take on an order that is no longer public (`api/views.py:595-596`). The guard `cancel_status` is compared with the status the coordinator stores, where a pre-take is public (1), never 3: `cancelOrder` takes the last read of the order and always sends that guard, 1 for a pre-take, so that a cancel that arrives after a bond locked is refused instead of costing the bond. A take or a cancel fails with an `OrderActionFailure`: `OrderActionRefused` only for the failures known to come before the action, `OrderActionOutcomeUnknown` for every other (the answer is the read after the action), which a trade use case answers by reading the order again, never by sending the action again.
- The buyer's payout (#89, phase 2): `CoordinatorClient.submitPayoutInvoice` and `submitPayoutAddress` of `packages/robosats` (its README, section « the buyer's payout ») send `update_invoice` and `update_address` with the robot's short header and session, taking the robot and the last read of the order as `cancelOrder` does. They are on the money members' list, and only the payout use case of #90 (`lib/src/domain/usecases/trade/submit_payout_usecase.dart`, « The buyer's payout use case and the trade keyring (#90) » below) names them. What they send is a `SignedPayoutInvoice` or `SignedPayoutAddress`, which only `UnlockedRobotPgpKey.signPayoutInvoice` and `signPayoutAddress` make, never free text; the rule for #90 and #91: **what is signed is exactly what was decoded and shown**, so the use case decodes and checks the invoice string itself (amount `RoutingBudgetPpm.invoiceSats` of the buyer's amount, network, expiry; design note § 4.4) or checks the address with `BitcoinAddress.check`, shows that very text, signs it, compares `SignedPayout.text` with what was shown, and never signs a string read again or edited after the screen showed it. `BitcoinAddress.check` is a pre-check (network, checksum, kind: P2PKH, P2SH, P2WPKH, P2WSH, P2TR), not a verdict: the coordinator's bitcoind decides, and a valid segwit address of a later version is refused as unsupported. Refusals are kinds without coordinator text (`PayoutInvoiceRefused` with its `PayoutInvoiceRefusal`, `PayoutAddressRefused` with its `PayoutAddressRefusal`, `PayoutMiningFeeTooLow`, `PayoutMiningFeeTooHigh`, `PayoutBelowDustLimit`, `PayoutSignatureRefused`, `PayoutNotBuyer`, `PayoutNotNow`, `PayoutSwapUnavailable`, `PayoutSignedByAnotherRobot`, `OrderNotParticipant`), each an `OrderActionRefused` since the coordinator records no payout before them (it may mark an unused `OnchainPayment` cancelled or create one, `api/logics.py:866`, harmless); everything else is an `OrderActionOutcomeUnknown`, answered by reading the order again, never by sending the payout again. `PayoutNotBuyer`, `PayoutNotNow` and `PayoutSwapUnavailable` may also be decided here from a stale last read, nothing sent (`PayoutRefused.fromLastRead`): after such a refusal the use case reads the order again and tries once more from the fresh read before telling the user anything, since at 6 or 8 the buyer's timer costs the bond. A signed payout is not tied to an order or a step: the use case signs just before submitting, from the fresh read, and never reuses a signed payout across orders or steps; signing (`signPayoutInvoice`, `signPayoutAddress`) happens only in the payout use case's file, and unlocking the robot's key only in the key work's isolate (`IsolateRobotKeyCompute.unlock`, off the UI isolate) at the request of the trade keyring alone, which `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` pins alongside the money members (#90). The routing budget is always sent (0 to 99,998 ppm, 1,000 by default) and so is the mining fee rate (2 to 500 sat/vB, 3 decimals at most): without it the coordinator would use its own suggestion unchecked.
- The chat step's actions (#95, phase 2): `CoordinatorClient.confirmFiat` (the buyer's « fiat sent »), `undoConfirmFiat` (« Payment failed? »), `openDispute` and `ratePlatform` of `packages/robosats` (its README, section « the chat step's actions ») send `confirm`, `undo_confirm`, `dispute` and `rate_platform` with the robot's short header and session, taking the robot and the last read of the order as `cancelOrder` does; the collaborative cancel is `cancelOrder` itself at 8 or 9, guarded as every cancel. `confirmFiat`, `undoConfirmFiat` and `openDispute` are on the money members' list (named before they existed, #83): once « fiat sent » is recorded the buyer can no longer cancel and the seller may release the escrow, and a dispute settles the escrow and both bonds to the coordinator at once; `ratePlatform` moves nothing and is not. Each is refused before sending (`OrderActionRefused`, `TradeActionRefused.fromLastRead`) when the last read does not allow it: `confirmFiat` only by the buyer at 9, `undoConfirmFiat` only by the buyer at 10, `openDispute` only by a participant at 9 or 10 once the client's clock has passed `expires_at` minus `CoordinatorClient.disputeWindow` (18 hours, a constant of the reference's code: never easier than the coordinator), `ratePlatform` only by a participant. The coordinator's refusals 1013, 1027, 1029, 1030, 1031, 1047 and 1054 come before it changes anything; every other failure is an `OrderActionOutcomeUnknown`, which a use case answers by reading the order again, never by sending the action again. An answer `200` at a status the action cannot lead to (`fiatSentFollows`, `fiatSentUndoneFollows`, `disputeFollows`, from `api/logics.py`: never 9 after « fiat sent », for instance) is malformed, so an outcome unknown, never done. The scripted coordinator of `lib/testing.dart` plays them, the collaborative cancel with `ScriptedCoordinator.peerAsksToCancel` and `peerAcceptsCancel`, and a request that lands after the reads that follow it with `ScriptedFault.landsLate` and `landLate`; the seller's side (#96: the escrow invoice at 6 and 7, `lockEscrow`, the seller's `confirm` refused with 1027 or 1028, answered `200` at 10 without settling, or completed on chain) and its known gaps are written in its doc comment.
- Making an order and pausing it (#175, phase 3): `CoordinatorClient.makeOrder` and `pauseOrder` of `packages/robosats` (its README, section « making an order and pausing it »), both money members: the make use case of #179 calls `makeOrder`, and the pause's of #184 (`PauseOrderUsecase`) calls `pauseOrder`, each in its own trade use case file. `makeOrder(coordinator, network, identity:, request:)` sends `POST /api/make/` once with the robot's short header and session (the reference sends the first-contact header: the phase 2 deviation, the robot being put on the coordinator first; a coordinator that knows the robot takes from that header only its Nostr key, to replace a placeholder key, `robosats/middleware.py:93-107`, which the short header does not carry) and the reference client's body, its 17 keys in the reference's order (`Order.make`, `frontend/src/models/Order.model.ts:196-215`, commit ff1998c), built only from a `MakeOrderRequest`: a checked value whose every field is bounded as the coordinator stores it (`MakeOrderSerializer` and the order model's validators, `api/serializers.py:587-627`, `api/models/order.py:77-184`), refused (`tryCreate` gives `null`) rather than rounded or cleaned: amounts above 0 and below 10^10 with at most 8 decimals, a range from 1.5 to 15 times its minimum, the coordinator's nominal bounds (`api/logics.py:164-175`; the coordinator compares sats it computes in floating point at its price of the moment, so a range at a bound may still get 1006 or 1009, a refusal known to come before the order is made, and the reference client's margins 1.6 and 14.8 are for the form of #183), a payment method of 1 to 70 code points and a description of 1 to 240 that `displayText` leaves as they are with no space at either end (the coordinator's serializer trims, so an untrimmed text would be stored otherwise than sent), and since #181 every character of which is seen as written (`isShownAsWritten`: never a text that shows as empty, nor an invisible or format character, the tag block, a space but U+0020, private use, or a joiner, a variation selector or a combining mark out of place, from the security review of #194), a premium above -100 and at most 999 with 2 decimals, a public duration of 600 s to 24 h and an escrow duration of 30 min to 10 h in whole seconds, a bond size with 2 decimals, any currency but bitcoin (1000). Not sent, `null` in the body as the reference writes it when unset: a price in sats. Since #189 the request carries a meeting point (`OrderLocation`, at most 6 decimals each, the coordinator's `decimal_places`) and a password as its SHA-256 only (`OrderPasswordHash`, below, « The order password and the meeting point (#189) »). The `201` answer is read as a `MadeOrder` only when it has every field of `ListOrderSerializer` but `maker` and `taker` (internal user ids, read as nothing), `id` 1 to 2^53, status 0, `expires_at` after `created_at`, and every term equal to the request's; it holds no bond invoice and does not prove that this robot is the maker: only the first `fetchOrder` by the robot, which checks `is_maker` against its hash id, does, so the use case shows nothing before that read. The answer does not carry the public duration, the description or the password (`api/serializers.py:83-107`), and fields beyond the serializer's are ignored, so those are not checked here: the first read gives them (`expires_at` at status 1, the creation plus the public duration; `description`; `has_password`), and the make use case of #179 compares them with what it asked. A make fails with `OrderActionRefused` (nothing was made) only for the empty `400` of the form check, `401`/`403`, 1036 and 7000/7003 (`RobotCredentialsRejected`), 7001 (`RobotUnknown`), 1037 (`OrderBookFull`), 1038 and 1039 (`OrderRequestRefused` with the code, unreachable with a checked request), 1000 to 1003 (`RobotHasActiveOrder`), 1004 to 1009 (`OrderSizeRefused` with its `OrderSizeRefusal`), 1010 (`OrderLocationRefused`), and `CoordinatorNotOnNetwork` or `CoordinatorTransportBusy` (nothing sent); everything else, a `201` that differs, a timeout or a lost connection included, is `OrderActionOutcomeUnknown`: an order may exist, which reading the robot (`fetchKnownRobot`, its active or last order) tells, and the make is never sent again. `pauseOrder(identity:, lastRead:)` sends `{"action":"pause"}` (`TradeBox/index.tsx:310-313`), **a toggle with no direction** (`Logics.pause_unpause_public_order`, `api/logics.py:1758-1782`: 1 to 2 and 2 to 1, whatever the maker saw), so the use case of #184 reads the order first and sends only when the fresh read shows the status the user saw, and never sends it twice; nothing is sent when the last read is another robot's (`OrderReadElsewhere`), does not show this robot as the maker (`TradeActionNotMaker`, new) or is not at 1 or 2 (`TradeActionNotNow`), each with `fromLastRead`; its answer is `TradeActionAccepted` at a status of `pauseFollows` (1, 2, 5, 6, 7, 8), any other an outcome unknown; The package does not say which way the toggle went: an answer at the status of the last read means the order was toggled the other way, or twice (the same robot paused or resumed it from another client meanwhile, for instance), so the caller compares the status of the answer with the one it wanted, shows the answer's, and tells the user when the order stayed or became public again, since a public order can be taken with the maker's bond at stake; 1032, 1033 and 1047, the empty `400` and the authentication's refusals are `OrderActionRefused`, everything else unknown. Error 1022 on the maker's read at 0 past its deadline (`api/logics.py:1254-1256`) is `OrderMakerBondExpired`, the order then read at 5 with `OrderExpiryReason.makerBondNotLocked`; after an action it is an outcome unknown, as every code of the read that follows an action. The package's tests build every answer from the reference's code, never recorded (`test/coordinator/make_order_test.dart`, `pause_order_test.dart`, `test/support/make_answers.dart`), the maker's reads at 0, 1, 2 and 5 written out field by field from `OrderView.get`. Deviations, declared: the short header (above), a premium of exactly -100 refused (the coordinator accepts it and then divides by a price of zero), a public duration from 600 s where the coordinator takes 597.6 s (the reference client's shortest, 00:10), and the make's answer read strictly (a `201` that differs from the request is an outcome unknown, where the reference client takes any answer with an `id`, `MakerForm.tsx:281-286`).
- **The trade's state machine (#86, phase 2): the only place that turns a coordinator's answer into what a screen may show or a wallet may pay.** `features/p2p/lib/src/domain/trade/`: `TradeStatus.from(previous:, answer:, terms:, now:, reader:)` is pure (no clock, no I/O, no randomness) and gives one class per status a taker can see, from a `TradeAnswer` (the `CoordinatorOrder` of a read, a take or a cancel, or error 1043 or 1044: `TradeAnswer.ofRead`) checked against the `TradeTerms` fixed at the take (`TradeTerms.take` from the public order, or `TradeTerms.takeRead` from a read of it, the amount taken and the coordinator's `taker_fee`, refused with `TakerFeeTooHigh` above `TradeTolerances.takerFeeCeiling`, 1 %) and against the trade's history (`TradeTrail`: the last position, the payment hash of the first invoice of each step, the last deadline, the pre-take's last read and deadline, any breach of trust, and whether the history is unknown). There is no status without a trail, and a trail belongs to one trade (order, coordinator, network, role: `from` refuses other terms with `TradeOrderMismatch`). A trade starts from the take's own answer, `TradeStatus.afterTake(OrderTakeStarted, …)` (the package's outcome that only `takeOrder` gives); otherwise `previous` is the last status, or `TradeStatus.beforeFirstRead(trail)` of a trail that only `TradeTrail.restore(record, terms:, now:)` (a recorded trade), `TradeTrail.forPendingTake` (a take whose answer was lost and which has no trail recorded yet: only a take's answers may come first) and `TradeTrail.recovered` (a trade found by recovery: anything may come first, and every status carries `TradeHistoryUnknown`, so the wallet never pays on it) make; the take's own fresh trail is private to the state machine, and `trade_records_test.dart` pins that only `lib/src/domain/trade/` and `lib/src/domain/usecases/trade/` name these factories. A restored trail at an end gives an end at once. The edges are `TradeEdges.steps`, each single move of the reference backend for a taker with its roles and its lines (pinned by `trade_status_test.dart` against a list written out apart from the table); any path of them is allowed between two reads, an unlisted edge to a status that goes on gives it with `TradeStatusUnexpected`, an unlisted edge to an end, a status a taker is never shown, a role, coordinator or order that is not the trade's, and any return to the bond step but from the pre-take are refused as answers (the last status stays, with the warning); a breach of trust (those, and an invoice whose payment hash is not the first of its step) is remembered by the trail, and every later status carries `CoordinatorInconsistent`. A terminal status is never left. Error 1044 during the pre-take is `PreTakeUnavailable`, not an end, until the pre-take's deadline and `TradeTolerances.coordinatorLoops` have passed: a maker who pauses the order gives the pre-taker 1044 and the pre-take comes back after the pause (`api/views.py:269-275`, `api/logics.py:1758-1783`). Every failed check is a typed `TradeWarning` with an effect on the QR code (shown, hidden until « Show anyway », hidden), every tolerance a constant of `TradeTolerances` with its reason, and no check ever triggers an action. An invoice of a coordinator becomes a `CheckedInvoice` only inside the library of `TradeStatus` (`checked_invoice.dart` is a part of it, `_checkStepInvoice` private): decoded by the `Bolt11Decoder` port through `Bolt11Reader`, which bounds it to 2,000 visible ASCII characters and turns anything the decoder throws into a `Bolt11Refusal`, on the trade's network or an `UnusableInvoice`, carrying the exact text decoded and every warning of its read. « Pay with this wallet » is offered only by `walletPaymentFor(accepted:, status:, step:, now:)`, which takes the current status, never an invoice a screen kept: no warning at all on the status or its invoice, at most the amount the user accepted, and at least `TradeTolerances.walletPaymentMargin` left on the invoice and its step. `CheckedInvoice` (this state machine's, built only by its checks, with the payment hash, the step and every warning) is the checked value; `WalletPaymentOffered` is the only source of the wallet port's input, `CheckedBolt11Invoice` (`walletInvoice`, built in `trade/wallet_payment.dart` with the exact string, amount, network, payment hash, expiry and `payableUntil`), `wallet_port_architecture_test.dart` naming that file, the type's own and the testing seam's helper as the only callers of its factory; the wallet type's own strict parse of the human-readable part stays as a second, independent check, and an invoice it refuses is never offered (`WalletPaymentRefusal.walletRefused`). The port's implementation is the shell's, `lib/features/p2p_bolt11/bolt11_decoder_impl.dart` (it imports `bolt11_decoder`, `decimal` and `package:p2p/p2p.dart` only, pinned by its test), with the app's pinned `bolt11_decoder`, which checks no signature and recovers no payee key, and reads amounts leniently: the adapter reads the amount again in the strict form of BOLT 11 and bounds the expiry field. The payee is compared with the coordinator's node keys of the federation list only when the invoice states it (`n`), which a coordinator's LND hold invoice never does: in practice the payee is not verified.
  Rules for the trade use cases (#87): build the terms from a fresh read of the order just before the take (the book reloaded or `takeRead`), never from a book left open; on `takeOrder`'s outcome, `OrderTakeStarted` starts the trade with `TradeStatus.afterTake`, `OrderAlreadyTakenByThisRobot` resumes the trade from its recorded trail (or as `TradeTrail.recovered` when none was recorded), and `OrderNotTaken` makes no trade; a pending take whose answer was lost and which has no trail recorded is read once on `TradeTrail.forPendingTake`, and its architecture test pins that only the take use case and the resolution of a pending take name `afterTake` and `forPendingTake` (the domain cannot know whether a trail was recorded: that remains a written rule); store the terms' `TradeTermsRecord` once, with the pending take, and never write it again; store the trail's `TradeTrailRecord` after each read only when `record.mayReplace(stored)` (`whyNotReplace` names the reason otherwise), never from a read older than the stored one; the schema step adds database triggers: « set once » for the terms (as `trades_set_once` for `trades`, #102), and monotonic ones for the trail (a breach and an unknown history never cleared, a pinned hash never cleared or changed); resume a recorded trade only with `TradeTerms.restore` and `TradeTrail.restore(record, terms:, now:)`: a refused record (`TradeRecordMalformed`, `TradeRecordOfAnotherTrade`) is a failure shown to the user, and a trade whose coordinator left the list (`TradeCoordinatorGone`) is shown read-only; none is ever restarted on a fresh trail; keep the last `TradeStatus` and pass it as `previous`; a read that is no `TradeAnswer` keeps it; keep one record per step of « payment started for hash H of step S » (a seller pays a bond and an escrow), written before the wallet is called, so that no step is paid twice; close a pending take with no pre-take by their own rule as well (the state machine makes a 1044 right after the take final after `TradeTolerances.longestPreTake` and `coordinatorLoops`). Rules for the screens (#88): they show and pay only what a status holds, the QR code only when `CheckedInvoice.mayShow` (or `mayShowAnyway` after « Show anyway »), never an `UnusableInvoice`, and the wallet only after `walletPaymentFor` offers it for exactly the amount shown on the confirmation the user tapped, bound to its payment hash; the payment use case (#88, #100) reads the order again and recomputes the status immediately before it pays, and pays only from that status, never from one a screen kept (`walletPaymentFor` cannot know a status is the latest) (a take confirmation that shows an estimate states its ceiling, the bond × 1.02 + 1 sat); never « nothing was taken from you » on `PreTakeEnded`, `TakenByAnother` or `Cancelled` with `bondMayHaveBeenLost`; `Expired` tells its reason (the reference's `NESINV`, neither escrow nor payout, settled the taker's bond too); `DisputeWon` is paid through the robot's rewards, which the app cannot claim yet; no rule of their own on statuses, amounts or invoices. `features/p2p/test/trade_status_test.dart` and `trade_review_scenarios_test.dart` drive every status, every edge, every tolerance at its bound, restarts through the records, and seeded random sequences of answers against an independent oracle, through the package's own parser; `test/features/p2p_bolt11/bolt11_decoder_impl_test.dart` checks that the app's decoder reads the tests' invoices (`Bolt11TestInvoice` of `package:p2p/testing.dart`, never real ones) as the tests' decoder does, refuses lenient amounts and repeated or wrapping fields, and never throws.
- **One robot, one coordinator (rule to enforce in code before any robot reaches a second coordinator and before any order).** The token hash is the whole credential of a robot and is the same on every coordinator: a coordinator that received it can act as the robot on another coordinator in everything that needs the bearer alone, which includes confirming that fiat was received, cancelling and opening a dispute, so it can have the robot's escrow settled there. That is a loss of funds. RoboSats fixed it on 2026-10-06 with a per-coordinator token and reverted the fix on 2026-10-15; the reference clone (`ff1998c`, 2026-11-06) has no such code, though its `frontend/src/models/AGENTS.md:36-47` and `frontend/src/utils/AGENTS.md:22,184` still describe it. So one robot index is bound to one coordinator, recorded before its first contact leaves and checked in the use case at every request (#28). What exists: `features/p2p` owns a Drift database, `p2p.sqlite` (`lib/src/data/p2p_database.dart`, schema version 5, a snapshot per version in `lib/src/data/schemas/` and the migration steps `make drift-migrations` writes from them, `p2p_database.steps.dart`, both tracked and checked by `test/migrations_test/p2p_database/`, the steps from 1 to 2, from 2 to 3, from 3 to 4 and from 4 to 5 over files holding rows included; the generated `.g.dart` is not tracked, and `make build-runner` and `make drift-migrations` cover the feature), apart from the wallet's database. Schema 2 (#29) rebuilds `garage_key_histories` with drift's `TableMigration` to widen the `CHECK` of its origin, the one `// ignore: experimental_member_use` of the feature (drift marks that class experimental; the wallet's migrations do the same), which the architecture test allows on that line only, and gives `robot_bindings` its provenance with `ADD COLUMN`, so that no binding is copied or dropped; drift writes the version only once a step is done, outside any transaction, so a step runs again over a file it left halfway and must stay idempotent (the column is added only when missing). Schema 3 (#85) adds the table `trades` (« Trade records » below), which drift creates only when it is missing, and touches no binding and no history; schema 4 (#87) adds the terms and the trail of each trade (`trade_terms`, `trade_trails`, « The trade use cases (#87) » below) and their triggers, each created only when missing, and touches no older row; every connection turns SQLite's foreign keys on when it opens (`beforeOpen`, the one statement the database runs itself, pinned by the architecture test). The shell passes its directory, the documents directory (out of cloud backups: `allowBackup="false"` on Android, the whole `Documents` excluded on iOS; Android's device-to-device transfer is not excluded, for the wallet as for this), to `P2pFeature.setup(databaseDirectory:)` together with the Garage Key handle, both or neither, under the same `withGarageKeys` flag: the WorkManager isolate gets neither, so no background task opens or migrates it, and the file is opened at its first query only. It holds no secret and is not encrypted, but tells whoever reads it which coordinators this device used, when, and for how many robots of how many keys. Table `robot_bindings`: per key fingerprint and robot index, whatever the network, since a robot's token does not depend on the network (`GarageKey.deriveToken` takes none, so the same key in both slots gives the same token hash on testnet and mainnet), the coordinator as the federation list gave it then (its network, short alias, onion address of that network, its node keys, its Nostr key), the time, and the provenance of the binding (`generated` or `acknowledged`: the history of the key on the robot's network when it was bound, copied by the statement that binds; `generated` for every binding of schema 1, all made under a key drawn here; it is the history of the robot's own network only, so a key drawn on one network and pasted into the other slot keeps `generated` bindings on the first, although their token is the same on both: phase 2 must read the other network's history too before it takes `generated` for a key that never left the device); a robot whose index is bound on the other network is refused as bound to another coordinator; a row is never changed or deleted, not even when the key is deleted, since a deleted key can come back (the keystore's delete reaches the disk asynchronously on Android) or be pasted back from the user's copy. Table `garage_key_histories`: per network and key fingerprint, `generated` (written by `GenerateGarageKeyUsecase` for the very key the vault drew, which `GarageKeyRepository.create` returns, with no read of the slot in between; the record is tried twice while the drawn key is in hand; when neither write succeeds, the key is stored all the same, its history is unknown so its robots are refused, and the use case says so with `Ok(GarageKeyHistory.unknown)` instead of `drawnHere`, which the Garage tells the user right after the draw (above)) or `imported` (written by `ImportGarageKeyUsecase` before the vault stores the key, never turned back into `generated`, and turning an `acknowledged` key back to `imported`, since a pasted copy may have travelled) or `acknowledged` (written only by `AcknowledgeGarageKeyHistoryUsecase`, on the user's acknowledgement that the key's robots may already live on coordinators, « Robots from elsewhere », which « Select Host » of the robot panel asks: a key without a row or `imported` becomes `acknowledged`, a `generated` one stays so, and a second acknowledgement keeps the first); a key without a row, or imported, has an unknown history, and the robots of an acknowledged one may be bound, each binding recording that provenance so that phase 2 can tell them apart. The fingerprint (`GarageKeyFingerprint`) is HMAC-SHA256 keyed by the token of the key's first robot over `troc/p2p/garage-key-fingerprint/v1`: no coordinator can compute it, it does not give the token back, it is computed from the `GarageKey` objects the repository hands out, without reading the key's bytes, and is the same for the same key on both networks, as its tokens are; it is never printed (`toString` is redacted); every `NetworkRobot` carries it with its network, so a robot is checked against the bindings of its own key, and the history of that key on its own network decides whether an index without a binding may be bound. `FetchRobotUsecase` binds before both kinds of request, since both send the token hash: one atomic `INSERT … SELECT …` from the key's history `WHERE` it is `generated` or `acknowledged` on the robot's network, copying it as the binding's provenance, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, synced to disk (`synchronous = FULL`) on a connection that caches no prepared statement (`P2pDatabase.open`, #128: a cached write that met `SQLITE_BUSY` stays active, and the connection's later writes, reported written, stay uncommitted and are then lost), then a read of the binding; only then does the request leave, and a binding is never undone, whatever the request gives (a 7004 « nickname taken » leaves the index bound to that coordinator). A refusal is a `P2pFailure` and nothing is sent: `RobotBoundToAnotherCoordinatorFailure`, `RobotCoordinatorChangedFailure` (the same alias or onion address, but anything else recorded changed: a federation change to assess first, as step 4 of the refresh below says), `RobotHistoryUnknownFailure` (no binding, key neither `generated` nor `acknowledged` on the robot's network: nothing written), `P2pStorageFailure` (an `Exception` of the database or of the directory callback); an `Error` there is a bug (rule 11) and is not caught: the call throws, and nothing is sent either. `features/p2p` makes `avoid_dynamic_calls` an error, which refuses a call on an implicitly dynamic value (not one through an explicit `as dynamic` cast, which the test below reports instead), and `features/p2p/test/robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` resolves the references of every Dart file of `features/p2p/lib` but the translations `gen-l10n` writes (`lib/generated/l10n/p2p_localizations*.dart`; any other file there, the rest of the generated folder and the untracked Drift code are included), calls, tear-offs, cascades, prefixes, extensions and implementations alike, with a fixture of such evasions that must each be seen, and reports any access through `dynamic`: only that use case refers to a member of `CoordinatorClient` that takes a robot's identity or token, whatever its name (but the order's requests of the trade use cases of #87 and #90: `fetchOrder` in `trade_reader.dart`, `takeOrder` in the take, `cancelOrder` in the cancel, `submitPayoutInvoice` and `submitPayoutAddress` in the payout), or to `RobotBindingRepository.bind`; no file refers to a robot's credential (`RobotIdentity.token`, `tokenSha256Hex`, `tokenSha256Base91`, `authorizationHeader`, `firstContactAuthorizationHeader`, `RobotToken.reveal`) but `garage_key_fingerprint.dart`, which keys its HMAC with the token, nor to `CoordinatorTransport.send`; no file holds a raw way out (`dio`, a Tor session or `TorHttpClientFactory`, the `HttpClient`, `HttpServer`, `WebSocket`, `WebSocketTransformer` and `HttpOverrides` that `dart:io` re-exports from `dart:_http`, the sockets and `Process` of `dart:io`, and `http`, `socks5_proxy`, `web_socket_channel` and `dart:html`, which the feature does not depend on) but the transport, the locator only handing it the factory; only the draw refers to `GarageKeyRepository.create` and `recordGeneratedKey`, only the import to `import` and `recordImportedKey`, only the acknowledgement to `recordAcknowledgedKey`, only the repository to the vault's `create` and `import`, and the delete use case to no binding type; from the syntax tree, only the data layer and the composition import the database, and nothing in it or in its migration steps changes or deletes a binding: the only upserts (drift's `DoUpdate`, no SQL text naming an update) move a key's history to `imported` or `acknowledged`, and the migration rebuilds the histories alone. What it does not see: code outside `features/p2p/lib` (the robot types stay out of the app-facing library so that nothing else holds them), `packages/robosats` itself, native code (`dart:ffi`, a platform channel), and anything an allowed file hands out (a credential as a plain `String`, a tear-off of a robot request, a client), which another file could then use: a change to an allowed file (`fetch_robot_usecase.dart`, `garage_key_fingerprint.dart`, `tor_coordinator_transport.dart`, `p2p_locator.dart`, and `isolate_robot_key_compute.dart`, which hands a robot's identity, token included, to an isolate, and brings a trade's unlocked key back from one, #90) needs the same review as these rules. Residual risk: a `p2p.sqlite` put back to an older copy of itself forgets the bindings made since, and their robots look free again; that needs the key and an older file together, which a stock device does not give: the vault's entry stays on the device (iOS `first_unlock_this_device`, Android values encrypted by a keystore key that does not leave it) and the file is out of cloud backups, so a restored or transferred copy of the file comes without a usable key. Known limit, to settle before any order: a coordinator that rotates its node key or Nostr key locks the robots bound to it for good, since a row is never updated, while only its onion address decides who receives the token hash; a reviewed way to accept such a rotation is needed. Where a robot stands is read with no request: `standingOf` gives, in one statement, the binding of its index, made on either network, with its provenance, or none, and the history of its key on the robot's network, and `ReadRobotStandingUsecase` hands it out as a `RobotStanding` (the network, the coordinator as recorded, the provenance and the history, never a token, a key or a fingerprint), which says whether the user must acknowledge the key's history first and whether the robot is bound on the other network; the PIN rule of a delete reads `exposureOf`, as before. The robot panel of the Garage reads where a robot stands and is the only screen that sends one to a coordinator (« The robot panel of the Garage » above). What does not exist yet: asking the user which coordinator a robot lives on, or Nostr index recovery, is not built; the current index is not stored, and the robot's PGP key pair is not stored by design: it is read back from the coordinator and proven by the token (« A robot on a coordinator » above). Never send a robot to the whole federation as the official client does.
- **Money in the P2P tab (#83): no approval gate, by the owner's decision of 2026-11-13.** The P2P tab may take orders and pay on mainnet in the builds the owner installs; it stays behind superuser and developer mode like the rest of the tab, and every real trial is the owner's, on his phone, with amounts he chooses.
  The constraint is on contributors and agents: they never take or cancel an order, never request or pay an invoice, never call a money member against a real coordinator, and stay on testnet for every authenticated call; automated tests do the same. The architecture test below checks `integration_test/` only; unit and widget tests use the fake transport, and no tool script or test under `packages/robosats` calls a money member against a real coordinator either.
  The money members of `CoordinatorClient` (`makeOrder`, `pauseOrder`, `takeOrder`, `cancelOrder`, `submitPayoutInvoice`, `submitPayoutAddress`, `confirmFiat`, `releaseEscrow`, `undoConfirmFiat`, `openDispute`; the first two since #175, phase 3) are named only in the trade use cases, the files `lib/src/domain/usecases/trade/<name>_usecase.dart`, so that every call that locks, moves or releases funds sits in one reviewed place; a money member added to `packages/robosats` under another name, a reward claim (`POST /api/reward/`) included, joins that list in the pull request that adds it.
  `features/p2p/test/trade_money_calls_architecture_test.dart` pins that list as written and that rule (reading every Dart file of `features/p2p/lib` as parsed text, a tear-off, a cascade, an import prefix, an implicit `this` and a `Symbol` counting as uses), and that the integration tests name no money member and give the live robot test no network value but `RobosatsNetwork.testnet`.
  It cannot see what a trade use case hands out, code outside `features/p2p/lib`, nor money moved by anything but these members (a payable invoice shown from `fetchOrder`, and the payment of `P2pWalletPort.payInvoice` behind « Pay with this wallet », which is not a coordinator member and is reviewed as money code, « The wallet's port » below): those are for the money review of the design note's § 4.6.
  What protects the user instead of a gate, as phase 2 is designed: the take confirmation states the bond at stake and says « Real bitcoin » on mainnet, the checks of amounts, network and invoices before any QR or payment (design note § 3.2, § 4.4), the confirmation in the trade screen before « Pay with this wallet » pays from the wallet (the amount, the quoted fees and what stays in the wallet; whether the wallet's PIN is asked too is an open decision for the owner, design note § 7), and, before « Confirm {{amount}} {{currencyCode}} received » (#96), the reference's dialog alone, the tap on its « Confirm » being the only one, as for « fiat sent » and « Pay with this wallet » (whether the wallet's PIN is asked there too is an open question for the owner, design note § 7, decision 7).
- **The wallet's port (#100): « Pay with this wallet ».** The trade screen is to pay a bond or an escrow from the wallet's Instant Payments wallet (its default Liquid wallet, which pays Lightning through the wallet's swap service) without leaving the screen. The P2P feature declares what it needs as a port, `P2pWalletPort` (`features/p2p/lib/public/p2p_wallet_port.dart`, exported by `p2p.dart`), with no wallet type: `instantBalance(network)` (a stream: the balance at once, then after each sync the app finishes; it ends when the wallet's syncs end, or with `Unavailable` if a sync shows the followed wallet is no longer the default Liquid one; a wallet deleted and replaced is not seen until the stream is listened to again) and `quote(invoice)` (the swap service's quote: payin, service fee as an amount, the wallet's estimate of the Liquid network fee, total, what stays, when quoted; the service gives no expiry, and the fee rate it lists is not carried), each returning a sealed `P2pWalletRefusal` without text (a minimum, a maximum, a balance or a wait carried as bounded numbers only); and the payment, `payInvoice`, `watchPayment` and `findPayment` (below; distinct names, since the architecture test finds a member by its name and `watch` or `find` would match unrelated code).
  The port takes only a `CheckedBolt11Invoice` (`lib/src/domain/checked_bolt11_invoice.dart`): the exact BOLT11 string the state machine decoded and checked for the current status and step, within the amount the user accepted, with its amount, network, payment hash, expiry and `payableUntil` (the earlier of the expiry and the coordinator's deadline for the step). The value checks again by itself the bounds (2,000 visible ASCII characters, one case), the human-readable part (`lnbc` on mainnet, `lntb` on testnet, signet's `lntbs`, regtest's `lnbcrt` and anything else refused, #113; an amount with no leading zero, a whole number of satoshis above zero and at most 21 million bitcoin, equal to the amount given), bech32 characters after the separator, a payment hash of 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters, `payableUntil` not after the expiry, and that it is still payable when built; not the checksum, nor the hash and expiry against the string, which the decoder of #86 checks; nor the signature and the payee, which that decoder does not check and only the wallet's own decoder checks, at payment time (« The wallet's BOLT11 decoder » below). One grammar for the human-readable part, stated in three places that cannot import each other (this type, `Bolt11Network.belongsTo` of the state machine, and the payout's shape check of `packages/robosats`, #111): a change to one is a change to the three. Its factory is `@internal`, and only `trade/wallet_payment.dart` (its one source, `walletPaymentFor`), its own file and the testing seam's helper may call it; tests use `checkedBolt11InvoiceForTesting` of `package:p2p/testing.dart`, with `FakeP2pWalletPort`.
  `P2pFeature.setup` takes the port as `wallet:`, an optional function (left out or `null`, no port is lent, so the compositions written before it, such as the fixtures of the custody gate's tests, still build): the shell passes `() => locator<P2pWalletFacade>()` from the foreground composition only, under the flag of the Garage Key handle, and `null` in the WorkManager isolate; the feature works without it, and only `PayBondFromWalletUsecase` calls it (the bond's panel, #100 W4, below in « The bond paid from this wallet (#100) », and the escrow's, #97, below in « The escrow paid from this wallet (#97) »).
  Only the trade use cases (`lib/src/domain/usecases/trade/<name>_usecase.dart`) may name a member of the port, and only they and the feature's own wiring its type: `features/p2p/test/wallet_port_architecture_test.dart`, which also refuses any import of the app (`bb_mobile`) or of the wallet's core (`bull_sdk`) in the feature, and pins that no file names `payInvoice`, `watchPayment` or `findPayment` until W4.
  The implementation is `lib/features/p2p_wallet`, a Troc feature of the app (rule 7: the module that may depend on both sides), so that nothing is added to `lib/core` and the wallet's send feature is unchanged: `P2pWalletFacade` implements the balance and the quote over `SwapFacade.getQuote` and the wallet readers that Pay, Sell and Autoswap use (`GetWalletsUsecase` without sync, `WatchFinishedWalletSyncsUsecase`, `GetAddressAtIndexUsecase` and `LiquidWalletRepository.buildPset` and `getPsetSizeAndAbsoluteFees` for the fee of an unsigned transaction, built from the wallet's public descriptor, to the wallet's own address at index 0: nothing is signed, that address is sent nowhere, and no address index advances). It asks the service only when called and only once the invoice is unexpired, of the wallet's network, and covered by the balance.
