  What a quote tells the swap service, while P2P otherwise talks over Tor (its other requests outside Tor: the wallet's price, below, and the map tiles of #246): one HTTPS POST over clearnet, through the wallet's own client, to the wallet's swap service (its staging host on testnet), a JSON-RPC `getBestSwapOption` with the invoice's amount, `isInAmountFixed: false` and the two networks, never the invoice nor an address; no API key, the default Dart user agent, the user's IP address, and a JSON-RPC id `mobile-<microsecond timestamp>-<counter>` whose counter is shared, for the life of the app, with the wallet's own polling of its swap orders, so the service can tie a quote to the user's other swap orders. A bond's amount follows from an order of the public book, so a quote just before a take lets the service's operator guess which order this address is about to take. Side effects: the quote waits behind the wallet's own calls to the service, one at a time, and an answer HTTP 418 sets the wallet's « app update required » flag, which stops its swaps. Whether P2P quotes get a client of their own, or Tor, is an open question for the owner.
  The feature logs nothing. On this path the wallet's own code (upstream) logs the quote's environment, route and amount at `info` (debug console only), a failure's type at `warning`, the built transaction's fee at `info` and with a `debugPrint` of its own (`LwkWalletDatasource.decodeAbsoluteFeesFromPset`), and a balance read deletes lwk's cache of the wallet when lwk answers `UpdateOnDifferentStatus` (`LwkWalletDatasource.getBalance`), as for any read of the wallet.
  Outside its one pay use case file it may not create an order, sign, store a signed payin, broadcast, sync or reveal a new address: `features/p2p/test/wallet_shell_architecture_test.dart` (in `features/p2p`, whose tests have the analyzer) holds an allow-list, pinned literally, of the imports the shell's feature may have from outside itself, conditional alternatives included, and of every member, constructor and type its code may name, each of them used. The allow-list counts the labels of named arguments too (the feature passes no `sync:`, so none is allowed), the fields a pattern reads (`case LiquidWalletRepository(signPset: final sign)` tears a method off), and an `extension`, `extension type`, `mixin`, `extends` or `with`, through which a member could be reached with an implicit `this`; it still cannot see a top-level function of an allowed import torn off under an allowed local name, nor a constructor torn off with type arguments. The payment adds per-file lists: the names that commit funds (`createOrder`, `signPset`, `savePreparedPayin`, `markBroadcastUnknown`, `markPayinBroadcast`, `BroadcastLiquidTransactionUsecase`, and `GetReceiveAddressUsecase`, whose address goes to the service as the refund destination) and the two imports they need are allowed in `domain/usecases/pay_checked_invoice_usecase.dart` only, and a test pins that no other file names any of them (the reviewers' evasion files are fixtures, one reaching `signPset` from another file through the locator, a pattern, a cascade and a symbol); the reads of a payment (`getOrders`, `watchOrder` and what they read of a record) are allowed in `data/wallet_payment_repository_impl.dart` only, and the wallet's BOLT11 decoder (`DecodedInvoice.fromString`, with its import of `package:bull_sdk/boltz.dart`) in `data/wallet_invoice_repository_impl.dart` only; since the security review of #116, the two use cases that quote and pay (`QuoteInvoicePaymentUsecase`, `PayCheckedInvoiceUsecase`) are named only by the facade and the feature's locator, the locator alone names `GetIt`, resolves anything and imports `get_it`, and the record of quotes is named only where quotes are given and paid (the review's file that quoted and paid through the locator is a fixture that must fail); the shared list gained no name of the wallet's code that the quote did not already use, only the port's new types, the feature's own classes and some of Dart's. What it still cannot see: a top-level function of an allowed import torn off under an allowed local name, a constructor torn off with type arguments, a class of the feature holding a function-typed field named `execute` and calling it as `execute(invoice, accepted: quote)` (harmless alone: filling that field with the pay use case's tear-off needs that use case's type or the locator, both per-file, and the class's own name is not listed), what an allowed file hands out (the facade hands the port to `P2pFeature.setup`), and code outside the feature (the app's `lib/locator.dart` names the facade, pinned below). The test also pins that `lib/locator.dart` names `P2pWalletFacade` once, in the `wallet:` argument of `P2pFeature.setup`, and that only the feature's locator constructs it. The BOLT11 decoder's adapter of #86 is not part of this feature: it gets a shell folder of its own (`lib/features/p2p_bolt11/`), so this list never names the decoder package.
  The payment (#100, step W3; reviewed as money code and key material, since it triggers signing): `payInvoice(invoice, accepted: quote)` returns once the funding transaction is broadcast, or a refusal; every refusal means nothing left the wallet except `P2pWalletBroadcastUnknown`, the only one whose `fundsMayHaveMoved` is true, which carries the payment to follow. **Never twice, but a retry after an attempt that moved nothing** (owner's decision, review of #116): an invoice is paid again, after a new quote and a new confirmation, only while every earlier order of the wallet for it (its text in either case, any wallet and purpose, or this feature's order of the same payment hash) is provably unspent, `WalletPaymentRepositoryImpl.isProvablyUnspent`: no signed payin, no transaction id, no payin the service reports (a payin status other than empty, « not started », « awaiting payment » or « pending », its manual review included, or a transaction id on its order), and a status in which nothing was signed (`creating`, `creationUnknown`, `awaitingUserConfirmation`, `preparingPayin` for this feature's orders, which nothing resumes; `expired`, `failed` for any). As soon as one may have moved funds (signed, « broadcast unknown », broadcast, paid out, completed, refunded, or an open order of the wallet's own screens), `P2pWalletPaymentExists` refuses it, with where the latest such order stands (its progress) and its handle when it is this feature's; an order is tied to the invoice by its text, or, for this feature's orders, by the payment hash its invoice yields (read leniently: an invoice without an amount or for a fraction of a satoshi, which the Send screen pays, yields its hash); an order whose invoice yields no hash at all is tied by its text only and blocks nothing else: an unspent one paid nothing, and this feature reads every invoice with the same decoder before it creates its order, so an order it made always yields its hash (such an order can only be another screen's that carries the feature's note, or follow a change of the decoder). So no single order can switch the payment off for other invoices (the second security pass's S3). The reason: the swap service is paid by a Liquid transaction of this wallet, and a second one while the first may exist would send the amount twice, its return depending on the service's refund. Nothing retries by itself. `PayCheckedInvoiceUsecase` runs the payments one after the other and, for each, checks first everything that can be known before the order: (1) the quote must be one this wallet gave (`IssuedQuotes`, by identity, since `P2pWalletQuote` has a public constructor) for the same Instant Payments wallet, unused (consumed by the first call that presents it, whatever its outcome), at most 2 minutes old and of the same invoice (`P2pWalletQuoteStale`); the invoice must stay payable for `invoiceMargin`, 60 s (the same guess as `TradeTolerances.walletPaymentMargin`, one Liquid block and the calls before it, to tune after the first trial); the wallet must be the default Liquid wallet of the invoice's network signing on this device; the wallet's own BOLT11 decoder (below) must read the same payment hash, amount and network and an expiry the margin away; (2) the earlier orders, as above; (3) the balance must cover the total accepted, and the quote's estimate is made again (an unsigned transaction of the payin accepted to the wallet's own address): coins to consolidate, too few coins, or a fee above the one quoted stop it before anything reaches the service; then, unless the wallet's « app update required » flag is up, (4) the order is created with `isInAmountFixed: false`, the invoice as destination, the Instant Payments wallet's last revealed address as the refund destination (as the Send feature takes it), the payin accepted as the quoted counterpart and the fixed note `RoboSats · Bull Market P2P` (the label the wallet puts on the transaction in its history once the order completes, and how this feature knows its own orders: a text a user does not type by chance as a send's label; the note is the only field set at the creation that the Send screen leaves to the user and every other field is a Send-screen order's own, a destination wallet excepted, which would make the history show the payment as a transfer; a Send-screen order labelled with that exact text would be taken for this feature's and could be paid on, safely: one order holds one signed payin, so the Send screen's own attempt on it then fails), retried once after a rate limit of at most 30 s that leaves the margin. The wallet's store answers an earlier active order of the same destination, amount, wallet and purpose instead of a new one while its deadline has not passed (`OrderSwapRepositoryImpl.createOrder`; past it, it expires that order and creates one; an order still `creating` is an unknown outcome): the order answered must be this payment's, new or an earlier unspent one of this feature (note, wallet, purpose, environment, destination and payout address equal to the invoice, payout amount, `isInAmountFixed`, quoted payin equal to the one accepted for a new order, nothing signed, awaiting its funding), else nothing is built on it (an order of another feature answered by that dedupe included); its own deadline must be `invoiceMargin` away at least, when it is answered and again before the signature (`P2pWalletOrderExpiresTooSoon`: nothing sent, and a new confirmation after that deadline gets a new order); (5) the cap: the order's payin at most the payin accepted, the app's own rule, much tighter than the wallet's tolerance of max(1 %, 1,000 sats) (`P2pWalletQuoteChanged`, the order left unspent at the service); (6) the PSET is built by the wallet, at 25 sat/kwu, to the order's payin address; the wallet's net outflow of L-BTC, fee excluded, must equal the payin (fail closed: `LiquidWalletRepository.getAmountSentToAddress` reads the wallet's net L-BTC change and ignores its address argument, so the destination is the one the wallet's builder was given, not one this check reads; checking the destination output and that only L-BTC moves needs a read of the transaction's outputs the wallet's repository does not offer, a `lib/core` change; the wallet's Send and Autoswap rely on the same), with a fee at most the one quoted, so a total at most the one accepted; the margin is checked again; (7) sign, `savePreparedPayin`, `markBroadcastUnknown`, broadcast the transaction read back from the order (and only if it is the one just signed), `markPayinBroadcast`: the order of the wallet's automatic transfers; a save that fails cleanly leaves the order unspent (`P2pWalletUnavailable`), but a save that does not answer in time or answers another record, and any failure of the mark, is `P2pWalletPaymentInterrupted(payment)`: this call broadcast nothing, yet the record may hold the signed payin or say « broadcast unknown » (a late answer), so the invoice is closed to the wallet and the caller looks with `findPayment` or `watchPayment`; (8) the handle (`P2pWalletPayment`: the swap's local id and the payment hash, nothing else). No resume and no retry inside a call: an order left signed or « broadcast unknown » by a crash is reported as such by `findPayment` and `watchPayment` (`P2pWalletPaymentSigned`, `P2pWalletPaymentBroadcastUnknown`), and nothing finishes it. Each step is bounded (`PayTimeouts`: 20 s for a read, 75 s for the creation, 30 s for a build or the signature, 10 s for a write of the record, 60 s for the broadcast): a step that does not answer ends the call (nothing moved before the broadcast, a creation that times out being `P2pWalletServiceUnreachable` and a save or mark `P2pWalletPaymentInterrupted`; `P2pWalletBroadcastUnknown` for the broadcast; the last mark changes nothing), the queue moves on, and the step's late answer is not used. Foreground only: the WorkManager isolate registers the classes lazily and never resolves them.
  **Trust.** The swap is custodial, as for the wallet's own Lightning sends: the device holds no HTLC and no refund key; once the funding is broadcast, the swap service holds the payin and may pay the invoice, refund the wallet's address, or keep it. The cap bounds what it can hold: the payin accepted.
  The wallet's BOLT11 decoder (`data/wallet_invoice_repository_impl.dart`): Boltz's `DecodedInvoice.fromString` of the wallet's SDK (`package:bull_sdk/boltz.dart`), the call `PaymentRequest.parse` makes for a BOLT11, made directly, without a Boltz URL, without the other parsers `PaymentRequest.parse` tries after it and without their logging (a failing decode reaches no log). What it verifies that the trade's decoder (`bolt11_decoder`, which checks no signature) does not: the Dart binding is generated and documents nothing; the Rust library behind it parses with the `lightning-invoice` crate, whose invoice parsing checks the bech32 checksum and that the signature is valid, recovering the payee's key. Those Rust sources are not in the build's package cache: this is the crate's documented behaviour, not read in this repository. So an invoice with a bad signature, which passes the trade's checks, is refused here (`P2pWalletInvoiceUnreadable`), after the user's tap and before any order. The binding reads an expired invoice too (it returns `isExpired`), so a payment's invoice reads again after it expires.
  `watchPayment` maps the wallet's record (`SwapFacade.watchOrder`, refreshed by the wallet's own watcher while the app is open; it asks the service nothing) to a sealed `P2pWalletPaymentProgress`: preparing (nothing moved), signed (saved, not sent), broadcast unknown, sent, pending (the service received the funds and pays the invoice; a hold invoice stays pending as long as the coordinator holds it), under review (the service's manual review), paid, refund pending (failed after a recorded broadcast), refunded, and expired or failed with what the wallet knows of the funds (`P2pWalletFunds`: not sent, maybe sent when a signed payin has no recorded broadcast, sent); `isFinal` is true when nothing more is to come for the user (paid, refunded, or ended with nothing sent), false for an expired or failed payment whose funds may have left (a refund may be due, and the wallet stops following such an order). `findPayment(hash)` gives the latest of this feature's orders whose invoice has that hash, in any state, with its progress, from the records only, so that a restart never pays twice; it fails closed (`P2pWalletUnavailable`, never « none ») when the records, or the invoice of any order of this feature, cannot be read, and it does not give an order of the invoice made by the wallet's own screens (`payInvoice` still refuses that invoice while such an order is open). A hash read once is kept for the run of the app (an order's destination never changes), so each call decodes only the orders it has not decoded before.
  What a payment tells the swap service, over the wallet's clearnet client as the quote: a JSON-RPC `createOrderSwap` carrying the invoice itself (so its payee, the coordinator's node, its amount, payment hash, expiry and description, which names the order unless the robot uses stealth invoices), the invoice's amount as the amount to pay out with `isInAmountFixed: false`, the two networks, an address of the Instant Payments wallet as the refund destination, and a request id `mobile-<random hex>`; the payin the user accepted stays on the device. Then the service polls the order as for any send, and the funding transaction reaches the Liquid network through the wallet's Electrum servers. #109 tracks the privacy of these calls. On this path the wallet's code logs, besides what the quote logs: the order's creation with its request id, route and purpose at `info`, its success with the order's number and the start of its id at `info`, a failure's type and API code at `warning` (`OrderSwapRepositoryImpl`), and the built transaction's fee at `info` and with a `debugPrint`. The feature itself logs nothing, and the invoice is passed only to the decoder and as the order's destination.
  The buyer's payout (#90; its buttons in the payout panel below): `newLightningInvoice(network, amount, payout:)` gives a `P2pWalletInvoice` (the BOLT11 text, its `amount`, `expiresAt`, and `received`, what arrives in Instant payments, the swap service's `fee` taken out of the amount) and `newReceiveAddress(network)` a `P2pWalletAddress` of the default Bitcoin wallet (« Secure Bitcoin »), each or a refusal (`P2pWalletNoBitcoinWallet` added). The invoice is what the wallet's Receive screen makes (`CreateReceiveOrderSwapUsecase`), mirrored by `ReceiveWalletInvoiceUsecase` through the Swap facade (rule 1 forbids importing `receive`'s use case): the quote with the amount fixed on the Lightning side, the Receive screen's address of the Instant Payments wallet as the destination (`generateNew: true`, which on Liquid is the wallet's next unused address, the same at each call until something pays it: orders of different amounts made before it is paid share it, which lets the service link them), the order created at the service (it moves none of the user's funds), retried once on a short wait, and its invoice read by the wallet's decoder: exactly the amount, the trade's network, at least a minute left, and expiring with the order, no earlier than its deadline (to the second, for parity with the Receive screen) and **no later than one second after it** (the expiry is in whole seconds) (what the service does with a payment that reaches it after its own deadline cannot be read in the wallet's code, and the order has no refund address: such an invoice is never given). `payout` is an opaque key of the payout, unique across trades, coordinators and networks, kept in memory and sent nowhere: the invoice made for that key in this run is given again while its order still waits for its payment (this feature's note, a receive order on the trade's network) and its invoice still passes the checks above for the same amount, rather than a new order, so that taps do not pile up orders. **One invoice, one payout** (security review of #150): the wallet's store answers an earlier active order of the same request (amount and destination) instead of creating one (`OrderSwapRepositoryImpl._createOrderLocked`), and with the repeated address a second payout of the same amount would get the first one's order; so the wallet's orders are read before the creation, and an order answered that was already listed and is not the one made for this key is refused with `P2pWalletAnotherInvoiceWaiting` and never remembered, whoever made it (another payout, the Receive screen, an earlier run, an order a check refused), as is an invoice whose payment hash was given for another key. The payout panel then says to try again once that order ends (at its deadline) or to use an invoice of another wallet. After a restart the keys are forgotten, so an order of the same amount still waiting from before is refused the same way until it ends. A risk left in the wallet's own store, recorded here and not fixed (the changes to the wallet's own code are kept minimal for the upstream syncs): its Receive screen, asked for the same amount while an order of this feature waits, is answered that order by the same lookup and could show the trade's invoice; the fix would be the request's note in the Swap feature's lookup (`getMatchingActiveRequest`). An invoice never paid leaves its order to expire at its deadline, shown as expired in the wallet's history. The address is `GetReceiveAddressUsecase(generateNew: true)` (`ReceiveWalletAddressUsecase`): a new one each time (owner's decision, 2026-11-15), nothing sent. Of the names that commit funds, `createOrder` and `GetReceiveAddressUsecase` are allowed in the invoice use case's file and `GetReceiveAddressUsecase` in the address use case's (`wallet_shell_architecture_test.dart`, `_receiveFundNames`); only `PayoutFromWalletUsecase` of `features/p2p` names the two members (`wallet_port_architecture_test.dart`). What the service learns: the quote and the order over the wallet's clearnet client, the amount, a new Liquid address of the wallet and the user's IP address, nothing of the trade.
  For W4 (the bond's panel): the trade use case that pays writes « payment started for hash H of step S » before it calls `payInvoice` and the swap's id after (its own table, `trade_payments`; an attempt per invoice and confirmation, a retry after an attempt that moved nothing being a new attempt of the same invoice, and a new invoice of the same step a new attempt too), is the single caller of `payInvoice` (pinned by the architecture test), re-reads the order and recomputes the status immediately before it pays, and pays only an invoice `walletPaymentFor` offers from that status, never one a screen kept; on reopen with a hash and no swap id it calls `findPayment`. The panel tells the user each progress value and each refusal: `P2pWalletPaymentExists` says from its progress whether the earlier payment's funds may have left (« a payment of this invoice is on its way » or « a signed payment exists, nothing left this wallet: pay with another wallet »); a refusal that moved nothing after the order (`P2pWalletQuoteChanged`, `P2pWalletOrderExpiresTooSoon`, a failed build or signature) may be retried after a new quote and confirmation, never by itself; `P2pWalletPaymentInterrupted` closes the invoice to the wallet although nothing was broadcast (a save or mark that answered late may leave the record signed or « broadcast unknown », later `Expired(maybeSent)`): the panel says so and offers « Pay with another wallet ». The wallet's order watcher polls every 30 s per order, and only every 5 minutes when its last pass found no pending order (`order_swap_watcher.dart`), so a fresh payment can show `P2pWalletPaymentSent` for longer than a bond's 150 to 200 s: the panel follows the trade through the coordinator's status (the bond seen locked), and asks for a refresh of the payment's progress rather than waiting on it. Every time check uses the device's clock. For the escrow (W6): a seller whose wallet attempt failed after its funds may have left must be told at once to pay with another wallet, since the escrow's timer costs the bond; one that moved nothing may retry or pay with another wallet.
- **Trade records (#85): a trade survives the app being killed, and a robot is never used for two trades.** Schema 3 of `p2p.sqlite` adds the table `trades` (design note `docs/design/phase-2-taking-an-order.md` § 3.5, § 4.5), read and written only by `TradeRecordRepositoryImpl` (`lib/src/data/trade_record_repository_impl.dart`, interface `TradeRecordRepository`, record `TradeRecord` and reservation `TradeReservation` in `lib/src/domain/trade_record.dart`).
  One row per robot that ever had a trade, never deleted: the primary key (key fingerprint, robot index) refuses a second trade of a robot, a foreign key (key fingerprint, robot index, network) to `robot_bindings` a trade of a robot without a binding on the trade's network (its parent key is the unique index `robot_bindings_network`, which adds no rule to the bindings), and CHECKs the values no version writes (a lowercase hexadecimal fingerprint, times from the epoch to the last a `DateTime` holds); network and coordinator are copied from the robot's binding by the statement that writes the row, never given apart from it.
  The row records the network, the robot, the coordinator's alias and onion, the order id, the role (`buyer`/`seller`), the origin (`takenHere`/`recovered`), `taken_at`, and the state as two times set once each: `confirmed_at` (the coordinator showed this robot as the taker) and `closed_at` (a terminal status, or a pending take that did not happen); a pending take has neither, a recovered trade is confirmed when written, and a closed record never changes.
  The database keeps that itself: the triggers of `lib/src/data/trades.drift` (`trades_never_deleted`, `trades_set_once`, `trades_inserted_once`) refuse any delete, any update but setting `confirmed_at` or `closed_at` once, and a second insert of a robot (`INSERT OR REPLACE` included, whose delete fires no trigger while recursive triggers are off; the insert is ignored, so the repository's `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING` keeps its answers), whatever the code; in the code, the only changes are `confirmTake` and `closeTrade`, each an `UPDATE` that sets its time only while it is unset, and nothing in it changes a binding (`robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` pins the statements, the `.drift` file and the steps).
  Payments are not in schema 3: « Pay with this wallet » (#100) added a table of its own in schema 5, `trade_payments`, one row per trade and step (a retry of the same invoice moves its start forward; a new invoice of the same step is refused by the trail's pinned hash, so never paid; the escrow of a seller added its step in schema 7, #97), with its own triggers (« The bond paid from this wallet (#100) » below).
  A record of origin `takenHere` is written only by `recordPendingTake`, which takes the `TradeReservation` that only `reserveForNewTrade` gives, when its statement wrote the robot's binding: a robot the Garage bound, or one a reservation lost, never takes a pending take; the statement still requires the binding, on the reservation's network, under a key drawn here (provenance `generated`), to that very coordinator, and the key still recorded as drawn here only (a key pasted in between is `GarageKeyNotForTradesFailure`).
  The origin is a fact about the trade, not a permission: a robot of a pasted key may finish an order it holds, as a `recovered` trade, but never starts one (#79); `recovered` is written only by `recordRecoveredTrade`, for a robot of any provenance bound to that coordinator, on an explicit take-over by the user of a trade recovery found.
  Only the repository constructs a `TradeRecord` or a `TradeReservation`, and nothing extends or implements either (`test/trade_record_test.dart`); the architecture test allows `recordPendingTake` in `lib/src/domain/usecases/trade/take_order_usecase.dart` only, `recordRecoveredTrade` in `lib/src/domain/usecases/trade/recover_trade_usecase.dart` only, `reserveForNewTrade` in the robot layer only, `confirmTake` and `closeTrade` in the trade use cases only, and the take (#87) is the only caller of `recordPendingTake`, the trade use cases of `confirmTake` and `closeTrade`, and no file calls `recordRecoveredTrade` yet.
  Nothing in the feature opens a database transaction (`transaction(…)`, pinned by the architecture test), and no call of the trade records or of the robot layer may run inside one: a reservation or a record that answered success there would be undone by a rollback after its robot may have left for the coordinator, and an unbound robot could then go to a second one (drift gives no public way to refuse it at run time).
  The robot of a new trade comes from the robot layer (`TradeRobots`: `robotForNewTrade`, `robotOfTrade`, `activeTrades`; § 3.3), implemented until #79 and #81 by `InterimTradeRobots`: the first usable robot (`NetworkGarageKey.firstUsableRobot`) of the Garage Key of the network whose index has no binding on either network and no trade, of a key recorded as drawn here on that network and as nothing else (imported, acknowledged) on any network, bound to the trade's coordinator by `reserveForNewTrade`, one `INSERT … SELECT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING` that reads the key's histories and the robot's record itself and says whether this call wrote, so that two calls at once, on one connection or two, never get the same robot; it returns a `ReservedRobot`, the robot and its reservation.
  The binding is the reservation: a robot bound and never recorded (the app killed in between) stays bound to that coordinator, without a trade, and is never returned again; a robot of a pasted or acknowledged key is never returned for a new trade (`GarageKeyNotForTradesFailure`), but may finish an order it holds once the user takes it over; more than `InterimTradeRobots.maxReservationAttempts` (32) reservations at once on one connection can make the last ones fail with `P2pStorageFailure`.
  The rule for #87: take the robot and its reservation from `robotForNewTrade`, never inside a transaction; make the first contact through `PutRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase`; write the pending take with that very reservation before the take's request leaves, and refuse the take when it fails; confirm the record when the answer or a later read shows the robot as the taker; close a pending take only once a read shows the robot is not the taker, after the time to lock the taker bond, and show a `TradeClosedFailure` from a later confirmation rather than drop it (as #87 settled it after its security review: a pending take whose terms or first trail were never written never sent its request and is closed at once; one never confirmed, on which no pre-take was ever seen, is closed as a take not made at the first read that ends it, the order public again with this robot no participant proving it holds no pre-take, cancelled or expired, and at error 1044 only once the pre-take is certainly gone); derive the network and the coordinator of every later call from the record (`robotOfTrade`, the binding's `admits`); show a `P2pStorageFailure` of the active trades rather than an empty list; and register the repository and the robot layer in the composition.
- **Schema 8 of `p2p.sqlite` (#176): the records of a maker, which no file writes yet.** Design note `docs/design/phase-3-making-an-order.md` § 3.2, § 3.4, § 3.5. One step for all of it, before any maker code, so that the user's records are migrated and reviewed once; nothing reachable writes a maker's record until the make use case (#179), and a taker's trade behaves as before.
  `trades`: the origin `madeHere` beside `takenHere` and `recovered`; `order_id` nullable for a pending make only (`CHECK ((order_id IS NOT NULL AND order_id > 0) OR (order_id IS NULL AND origin = 'madeHere' AND confirmed_at IS NULL))`, a make confirmed when and only when its order id is written, a recovered trade always confirmed). Triggers: `trades_made_pending` (a make is inserted pending: no order id, not closed), `trades_set_once` (as before, but a pending make gains its order id once, in the update that sets `confirmed_at`, and only once its `maker_terms` exist), `trades_one_open_per_order_update` (the rule of one open trade per order for an order id written by an update), the others as before. The repository: `recordPendingMake` (the conditions of `recordPendingTake`, `PendingMake` returned: a make with no order id is never a `TradeRecord`), `confirmMake` (order id and confirmation in one `UPDATE`), `closePendingMake`, `watchPendingMakes`; `watchActiveTrades` leaves pending makes out. The pin of `test/trade_record_test.dart` covers `PendingMake`'s constructor; `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` pins that only the make use case (#179) calls the four writes of a make.
  `maker_terms` (new, `maker_terms.drift`, `MakerTermsRecord`, written and read by `TradeTrailRepositoryImpl.recordMakerTerms` and `makerTermsOf`): what the maker asked, bounded as `MakeOrderRequest.tryCreate` bounds it (a fixed amount or a range of ratio 1.5 to 15 compared on the same doubles, every bound written out), the maker's role rule (a buy order's maker buys), whether there is a password and never the password; written once, never changed nor deleted, only for a pending make (no order id, open) of the same network, coordinator and role. `trade_terms_of_their_trade` refuses a trade made here.
  `trade_trails`: the positions `makerBondStep`, `public`, `paused`, and a maker's current contract (`take`, `take_deadline`, `taker_hash_id`, `taken_amount`; `TradeTrailRecord` fields with defaults, a taker's trail keeping 0 and none). `trade_trails_of_their_trade`: a trail only for a trade whose order id is known, its terms in `trade_terms` (taken here, recovered) or `maker_terms` (made here), first written at contract 0, a taker's with no contract. `trade_trails_forward`: as before, and a contract only forward (`take` moves by one, only for a trade made here, from `public`, `paused`, `waitingForBoth`, `waitingForEscrow` or `waitingForPayout` with a deadline to one of the last three with a later deadline; the contract's columns written from NULL with `take` unchanged, changed only when it moves, never cleared, never written for a trade not made here; the escrow's hash pinned within a contract and, in the write that moves `take`, cleared or replaced by another, never kept). `TradeTrailRecord.whyNotReplace` refuses the same moves of a contract (`TrailReplacementRefusal.contractMoved`), and since #178 lets the write that moves `take` clear or replace the pinned escrow, never keep it. The first trigger refused an escrow kept as it was only in the write that moves `take`, and kept no record of earlier contracts' escrows: #178 closed it, before any release, with the column `earlier_escrows`, in the trigger as in the state machine (`EscrowNotOfThisContract`, below).
  `trade_payments`: `take` in the primary key (0 for a bond and for every payment of a taker, `CHECK (step = 'escrow' OR take = 0)`); `trade_payments_of_their_trade` asks the trail to be at the row's contract, a contract after the first for a trade made here only; `trade_payments_inserted_once` and `trade_payments_forward` compare `take`; `trade_payments_hash_once` refuses a payment hash another row holds, of any trade, step or contract (a trigger, so that a file holding one hash twice still migrates). The repository's members take `take`, 0 by default. Limit, for #178 and #179: the refusal of a hash already paid holds for a taker too (a coordinator serving one invoice to two trades), and reaches the screen through `recordStart`'s `P2pStorageFailure` as `BondPayNotRecorded` (« Bull Market could not write its records on this device. Nothing was paid. »), true but silent on the reason; a new attempt (`recordStart` of a hash already recorded) is an `UPDATE` the contract trigger does not see, so a maker's escrow of contract 0 is accepted again while the trail is at contract 1 (`Ok(true)`): the use case (#187) compares `take` with the trail it has just read; and `confirmMake` makes trades of origin `madeHere` that `watchActiveTrades` lists, which `TradeReader.open` and `ListActiveTradesUsecase` refused before reading anything from #178 until #179, which opens them from their `maker_terms`.
  The step from 7 (`P2pDatabase`, `from7To8`): no transaction and no `TableMigration`, as 6 → 7; the foreign keys read first (`PRAGMA foreign_keys`) and, when on, turned off (`PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF`, outside a transaction: drift opens none around a step; `beforeOpen` turns them on after every step) and read again, the step refused (`P2pSchemaStepRefused`, an `Exception`) only when they stayed on, before anything is written (the SQLite the app ships on Android, `sqlite3_flutter_libs` 0.5.42 with the prebuilt `sqlite3-native-library` 3.52.0, is built without `SQLITE_DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS`, as its compile options read in an APK of this app show, so they are off at a step; it is built with `SQLITE_DEFAULT_RECURSIVE_TRIGGERS`, unlike the tests' SQLite, which changes nothing for an `INSERT OR REPLACE` of a row already there, since the `BEFORE INSERT` trigger that ignores it fires before the conflict); `trades`, then `trade_trails`, then `trade_payments`, each copied aside (`*_of_schema7`) only while it is schema 7's, dropped, made again only while missing, its rows copied back only while missing, before any trigger of the step exists; `maker_terms`; every trigger of each rebuilt table dropped (`DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS`) and all made again together, in the order a new file has them, at every run (SQLite fires the triggers of one event newest first, and the order decides which refusal a write meets: `trades_inserted_once`, which writes nothing, before `trades_one_open_per_order`, which aborts, as a new file has them; a file born before schema 4 had them the other way, which the step repairs), the replaced trigger of `trade_terms` after its own drop; `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` on the whole file, the step refused when any row breaks a foreign key (the version stays 7, the copies kept with every row, at every opening); then the copies dropped. Every statement is a constant of `P2pDatabase`, pinned by value, and the step's calls are pinned in their order, by `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart`. `test/migrations_test/p2p_database/schema8_migration_test.dart` compares every row of a schema 7 file holding every shape of row, column by column with its storage class, stops the step after each of its statements (a drift `QueryInterceptor` beneath the opening) and runs it again to the very file of a step run at once, compares every table's triggers, in their order, with a new file's for a file born at each schema 1 to 7, and opens it with the foreign keys on (turned off, then on again), with them stuck on (refused, nothing written) and with an orphan row; `test/maker_records_test.dart` tries every CHECK and trigger of schema 8 for what it refuses and what it lets through.
  A file of schema 8 is not opened by an older version of the app: drift refuses to go down a version with a `StateError` at the opening, which reaches the older version's repositories as an `Exception` (`DriftRemoteException`, the database running in a background isolate since #60), so its P2P tab says its records are unreadable and writes nothing (security review of #176; not seen on a phone). Going back to an older APK needs no uninstall: every APK of this fork is built without `--build-number` (`makefile`), so they all carry the version code of `pubspec.yaml` and the same signature, and an older one installs over a newer one (functional review of schema 9). It then refuses a file of a newer schema as above: its P2P tab says the records are unreadable and writes nothing, nothing is erased, a trade under way is finished meanwhile with the official app and the Garage Key, and installing the newer APK again opens the file as before. A file left at version 7 in the middle of the step (the app killed during it) and opened by an older version is opened as its own, with tables already rebuilt whose triggers may not be made again yet; the same held for the step 6 → 7.
  Feature-wide rules of `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` since its security review: no file but `P2pDatabase` holds a pragma or names `customStatement`, and none but it and drift's code holds a `CREATE TABLE`, `TRIGGER`, `INDEX` or `VIEW`; the exemptions for the step's copies of the rebuilt tables are their exact texts, in `p2p_database.dart` only, and the schema 7 texts of the replaced triggers are exempted in the generated steps only. The free texts of `maker_terms` (`payment_method`, `description`, and `coordinator_alias`) hold no NUL (`instr(CAST(x AS BLOB), X'00') = 0`, the file being UTF-8), so that `length`, which counts characters up to a NUL, counts their code points. What these rules do not reach, written after the security review: a rule on SQL text sees literals, so a pragma cut into pieces and run by `customSelect` in a repository, or a statement run by `executor.runCustom`, escapes it, and the review stays the barrier (closing it would mean pinning every query of the repositories on constants); a raw `SqliteException` shows the statement's parameters (a key fingerprint, a description), so an error of the database is never logged nor shown raw; and the hexadecimal columns (`payment_hash`, `taker_hash_id`, `key_fingerprint`) accept a text followed by a NUL in the file (their `length` stops at it), which only the decoding on the Dart side refuses.
- **Schema 9 of `p2p.sqlite` (#204, #231, #233): what each payment took out of the wallet, where each contract's payout came from, and a trace of terms shown otherwise.** Design note `docs/design/phase-3-making-an-order.md` § 3.5 bis. **Schema 8 is frozen**: it is in the APKs given to the owner, with real trades in them; `drift_schema_v8.json` and the generated `schema_v8.dart` are pinned by their bytes (`schema9_migration_test.dart`), and no statement of the steps up to 8 changes. The step from 8 (`from8To9`) rebuilds no table and replaces no trigger: four columns added in place with drift's `addColumn`, each with its constraints as the column's own and each only while missing (`trade_payments.confirmed_sats`, `trade_trails.terms_changed_at`, `terms_changed_premium_low` and `terms_changed_premium_high`), the new table `trade_payouts` (made only while missing), its four triggers dropped (`DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS`), the marks of the buyers' contracts written (`_markPayoutsUnrecorded`: every buyer's trail at those positions, closed trades included; each only while missing), its triggers made again in a new file's order, then three new triggers (`trade_trails_terms_trace`, `trade_payments_sats_known`, `trade_payments_sats_forward`), each the newest of its table, as in a new file; no transaction, no foreign key turned off, no row of a schema 8 table copied, changed or deleted, every statement a pinned constant and every call pinned in its order (`robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart`). The new columns of an older row are `NULL`: « unknown » for an amount or a payout, « no trace recorded » for terms, never zero. `schema9_migration_test.dart` migrates a file of schema 8 holding every shape of row (trades taken, made, recovered, pending, closed; a maker's next contract with its earlier escrow; payments of contracts 0 and 1), compares every row column by column with its storage class, stops the step after each of its 17 statements and runs it again to the very file of a step run at once, and compares the objects with a new file's; `schema8_migration_test.dart` opens its files as an app of schema 8 did (`_atSchema8`) where it tests the step 7 → 8 alone.
  `trade_payments.confirmed_sats` (#233): the total of the quote the user confirmed (`P2pWalletQuote.total`: the invoice, the swap service's fee, the network fee), for the bond and the escrow, written by the same statement as the payment's start (`recordStart(…, sats:)`: the `INSERT` of a first attempt, or the `UPDATE` of a new attempt of the same invoice, which keeps the larger amount and leaves an unknown one unknown), committed before `payInvoice` is called; killed between the two, the row holds the amount and the wallet was asked nothing. `trade_payments_sats_known` refuses a payment recorded without it (so nothing is paid whose amount was not written first), `trade_payments_sats_forward` an amount lowered, cleared or invented for a payment of schema 8. `EarlierEscrow.of` takes an earlier escrow's sats from its record (`TradePaymentRecord.confirmedSats`), so the alert of a next contract gives the exact total after a restart, « at least … sats » only for a payment recorded before schema 9; `EscrowAmounts` is removed.
  `trade_payouts` (#204, `trade_payouts.drift`, `TradePayoutRepositoryImpl`, its only writer, `PayoutTrace` in `lib/src/domain/trade/payout_trace.dart`): one row per trade and contract, `thisWallet` (an invoice or an address this wallet gave during the run that submitted it, `WalletPayoutDestinations`, in memory, noted by `PayoutFromWalletUsecase`; an invoice and a segwit address compared without case), `pasted` (anything else), or `unrecorded` (the step's mark of the current contract of every buyer's trail at a position where its payout may already have been given, closed trades included, where it changes nothing: « unknown », never « given elsewhere »); `invoice` or `address`, the time; never the invoice, its payment hash, nor the address (security review of schema 9, R4). `SubmitPayoutUsecase` writes it for the contract the payout was signed for, right after the coordinator's answer took it (`PayoutGiven`) or may have (`PayoutOutcomeUnknown`), before its read after, never for a refusal; a write that fails is a `recordProblem`. Triggers: never deleted, trade and contract never changed, never turned into `unrecorded` (which the app never inserts), time never back, only for an open trade of a buyer whose trail stands at the row's contract, a second insert ignored. `TradeReader` reads the trace of the trail's contract into `TradeSeen.payout` for a buyer, at the opening and after each answer kept; the trade screen says it before « Confirm … sent » and in its dialog (`payoutTraceOf`): nothing from this wallet, `p2pPayoutTracePasted` / `p2pPayoutTracePastedAddress`, `p2pPayoutTraceNotGivenHere`, `p2pPayoutTraceUnknown` (a mark, or records that cannot be read), none of them beside the red `p2pPayoutRateNotSeen` of a contract seen late (an alert said alone); a trace not read is said as unknown, never silence (R6); for a taker buyer too, concerned the same way (a payout given from another client with the same Garage Key, a trade taken over after recovery). Nothing is blocked. Only `SubmitPayoutUsecase` calls `recordPayout` and only `TradeReader` `traceOf` (pinned).
  The trace of terms shown otherwise (#231): the first read kept with `TradeTermsChanged` (every term it compares, a taker's and a maker's) sets `terms_changed_at` to its read time, kept to the end, and `terms_changed_premium_low` / `_high` keep the lowest and the highest premium reads kept showed otherwise than the trade's own (its terms kept here; an order not priced in sats, −100 to 999), each written as soon as one differs, even after another term changed first; the lowest only goes down, the highest only up, neither cleared, low ≤ high, both only with the time (security review of schema 9, C1, and its verification, R8: never less than was seen, on both sides, the side that costs the user included); in the same write as the trail (`TradeTrail.termsChangedAt`, `termsChangedPremiumLow`, `termsChangedPremiumHigh`, set in the two places that build the trail of an order read, `_TermsChange.of`). `trade_trails_terms_trace` (each column compared with its own old value, no subquery), `TradeTrailRecord.whyNotReplace` and `TradeTrail.restore` keep it; `schema9_migration_test.dart` tests the trigger on a migrated file and on a new one. Not a `TradeWarning` (it forbids no payment once the terms are back, as before): the trade screen says it in one alert line under the warnings until the trade ends, unless the status carries `TradeTermsChanged` itself (`termsShownOtherwise`, from the first change, with the trade's own premium from its terms kept here, `TradeSeen.ownPremium`, never the coordinator's read: `p2pTermsShownAbove` up to the highest when all were above, `p2pTermsShownBelow` down to the lowest when all were below, `p2pTermsShownBoth` both; `p2pTermsShownPremiums` / `p2pTermsShownPremiumAlone` without the own premium, `p2pTermsShownOther` for another term only, each with the day and time; never once the trade ended), and in the dialog of « Confirm … sent ». Not covered: the value of another term than the premium, the time of a premium changed after another term (the first change's is said), checks that are no terms, a change put back before the step.
  A file left at version 8 in the middle of the step (the app killed during it) and opened by an APK of schema 8 is opened as its own; the triggers already made then hold for it, so a first payment from the wallet (a new attempt of an invoice already recorded being an `UPDATE`) is refused there when `trade_payments_sats_known` exists (records not written, nothing paid), and an APK of schema 9 completes the step at its next opening.
  An amount read back out of 1 to 2.1 × 10^15 (a file changed outside the app) is records unreadable, never a figure (R2).
  Tests: `schema9_migration_test.dart`, `payout_trace_test.dart`, `terms_trace_test.dart`, the amounts in `maker_records_test.dart` and `maker_escrow_from_wallet_test.dart`, and `schema9_relaunch_test.dart`, by gestures on the app's composition with the app started again (a new composition over the same file, `setUpP2pForTesting(databaseOpener:)`).
- **A maker's terms (#177): what a maker asks, and what this wallet would pay for it, which nothing builds yet.** Design note `docs/design/phase-3-making-an-order.md` § 3.2, § 3.3. `features/p2p/lib/src/domain/trade/maker_terms.dart`: `MakerTerms.create(network:, coordinator:, request:, createdPrice:, makerFee:)` holds the `MakeOrderRequest` itself (so the terms and what is sent have the very bounds of `tryCreate`, and the conversion is the identity), the coordinator's price of `/api/limits/` read just before the make (from `MakerTerms.minCreatedPrice`, 0.0001, the smallest the reference's `exchange_rate` holds, `DecimalField(decimal_places=4)`, `api/models/currency.py:17-23`, to below `MakerTerms.maxCreatedPrice`, 10^15; within them every figure for display is finite) and the maker fee of `/api/info/` (refused with `MakerFeeTooHigh` above `TradeTolerances.makerFeeCeiling`, 1 %; `MakerTermsUnusable` for a fee or price that is no figure, or a coordinator not on the network); its record is `MakerTermsRecord` (since #189 `has_password` and the meeting point as asked, never the password nor its hash; `has_password` false and no coordinates before), restored with the same checks, `TradeCoordinatorGone` for a coordinator that left the list. The role is `tradeRoleMaking` (a buy order's maker buys). Its money rules are exact (`ExactRatio`, `exact_ratio.dart`: rationals of `BigInt`, a double read as the decimal its shortest text writes, the one rounding being each ceiling's final `floor`, down), since the same sums in doubles can fall one sat below the rule: `createdSatoshis(amount)` (`amount × 10^8 / (price × (1 + premium / 100))`, `Logics.calc_sats`, `api/logics.py:256-259`); the maker bond expected on the range's maximum (`api/logics.py:1266-1268`), `bondCeiling` (`floor(expected × 1.02 + 1)`, the taker's rule) and `bondAgrees` (within 2 % and 1 sat, both ways, every bond it accepts under the ceiling; a known limit, the taker's too, set by the size of the order: the coordinator's two truncations can take a legitimate bond under the lower mark only when S × (0.02 − x / (1 + x)) < 1, S the order's sats and x the rise of the price before the bond, so below 50 sats of order at the same price, about 738 sats for a rise of 1.9 %, 2,550 for 2 %, the smallest order a recorded coordinator allows being 1,000 sats); `contractAmount(rangeAmount)` (the fixed amount, or a range's amount of the current contract within its bounds); `escrowCeilingFor(rangeAmount)` for a maker seller, removed by #187 with `TradeTolerances.makerEscrowFactor` (a factor F of 2 of the sats at the creation's rate, a proposal the owner closed on 2026-11-17 by a rule of his own, the design note's § 3.3: each payment of an order made here compared with the amount at the Bull wallet's rate at the moment of paying, one threshold of 10 % on the side that costs the user, a red warning with both amounts beyond it, the user free to confirm); `feeAgrees(tradeSatoshis:, paid:)` (the implied fee, escrow less `last_satoshis` for a seller, `last_satoshis` less the payout for a buyer, from 0 to `last_satoshis` × the maker fee + 1 sat, `api/logics.py:650-668`, `:733-749`); `rateGap(rangeAmount:, tradeSatoshis:)` (`TradeRateGap`: the rate the contract's sats imply, the rate of the creation, their gap in percent, doubles for display only; a positive gap is fewer sats, unfavourable to a buyer and favourable to a seller, which a screen says by side). The ceilings are `Sats` (`BigInt`) and never go through `BigInt.toInt`, which stops at 2^63 − 1 on the VM. **Every protection of a maker rests on the price of the creation, which the coordinator gives**: a price k times too low makes the expected bond, both ceilings and the expected sats k times too large, and the gap of a contract whose sats follow the same lie reads 0 % (100 USD at 3 %, 5,000 announced for 50,000: a bond ceiling of 61,201 sats instead of 6,121, an escrow ceiling of 4,001,001 instead of 400,101); a price about twice too high makes this wallet refuse a legitimate escrow. No weaker than the taker or the reference; the independent bounds are the swap quote the user confirms, the balance and the service's limits; the design note's proposal 22 (§ 7) puts the wallet's own rate beside the payments to the owner. The rules for the next issues (#178 to #187) are in the design note's § 6.
  `TradeTerms<S extends TradeSide>` (`trade_terms.dart`): what both sides share stays on it (network, coordinator, order, role, type, currency, premium, bond size, escrow duration), with `expectedBond` and `bondCeiling` by side (this robot's bond: the taker's, formula unchanged, or the maker's); the rest is the sealed `side`: `TakenSide` (amount taken, `isRange`, `explicitSatoshis`, `bookPrice`, `takerFee`, `expectedSatoshis`, `escrowCeiling`) or `MadeSide` (the `MakerTerms` only; a range's amount taken is the contract's, kept in the trail). The take's factories (`take`, `takeRead`, `restore`) give `TradeTerms<TakenSide>`, read through the extension `TakenTradeTerms` (exported by `trade_take.dart`), whose `record` is the taker's `TradeTermsRecord`; `TradeTerms.made(maker, orderId:)` and `restoreMade(record, orderId:)` give `TradeTerms<MadeSide>`; every factory instantiates the type of its side, so `terms is TradeTerms<TakenSide>` holds exactly when the side is a taker's, which the code holding terms of either side tests instead of casting. `TradeStatus.from` follows a maker's trade since #178 (below), and `PayBondFromWalletUsecase` pays a maker's bond at 0 since #180 (below) and a maker seller's escrow at 6 and 7 since #187 (below), nothing else on a maker's terms (`BondPayNotAtBondStep`). `test/maker_terms_test.dart` checks each rule at its bounds with figures computed by hand and against a simulation of the reference's own arithmetic (Python floats are Dart's doubles, its decimals simulated with integers, its `round` to even); `test/maker_terms_bounds_test.dart` tries each bound, at it and one step past it, in `MakeOrderRequest.tryCreate`, `MakerTerms.restore` and the CHECKs of `maker_terms`, which give the same verdict, and pins where the database is wider by design (it counts no decimals, cleans no text, knows no currency, keeps any version from 1, a password, a meeting point and any price above 0; it ties the coordinator to its make's, where the terms ask the federation list): `MakerTerms.restore` refuses those records, and no code writes them.
- **A maker's state machine (#178): how an answer about an order made here becomes what a screen may show or a wallet may pay, which nothing reaches yet.** Design note `docs/design/phase-3-making-an-order.md` § 3.2, § 3.3, § 3.5, § 3.8. `TradeStatus.from` follows terms of a maker (`TradeTerms<MadeSide>`) along a maker's edges with a maker's checks, under the same rules as a taker's (pure, an end never left but refreshed, an answer refused as a whole leaving the last status with the breach recorded, every failed check a typed `TradeWarning`, no check ever an action); a taker's terms take the very path they took before, which `trade_status_test.dart` and a comparison of the same seeded taker sequences on `main` and on the branch show. A maker's trade starts from the first read of its order, `TradeStatus.afterMake(firstRead, terms:, now:, reader:)`, on a fresh trail (`TradeTrail.made` true; the side is not stored, it follows from the terms, and `isOf` compares it), named by no file of `lib/` until the make use case (#179), as `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` pins; that first read can only be the maker bond (0), the order expired at 0 (5) or error 1043 (which proves nothing about the maker). Positions: `makerBondStep` (0, `WaitingForMakerBond` with the maker bond's `CheckedInvoice`, pinned for the order's whole life), `public` and `paused` (1 and 2, `MakerOrderPublic` with `paused`, the deadline of the start and `backInBook` once a contract was kept), then the shared ones; the edges are `TradeEdges.makerSteps` (each single move of the reference for a maker with its roles and lines: back to the book from 6 and 7 for both roles and from 8 for a maker seller only; from 9 on a taker's) and `TradeEdges.allowsMade` its closure, pinned by `maker_status_test.dart` against a list written out apart, with every pair of a position and an answer (an order at each status but 3, which the package refuses, and errors 1043 and 1044) for a maker buyer and a maker seller. Error 1044, which goes only to a robot that is no participant, and 3, 4 or 12 in an order are refused for a maker (`TradeStatusUnexpected`, `TradeStatusRegressed`), so is the bond step after the bond locked; an answer that does not show this robot as the maker on its order's side is `TradeRoleMismatch`. 17 is `DisputeLost` and 18 `DisputeWon` for a maker (the positions keep the coordinator's names: both classes carry their `position`). Back in the book is not an end. Cadence: 3 s at 0, 35 s at 1, 180 s at 2 (`TradeCadence`).
  **The contracts.** The trail keeps a maker's current contract (`take`, `takeDeadline`, `takerHashId`, `takenAmount`; 0 and none for a taker, which `TradeTrail.restore` refuses otherwise): a read at 6, 7 or 8 with no contract kept opens one (`take` unchanged); the same deadline and the same taker is the same contract, within which the steps only move forward (6 to 7 or 8; any step back, which goes through the book, is `TradeStatusUnexpected`); a later deadline is the next contract (`take` + 1, its deadline, taker and amount, the escrow's pinned hash replaced in the same write by the new contract's or cleared), whatever was read before, the taker possibly the same robot, but only from `public`, `paused`, 6, 7 or 8 and where the order can go back to the book (not a maker buyer's 8, not the chat); the same or an earlier deadline with another taker is `TradeTakerChanged` (a breach, refused as an answer); an earlier deadline with the same taker is the same contract with `DeadlineMovedBack`; after a return to the book, a deadline that is not later is refused (`TradeStatusUnexpected`): the book never gives back a contract it ended. From 9 on, the maker may have acted from another client with the same Garage Key (the official app, as the owner's trials do): a read at 9 or later is a **next contract seen late** when the trail stands where the order can have gone back to the book (`public` and `paused`, where any read is one, the book never giving back the contract it ended; 6, 7 and a maker seller's 8, where another taker is one): `take` + 1, the taker and amount read, the contract's deadline kept as the last one read (its own is no longer readable), and `ContractSeenLate`, information and not a breach (« the trade moved on from another app », worded by #182), said at every status while that contract lasts, after a restart too: the trail keeps it (`TradeTrail.seenLate`, the column `seen_late`, set only in the write that opens that contract; security review of #178, which found it said once only). Elsewhere (the chat or later, a maker buyer's 8) another taker is `TradeTakerChanged`; a trail with no contract kept keeps the taker it reads, with no deadline. A known limit: a next contract taken by the very robot of the one before, read from 6, 7 or 8 at 9 or later, is taken for the same contract: for a fixed amount nothing shows and nothing is wrong but `take`; for a range, the other amount is said as `TradeTermsChanged` at each read, and the user sees the amount of the contract before (`Chatting.amount`, which words « Confirm … sent », and the gap is computed with it). The coordinator's clock is taken not to go back (a next contract's deadline is later only because its take is). A range's amount taken belongs to its contract: kept from the contract's first read, constant within the contract (`TradeTermsChanged`), another one at a next contract being that contract's own. A read at 6 or later whose amount is not the order's is refused, never kept (`TradeTermsChanged`, security review of #178): a fixed order's other amount, a range's amount taken missing or outside the range (it would word « Confirm … sent », and one at the package's bound of 10^10 could not be stored); every other value the package lets through is storable (dates of 2020 to 2100, hash ids of 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters, `take` growing by one a read). The trail keeps every escrow hash its earlier contracts pinned (`TradeTrail.earlierEscrows`, the column `earlier_escrows`): the one it holds joins them in the write that moves `take`, and an escrow of any earlier contract served again later, whatever came in between (a contract read at 8, or at 6 or 7 without its escrow), after a restart too, is `EscrowNotOfThisContract`: a breach, never shown, never pinned (security review of #178, which found the first version compared only with the contract just before). A contract opened without its escrow then pins the one shown later, which no earlier contract held. `TradeTrailRecord.whyNotReplace(stored, madeHere:)` (and `mayReplace`) takes the trade's side from the repository (`TradeOrigin.madeHere`), a required parameter so that no caller applies a taker's rule to a maker's trail by forgetting it: it says the trigger's rule exactly, role included (no next contract from a maker buyer's 8; a contract seen late with no escrow, and from 6, 7 or 8 another taker; the earlier escrows grown exactly; the mark of a contract seen late), a maker's edges besides, and no contract at all on a trail not made here. `TradeTrail.restore` refuses a position of the other side (a pre-taker's for a maker, a maker's own for a taker), earlier escrows or the mark at contract 0, and earlier escrows that are not hashes each followed by `;`. `WalletPaymentOffered.take` is the contract of the status an invoice was offered at, under which #187 records a payment: a status never offers an escrow of an earlier contract, so `recordStart` is given the trail's own contract.
  **The checks of a maker.** The order's terms as asked (`TradeTermsChanged`: type through the role, currency, premium, never priced in sats, bond size, escrow duration, payment method text as sent, whether it has a password and its meeting point since #189, the public duration where the read gives it at 5 and 13 to 15, the fixed amount or the range's bounds, and a range's amount taken as above); the maker bond at 0 with `MakerTerms.bondAgrees` (`BondAmountOutsideOrder`, a warning, so never paid from this wallet) and its invoice's amount equal to `bond_satoshis` (`InvoiceAmountMismatch`); the escrow equal to `trade_satoshis` (`EscrowAmountMismatch`), the payout too (`PayoutAmountMismatch`); the implied fee with `MakerTerms.feeAgrees` on `satoshis_now`, the order's `last_satoshis` (`MakerFeeAboveAnnounced`, also when a participant's read at 6 or later gives no `satoshis_now`, which the reference always gives). **No warning compares the trade's sats with a price** (§ 3.3): the gap is information, `rateGap` on the statuses of 6 to 10 (`WaitingForBoth`, `WaitingForEscrow`, `WaitingForPayout`, `Chatting`; `null` for a taker), computed by the state machine alone from the order's `satoshis_now`, which `package:robosats` bounds (`MakerTerms.rateGap` is slow on a huge figure: only `trade_status.dart` calls it, pinned), and said by side with `TradeRateGap.effectFor(role)` (`RateGapEffect`: a positive gap, fewer sats, is unfavourable to a buyer and favourable to a seller, never neutral but at exactly 0). `Cancelled.bondMayHaveBeenLost` is said for a maker after 6, 7 or 8 as for a taker, after `public` and `paused` too (taken, then cancelled alone at 6 or 7 from another client, the bond settled), and at 0 once the bond was shown (it may have been paid, the order taken and cancelled elsewhere, or the bond kept: security review of #178); only at the first read, when no bond existed, is it false. The ordinary cancel of a public order from this app, where the bond is returned, is then said « maybe lost » too: #179 refines it, its cancel use case knowing what it sent. The new warnings are worded by the screens with existing texts until #182 (`trade_l10n.dart`).
  **Not yet**, by the design note's § 6: the make, its resolution and the maker's cancel came with #179 (below: since then `TradeReader.open` and `ListActiveTradesUsecase` open a trade made here from its `maker_terms`, never from a taker's terms, as `made_here_records_test.dart` shows; before, they refused it); the maker bond and the escrow from this wallet (#180, #187); the screens (#181, #182). What a coordinator that lies can still obtain is in the design note's § 3.2 and § 4.1: a faked next contract (a later deadline, which nothing tells from a real return to the book) has a new escrow taken as legitimate, which this wallet pays only after a confirmation under the owner's payment rule of 2026-11-17 (#187, which removed `MakerTerms.escrowCeilingFor`) and, by #187 after the owner's decision of 2026-11-19, after a red alert with the earlier escrows of the order not back yet and the total out, nothing limiting how many are out at once; from another wallet nothing bounds it, as in the reference. `maker_status_test.dart` drives every pair of the table; `maker_contracts_test.dart` the contracts, the checks, the lies, seeded sequences of the reference's own answers read with gaps, the clock moving (`test/support/maker_reference.dart`: never a warning, a contract only forward, every trail storable and restored as it was; and with the maker acting from another client too, never a breach, next contracts seen late said as information), random lies (an end never left, a contract never back, no escrow of an earlier contract offered, every trail storable), and every trail of such sequences, the second client's included, written to the database of schema 8 through the repository, whose triggers take each.
  **Schema 8 amended before any release, by #178, twice.** First (after its functional review), `trade_trails_forward` lets `take` move by one also to a position from the chat on (`chatting` to `takerLostDispute`, not `expired` nor `cancelled`) with the same `take_deadline` and a taker, for a next contract seen late. Then (after its security review), `trade_trails` gains two columns, `earlier_escrows` (text, default `''`: the escrow hashes of earlier contracts, each followed by `;`, `CHECK` on its form) and `seen_late` (a flag, default 0), both empty at contract 0 (`CHECK (take > 0 OR (earlier_escrows = '' AND seen_late = 0))`, so on every first trail and every taker's); and `trade_trails_forward` says the domain's rule exactly: `take` moves from a maker buyer's 8 never, the held escrow joins `earlier_escrows` exactly in the write that moves `take` (`OLD.earlier_escrows || coalesce(OLD.escrow_hash || ';', '')`), neither column changes otherwise, a contract seen late has no escrow, from 6, 7 or 8 another taker, and `seen_late` 1 (0 at a step), and an escrow hash pinned is never one of the earlier ones (`instr` on `;`-delimited hashes). A column of the trail rather than a table of its own, so that each change is one statement, atomic with the trail it belongs to: the feature opens no transaction, and an app killed between two writes could otherwise leave an escrow remembered without its trail, or the reverse. `TradeTrailRecord.whyNotReplace` says the same as the trigger. It was changed in place, not by a schema 9: no APK carried schema 8 (it came into `main` with #195), so a single transformation of the user's records stays; the snapshots and generated schemas 1 to 7 are unchanged, `drift_schema_v8.json`, `p2p_database.steps.dart` and the generated `schema_v8.dart` were made again, the pin of `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` follows, and a file made new and a file migrated from 7 give the same table and triggers, in the same order (`schema8_migration_test.dart`); the step 7 → 8 needs no new statement, since it makes the rebuilt `trade_trails` from the schema it generates and copies a row back by naming its columns, the new ones taking their defaults. A device that opened a file at the first schema 8 (a build of `main` between #195 and #178) keeps that file as it is: its `user_version` is already 8, so no step runs, and its `trade_trails` has neither `earlier_escrows` nor `seen_late`. The trail repository names both in every query of a trail (the insert, the update, the reads of `historyOf` and `watchHistories`), which SQLite then refuses at once (« no such column »): every such query gives `P2pStorageFailure`, so **no trade of that device, a taker's included, can be read or written any more** (nothing is shown wrong and nothing is paid, but the P2P trades are unusable). What is established: the APKs given out so far were built before #195, at schema 7, and no build of `main` after #195 was given to a user (the project's records; the repository holds no `-p2p` release tag). The remedy, should it ever happen: uninstall and install the app again (which erases `p2p.sqlite`; a running trade is then finished with the official app and the Garage Key), or a schema 9 step that adds the two columns when they are missing.
- **The make use case (#179): make an order, never lose it, cancel it as its maker, which no screen calls yet.** Design note `docs/design/phase-3-making-an-order.md` § 3.4, § 3.2, § 3.9. `lib/src/domain/usecases/trade/make_order_usecase.dart`: `MakeOrderUsecase.execute(request, {coordinator, network})`, under `TradeMakeLocks` (one make or resolution at a time per network, a lazy singleton of the composition; keyed by network since a make has no order id), in this order, each step only once the one before it succeeded: the coordinator in the federation list on the network; `/api/info/` (the maker fee) and `/api/limits/` (the price of the currency) read once, at once, anonymously (`fetchInfo`, `fetchLimits`: no robot, no token); `MakerTerms.create` with that price and fee; the amount within the limits of the reference's form (`MakerAmountLimits` of `trade/trade_make.dart`: the coordinator's bounds with the premium, the maximum capped by `CoordinatorSizeLimit` at its price, times 1.03 and 0.98, `MakerForm.tsx:96`, `:140-158`; a range's bounds within 0.99 and 1.01 of them, `AmountRange.tsx:57-77`): a make refused there costs no robot (`MakeRefusal`: `MakeCoordinatorNotOnNetwork`, `MakeCoordinatorUnreadable`, `MakePriceUnknown`, `MakeTermsRefused`, `MakeAmountOutsideLimits`); a fresh robot from `robotForNewTrade`, put on the coordinator (`PutRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase`), refused when the coordinator already knew it or it held an order (active or last), or when its Garage Key changed meanwhile (`MakeRobotRefused`, `MakeRobotNotPut`); `recordPendingMake`, then `recordMakerTerms`, then the terms read back and compared with those built (`MakeNotRecorded` otherwise, the pending make closed); `makeOrder` sent once through the robot's places of `RobotRequestLimiter`, with exactly `MakerTerms.request`, never again. Its answer: a refusal known to come before the order (`OrderActionRefused`), or a transport that never sent it, closes the pending make (`MakeNotMade`); an outcome unknown leaves it pending (`MakePending`, `MakePendingReason.outcomeUnknown`); a `MadeOrder` is read by the robot (`TradeReader.readOrderOf` with ranked times), and only a read that shows this robot as the maker (`TradeReader.showsMaker`: `is_maker` checked by the package against the robot, not its taker too, the coordinator, network and number asked; the order's creation time is the coordinator's word and is not compared with the device's clock, the robot being fresh and the order compared with what was asked by the state machine) confirms it: the terms read back from `maker_terms` (`TradeTerms.restoreMade`, never rebuilt from a read), `confirmMake` with the order id, then `TradeStatus.afterMake` of that read kept through `TradeReader.keep` (`MakeStarted`, only a status marked `stored` showing an invoice); any other read goes on as the resolution. `MakeOrderUsecase.resolve(pendingMake)`, under the same lock (so a robot read is sent after the make's request ended in this process; after a restart the request ended with it): a make no longer pending in the records sends nothing (`MakeNoLongerPending`); one without terms never left and is closed at once (`MakeNeverSent`); otherwise the robot is read back (`ReadRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase.execute`, its binding's coordinator, the short header) and its `active_order_id`, else its `last_order_id` (`api/views.py:725-737`), read by the robot: shown as its own, confirmed as above; error 1043, which proves nothing, or no order named closes the make (`MakeNotMade`); error 1022 is read once more (the order then at 5); an order not shown as this robot's own leaves it pending (`MakePendingReason.notRecognized`), as does any read that fails. A request still in flight that lands after the robot read named nothing makes an order at 0 that this app never shows (its bond invoice is made at the maker's first read only, `api/views.py:373-379`), which the coordinator expires 300 s after its creation with nothing paid (`api/logics.py:329-338`): tested, as is every point where the app can be killed (`test/make_order_usecase_test.dart`, `test/support/make_harness.dart`). `MakeOrderUsecase.pendingMakes(network)` lists the pending makes with whether their terms were never written (`ActivePendingMake.neverSent`: no terms row at all, never a row it cannot read), for the « Active trades » sheet of #182. `TradeMakeLocks` lives in the foreground process only: a background task that would resolve makes must run with no make in flight or under a shared lock. What the make's answer does not carry is compared by the state machine at every read since #179: the description (`TradeTermsChanged`, the bond hidden until « Show anyway » and never paid from this wallet) and, at 1 and 2, the public duration (`expires_at` − `created_at`), beside `has_password` (#178). A trade made here is opened by `TradeReader.open` from its `maker_terms` (and a confirmed make without its first trail on `TradeTrail.forConfirmedMake`, whose first read is `afterMake`'s), listed by `ListActiveTradesUsecase` from `TradeTrailRepository.watchMadeHistories`, and never confirmed, closed or read as a take. `CancelTradeUsecase` cancels as the maker at 0, 1 and 2 for free (only for a record `madeHere` whose last read shows this robot as the maker, each half of that guard tested alone; a taker's trade at those statuses is still `CancelNotPossible`, and so is a record `madeHere` read at 3, which a maker never sees), at 6 and 7 with `BondLossAcknowledged` as a taker's; the guard is the status shown; an answer that ends the order (`OrderCancelEnded`, or for a maker an order answered whole at 4 or 12) is kept as error 1043 says it, never as a read (which would be `TradeStatusRegressed`, a breach); `CancelDone.makerBondReturned` says when that very cancel ended a public or paused order, whose bond the coordinator returns first (`close_public_order`, `api/logics.py:961-1003`), which `Cancelled.bondMayHaveBeenLost` cannot know: a screen says « nothing was taken » only once the bond's payment record is final with nothing sent; an order answered unchanged (alive) to a cancel is no end, the record stays open, and `CancelDone` then carries the live status, so a screen judges by `seen.status`, never by the outcome's type (security review of #179). `PayBondFromWalletUsecase` offered nothing on a maker's terms until #180, which pays the maker bond at 0 (below); a maker seller's escrow at 6 and 7 was never offered, quoted or paid until #187, which pays it under its own rules (below). `PayoutFromWalletUsecase.payoutKey` appends the contract (`network:alias:orderId:take`) from contract 1 on, the key of contract 0 unchanged. `CoordinatorLottery` (`lib/src/domain/coordinator_lottery.dart`) is the reference's `federationLottery` for the form's default host (#181). The scripted coordinator of a maker is `ScriptedMakerCoordinator` (`lib/testing.dart`), apart from the taker's `ScriptedCoordinator`, which is unchanged. `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` pins that only the make use case writes, confirms or closes a pending make, writes a make's terms, names `afterMake` and sends `makeOrder`, that only it and the reader name `TradeTrail.forConfirmedMake`, and that it, the take and the panel's cubit put a robot on a coordinator; `trade_money_calls_architecture_test.dart` pins which use case names each money member, `makeOrder` in the make's only, `pauseOrder` in the pause's only (#184). Rules for #180 to #182: call `MakeOrderUsecase` from the create screen's section only; show a bond only from a `MakeStarted` or a watch status marked `stored`; resolve a pending make from its line of « Active trades » (`resolve`), never make again; word `MakePending`, `MakeNotMade`, `MakeNeverSent` with texts of their own, never a coordinator's.
- **The maker bond paid from this wallet (#180): « Pay with this wallet » at a maker's 0, under the owner's payment rule of 2026-11-17.** Design note `docs/design/phase-3-making-an-order.md` § 3.3, § 4.3, § 1.11. No screen reaches it yet (« Create » is enabled by #182). `PayBondFromWalletUsecase` (the bond's instance) pays the maker bond from a stored `WaitingForMakerBond` by the very path of a taker's bond (`checkBond`, `quoteBond`, `payBond` under `TradeTakeLocks` with its fresh read, the start recorded in `trade_payments` under the step `bond`, contract 0, before `payInvoice`, `followBond`), within `TradeTerms.bondCeiling`, which for a maker is `MakerTerms.bondCeiling` (the bond at the creation's price with 2 % and 1 sat, rounded down), never through a cast: `_offered` tests `terms is TradeTerms<TakenSide>` and, for a maker, pays only the step `bond` at `WaitingForMakerBond`; a taker's terms are never paid at a maker's step, a maker seller's escrow at 6 and 7 only by the escrow's instance under #187's rules (below), and the escrow's instance never pays a maker bond.
