Tests: `protected_take_test.dart`, `protected_take_cubits_test.dart`, `protected_order_route_test.dart` (by real gestures on the real composition, in English and in French: a protected order, a public order the book in memory does not hold, an order already taken), the package's `order_test.dart`; the scripted coordinator leaves a protected order out of its book, hides its description and place from a robot that takes no part, refuses a wrong password with the reference's 403 and message, and takes a payment method and a meeting point of the test's.
- **The owner's payment rule for an order taken here (#249): the taker bond, a taker seller's escrow and a taker buyer's payout compared with the Bull wallet's price.** Design note `docs/design/phase-3-taken-order-price-check.md` § 3 to § 8 (slices 1 to 3; slice 4, the order's page before the take, option B of its question 1 as the owner decided on 2026-11-24, the alert only: « The order page before the take » below). The owner's decision of 2026-11-20 extends to a take the rule of 2026-11-17 for orders made here: information and a warning beyond `TradeTolerances.walletRateGap` on the side that costs, never a lock.
  The figure expected comes from the taker's terms kept at the take, never from the coordinator: the amount taken (`TakenSide.amount`, a range's amount chosen, never its maximum) at the Bull wallet's price of the moment with the premium kept (`TakenTradeTerms.satsAtPrice`, over `amountSatsAtPrice` of `lib/src/domain/trade/sats_at_price.dart`, which `MakerTerms.satsAtPrice` and `.bondAtPrice` now call too; a premium of −100 % or a price outside `TradePriceBounds` gives no figure, « rate not checked »); for the bond, times the bond size. `TakenSide.bookPrice` is the coordinator's word and is never the reference. The taker fee is not added, so that it shows in the gap; at 9 a taker buyer's sats are `TakenSide.payoutAt` of `satoshis_now`, the taker fee off (`payoutLessFee`, shared with `MakerTerms.payoutAt`).
  `WalletRateCheck.ofTakerBond`, `.ofTakerEscrow` (more costs) and `.ofTakerPayout` (fewer costs); `PayBondFromWalletUsecase.rateOfBond` answers for a take at both steps (at the escrow step only for a taker seller, and `BondPayInvoiceChanged` for a quote of another invoice than the escrow the trail pins, as for a maker seller); the confirmation draws it for a take (`PayConfirmationCard` since #250; before, `_Confirmation`, `_RateLines` taking the escrow's keys at the escrow step (`p2pPayRateWalletEscrow`, and beyond the threshold the line and `p2pPayRateWarningEscrow` as one red alert, as a maker seller's confirmation draws them)). `CheckPayoutRateUsecase.termsOf` gives a sealed `PayoutRateTerms` (`MakerPayoutTerms`, `TakerPayoutTerms`); `subjectOfTake` compares at 6, 8 and 9 as for a maker buyer, without a maker's per-contract cases, and nothing at all while `takeDisagrees` (the maker's `amountsDisagree` and `TradeTermsChanged`), the invoice kept included; `PayoutRateCubit` runs for any buyer's trade, `termsOf` saying which (« Reading » before the terms are known only for an order made here). What counts as an alert beside a comparison is written once, `ShownAlerts` (`lib/src/domain/trade/shown_alerts.dart`): a warning of the state machine on the status or its invoice but `ContractSeenLate`, the trace of terms shown otherwise (#231), at 9 the trace of a buyer's payout not given from this wallet (#204); beside any of them a comparison within the threshold is not drawn, at the bond's and the escrow's confirmation (a maker seller's escrow confirmation adding its own alerts: earlier escrows not back or kept, the bond held too, a confirmation stale or asked again) and at a buyer's payout (`PayoutRateAlertAlone`, `CheckPayoutRateUsecase.reassuresBesideAlert`), for a taker and a maker alike; a comparison that is itself an alert stays (red, « rate not checked », a currency not priced, a figure that moved since the invoice), but at a payout beside an alert of the amounts (for a taker of the terms too, `takeDisagrees`; for a maker of the contract's amount, `amountsDisagree`, `contractAmountMovedFrom`), where nothing is compared at all (for an order made here at 9 the kept invoice alone, drawn only when red): an alert never silences another, in the confirmations too, whose comparison stays drawn, red, when a read brings a warning while it is open (security review of #249, C5; a maker seller's escrow line names the contract's amount the check carries, `WalletRateCheck.amount`, never a read's). `termsShownOtherwise` and `payoutTraceOf` of the trade screen draw their traces on the same conditions (reviews of #249: functional C1, security C1 and C2).
  The two points the owner decided on 2026-11-24 (the note's § 7, questions 2 and 3, as it recommended) are `TakenRateRules`: a currency the Bull wallet does not price gives the « not priced » line alone (`notPriced`), and an order priced in sats is compared as the others (`comparesOrdersPricedInSats`). No text is new or changed: the existing keys of the rule for orders made here, whose rewording is #250. Tests: `taken_price_check_test.dart` (the figures, by hand), `taken_price_usecases_test.dart` (the use cases over `ScriptedCoordinator`), `taken_price_route_test.dart` (the gestures on the real composition, in English and in French); `ScriptedCoordinator` takes the order's `currency`, `premium` and `price` for them, its defaults unchanged.
  **The order page before the take (slice 4, option B: the alert only).** Once « Take Order » has shown the robot's read (`TakeReady`), `TakeOrderCubit` compares the coordinator's price of a bitcoin without the premium from the terms the take is sent on (`TakenPriceCheck.coordinatorPrice`: `TakenSide.bookPrice` ÷ (1 + premium ÷ 100), exactly, then the nearest double; `null` for a premium of −100 % or a price outside `TradePriceBounds`, drawn « Price not checked » with nothing read) with the Bull wallet's, through `CheckMakePriceUsecase.execute` as it is (`CheckTakePrice`, given by the locator only where the wallet's port was lent, to the three registrations of the cubit: the book's order, the order by its URL, the order public again from the trade screen), the result kept per currency for `TakeOrderCubit.staleAfter` (3 minutes, by the cubit of one page, as the create form keeps it: five pages taken one after the other make five reads; a « Price not checked » is kept so too, « Retry » reading again) and read again at each « Retry » (`retryPrice`); never when the page opens, never while the book is browsed, never for an order whose read is not shown; an answer for a read no longer shown is dropped (kept for the next comparison of its currency). `MakePriceCheck.warnsFor` is given the taker's own side (`TakenPriceCheck.sideOf(role)`: a taker of a buy order is a seller), and what the page shows is `TakenPriceShown` (`shownOf`): nothing within the threshold, while it is read (the form's « Reading … » line is not used: it does not say « Bull Wallet », and it would come and go at each take, moving « Continue » twice), or for a seller and a price above the Bull wallet's; « Price against you » and the signed gap (a buyer above, a seller below); « Price far below the Bull price » for a buyer below (the form warns a buyer both ways); then « Bull Wallet uses X CUR/BTC, the coordinator Y. » (both without the premium); « Price not checked » in orange with « Retry » (kept drawn, « Retry » not offered, while it reads again) and its line `p2pPayoutBreakdownUnverifiedLine`; the explanation of either (`p2pTakePriceDetail`, `p2pTakePriceUnverifiedDetail`) behind « Show details » / « Hide details » (`DetailToggle`, `TakeOrderDetailsCard.priceDetailToggle`) just under the alert's lines in the same frame, the detail opening under the link (the owner's decision of 2026-11-25, #270): folded at first and for each new alert (another `TakenPriceShown`, or another `MakePriceCheck`: a read compared anew, « Retry » answered), kept while « Retry » reads again, never opened by itself, the link's state said to a screen reader (`Semantics.expanded`); the grey « not quoted » line (`PriceCheckLines.notPriced`), with no explanation and no link. Keep it so: the bold line and the two prices (or « Retry » and its line) never fold. It is drawn under « Price and premium » of `PublicOrderDetailsCard` (`priceAlert`, built by `TakeOrderDetailsCard`) with `PriceCheckLines` (its bold line given by `label`), above « Continue », always without a tap; it silences nothing: « The order changed. », the marked rows, the reversible-payments warning and the lines under the button stay as they are. « Continue » is in a `MovedTapGuard` (`TakeOrderSection.continueButton`), its tap ignored for 700 ms after the alert, its detail opened or folded (#270), « The order changed. » or « This page has changed » moved it; pressed while the Bull wallet's price is read (a first read, or one « Retry » started) it waits (busy, `priceSettled`), then stops once with « This page has changed » / « Read the price line again, then continue again. » (`TakeOrderSection.pageChangedBlock`, an orange block on the model of « The amounts have changed ») only when `priceShown` is now a line it was not at the press (the red alert, « Price not checked » or « not quoted »), never when the page shows nothing (a read again after « Retry » back within the threshold goes on), and never for a read no longer shown (gone back, or taken again meanwhile: nothing opens), a press again going on. It is the create form's rule: « Create order » stops only when the line read during its wait warns, is not priced or is not checked and differs from the one at the press (`MakePriceSeen.sameLineAs`, `MakePriceVerdict.stopsPressFor`), the form drawing its reading line where the page keeps « Price not checked » drawn while « Retry » reads, so that « not checked » again is no new line here. The verdict is not checked again at `confirm`'s read (§ 8 point 4): the take is sent on the very terms compared, never a lock. What is compared is shown (security review of slice 4, C1): a read's « Price and premium » is always the terms' (`TakenSide.bookPrice`, from the read's sats, never its `price_now`, and `TradeTerms.premium`), even for an order priced in sats and a read without `price_now`, whose « Amount of satoshis » follows in a row of its own; for such an order `TakenPriceCheck.premiumMovedFrom` marks that row (`OrderDetailRow.changed`, never a lock), and « The order changed. » is said above « Continue » with it (#270: where a confirmation that found the order changed says it, once when both hold, so that the mark is not a colour only), when the premium read moved from the one the page showed before « Take Order » (the book's, or for an order by its URL the read made when its page opened) by more than `TradeTolerances.price`: the coordinator computes it as the order's rate over its exchange rate, less 1, truncated to two decimals (`api/logics.py:277-296`), so between two reads it moves only with that rate, which the trade's checks let move by `TradeTolerances.price`. A read of the Bull wallet's price that throws (an `Error`, a bug of the price's service that `ReadWalletRateUsecase` lets through on purpose, or anything else, never a failure it answers) is « Price not checked » since #270, as a price not read (« Retry », « Continue » possible, kept for the currency as any answer): whatever it throws is caught in `TakeOrderCubit._checked` only (`on Object`), so that `priceSettled` ends and « Continue » opens « Store your garage key », and reported to the zone with its stack (`Zone.current.handleUncaughtError`, as the trade's use cases report what a callback throws), as thrown, the feature adding nothing to it; the app's zone hands it to `log.severe` as any unhandled error (`lib/bootstrap.dart`), which writes it to the support log and passes it to the error reporter: in a release build, when the user consented to error reports, the wallet's Sentry receives its type and its stack, not its value (`lib/core/utils/report.dart`); a debug build reports nothing. **A declared exception to rule 11** (an `Error` is never caught): « Continue » must never stay waiting on a read that failed, and the report to the zone keeps the bug from going unseen (review of #276, #270). `_continue` still resets `_waitingPrice` in a `finally`. « Retry » that fails again does not stop a « Continue » pressed while it reads (« Price not checked » drawn at the press), where the create form, which draws its reading line meanwhile, stops (tested since #270). The words (`p2pTakePrice*`) were proposed to the owner on 2026-11-24 on the model of #250's, and `p2pTakePageChanged*` on 2026-11-25 after the functional review; the owner validated the alert's sentences on 2026-11-25 (#270); the other 20 languages fall back to English. What the price service learns more than before: the currency, the time and the IP address at « Take Order », the moment a user is about to take an order of that currency, outside Tor; nothing of a read that answers is logged, and a read that throws is reported as said above. Tests: `taken_price_page_check_test.dart` (the price without the premium, the bounds at a hundredth, each role's side), `take_order_price_cubit_test.dart` (one read per 3 minutes and currency, « Retry », nothing at the opening, the wait), `taken_price_page_route_test.dart` (the gestures on the real composition, in English and in French, both roles, from the book, by the URL and from the trade screen of an order public again, the stops of « Continue » and its goings-on, the tap guard, 320 points at ×1, ×2, ×3; since #270 the explanation opened and folded by taps that never open the take, a new alert folded, the screen reader's state of the link, « The order changed. » with the premium's mark, a read that throws, reported to the zone, « Retry » that fails again).
- **The buyer's payout use case and the trade keyring (#90, phase 2): `SubmitPayoutUsecase`, `HoldTradeKeyUsecase`, `TradeKeyring`** (`lib/src/domain/usecases/trade/`; values in `lib/src/domain/trade/trade_payout.dart` and `trade_payout_outcome.dart`). The port of the wallet that makes an address or an invoice (`newReceiveAddress`, `newLightningInvoice`, the third point of #90) is not built yet: what it gives will be a text, given to the same check.
  The keyring (`TradeKeyring`, a lazy singleton of the composition): the pair of a trade's robot is not stored; a lease reads the robot back from the coordinator of its binding (`ReadRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase.provenPairOf`, the read of the Garage's panel, pair proven by the token, through `RobotRequestLimiter`) and unlocks it away from the UI isolate (`RobotKeyComputePort.unlock`, `IsolateRobotKeyCompute`: 32 MiB of SHA-256 for the proof and as much for the unlock). The chat's read decrypts away from it too (`RobotKeyComputePort.decryptChat`, #93): for each page that holds something to decrypt, a short-lived isolate (`Isolate.run`) gets a copy of `UnlockedRobotPgpKey.chatDecryption` (the key and the scalar of its encryption subkey only, never the robot's identity, its token, nor the seed that signs), the jobs (armored messages and public keys) and the time; the decrypted results come back, their texts included; the worker's heap is freed when it exits, not wiped, as for the unlock. The lease is closed in a `finally` on every path, an exception or a `RemoteError` of the worker included; nothing cancels the work and no timeout bounds it, the work being bounded by `PgpLimits`. The key is kept per robot of a trade while a hold or a lease is open, and dropped as soon as none is: the trade screen takes a hold (`HoldTradeKeyUsecase.execute(record)`) while it is shown with the app in the foreground and releases it (`TradeKeyHold.release`, idempotent) when it closes or the app is hidden or paused, taking a new one on return; a hold unlocks and sends nothing by itself (the first lease does it, so a screen at 3, 7 or 9 costs nothing); the payout use case takes a lease only to sign and closes it right after the signature, before the request leaves, so that without a hold the key lives for one signature; two screens of one trade share one key; a lease open when the last hold goes (the app hidden during a signature) keeps the key until the signature is over; a superseded watch changes nothing (holds are not the watch's); one unlock per robot at a time, shared, and a failed one is not kept, nor one that threw (an `Error`, which still reaches the caller); an entry dropped is emptied and a closed lease holds neither the key nor the entry (review of #127), so that nothing released leads back to the key. The unlocked key lives in the main isolate's heap (an `Isolate.run` of the same group brings it back), referenced by the keyring alone: « dropped » means unreferenced, since `UnlockedRobotPgpKey` offers no way to wipe its secret values (to add in `packages/robosats` with the Rust replacement of the PGP layer). The pair is not compared from one hold to the next (nothing is stored, by design); `TradeKeyChanged` of the design note § 3.4 is left to the chat (#93), where the peer sees the key. `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` pins that only the keyring names `RobotKeyComputePort.unlock` and `ReadRobotOnCoordinatorUsecase.provenPairOf`, only `IsolateRobotKeyCompute` names `RobotPgpKey.unlock`, only the payout use case takes a lease, reads `TradeKeyLease.key` and names `signPayoutInvoice`/`signPayoutAddress`, only `HoldTradeKeyUsecase` takes a hold, no file names another member of `UnlockedRobotPgpKey` (`signCleartext`, the chat's), and which files may name the type.
  The checks (`PayoutAsk.of(record, seen)`, `PayoutAsk.check(destination, now:, reader:)`, pure): only from a `TradeSeen` marked stored with its order (`PayoutNeedsARead`), of this robot as the buyer of the record's order (its number, network and coordinator, alias and onion, compared with the record's), at a step that takes it: an invoice at 6 or 8, or at 15 once `invoice_expired`; an address at 6 or 8 with `swap_allowed` (`PayoutNotAsked`, `PayoutOnchainNotOffered`). The amount asked is the reference client's, from `trade_satoshis` (`LightningPayout.tsx:110-113`): `RoutingBudgetPpm.invoiceSats`, `floor(n − n × ppm / 10⁶)`, 1,000 ppm by default, always sent. An invoice (`PayoutInvoiceText(text, budget:)`): the reference's `lightning:` prefix taken off (case-sensitive, `:145-147`), read by `Bolt11Reader` (`PayoutInvoiceUnreadable`; when the reader refuses the amount, the human-readable part tells the reason, the network first: `PayoutInvoiceOfAnotherNetwork`, then `PayoutInvoiceAmountNotWholeSats` for no amount or a fraction of a sat, and `PayoutInvoiceUnreadable` for an amount in no strict form such as `1.5u`), on the trade's network (`PayoutInvoiceOfAnotherNetwork`, no way around it), exactly the amount asked (`PayoutInvoiceAmountNotAsked`, as the reference disables « Submit »), not expired at the check (`PayoutInvoiceExpired`), and `PayoutWarning.invoiceMayExpireFirst` when it expires before the step's `expires_at` and `TradeTolerances.payoutInvoiceAfterStep` (24 h, the chat's `FIAT_EXCHANGE_DURATION`; not at 15, where the coordinator pays at once). An address (`PayoutAddressText(text, rate:)`): `BitcoinAddress.check` for the trade's network (`PayoutAddressUnusable` with its reason), a `MiningFeeRate` (2 to 500 sat/vB, 3 decimals), what it receives as the coordinator computes it (`OnchainAsk.sentSatsFor`, `api/logics.py:806-813`, from `invoice_amount` as the reference's panel), and `PayoutWarning.sentBelowMinimum` under 20,000 sats. The status's warnings are carried (`CheckedPayout.statusWarnings`). Warnings are information, never refusals (design note § 3.7); the refusals are the coordinator's own rules, said before anything is signed. The result, `CheckedPayoutInvoice` or `CheckedPayoutAddress`, only `PayoutAsk.check` builds, and it carries the exact text decoded.
  The submission (`SubmitPayoutUsecase.execute(record, shown)`, in this order, each step only once the one before it succeeded): the payout shown is the record's (`PayoutOfAnotherTrade`); the trade opened from its records; one action of the order at a time in the process (`TradeTakeLocks`, shared with the take); the lease (`PayoutKeyUnavailable`); the order read again and kept, a status marked stored only (`PayoutReadFailed`); the destination shown checked again against that read (`PayoutNoLongerValid` with the reason and the status read; `PayoutChangedSinceShown` with the fresh value when a figure, the network or a warning the user did not see would change); the very text shown signed (`signPayoutInvoice`, `signPayoutAddress`), the signed text, network and signer compared with what was shown (`PayoutNotSigned`), the lease closed; sent once through the robot's places (a busy transport tried again as the limiter says); a refusal the client decided from that read (`PayoutRefused.fromLastRead`) followed by one more read and one more try, signed again, then `PayoutRefusedFromRead`; then the answer kept and the order read again and kept: `PayoutGiven`, `PayoutNotTaken` (with its cause: a `PayoutRefused` kind, `OrderNotParticipant`, or a `P2pFailure` when the request never reached the coordinator), `PayoutOutcomeUnknown` (never sent again: the read after it tells; a status past the step means the coordinator has it). `Err` always means nothing was sent.
  Deviations from the reference, each with its reason: an invoice whose amount is not a whole number of sats (a millisatoshi precision such as `…500p`) refused (`PayoutInvoiceAmountNotWholeSats`), although the coordinator takes one whose whole sats are right (LND compares `num_satoshis`, `api/lightning/lnd.py:514-520`) and the reference client floors it (`LightningPayout.tsx:115-128`): the amount shown to the user and signed is a whole number of sats, and an invoice that asks a fraction more is not exactly what is shown (review of #127); the expiry checked before signing (refused when already expired, as the coordinator would; said when it may expire before the coordinator pays: design note § 4.4, information); what an address would receive under 20,000 sats said before signing (information; the coordinator refuses it); the status's warnings shown with the payout (the state machine's checks, information); « What the address receives » computed as the coordinator computes it (`int(n − n × fee / 100 − int(rate × 280))`, which can be a sat above the reference panel's `floor`); the fresh read just before signing, and the payout shown again when it changed (integration: what is signed is what was shown); one action of an order at a time in the process (integration); no Lnproxy (a third-party server, design note § 2.2, for the panel).
  Rules for the payout panel (#91): call `SubmitPayoutUsecase.ask(record, seen)` with the `TradeSeen` it shows (marked stored) and show its figures: « Submit invoice for {{amountSats}} Sats » from `PayoutAsk.invoiceSatsFor(budget)` (never computed by the screen), the routing budget as an advanced option (`RoutingBudgetPpm.of`, 0 to 99,998, default `RoutingBudgetPpm.referenceDefault`), the « Onchain » tab only when `PayoutAsk.onchain` is not null (otherwise `onchainRefusal` in a line), with `OnchainAsk.swapFeeSats`, `miningFeeSatsFor(rate)` and `sentSatsFor(rate)`, the rate's default the coordinator's suggestion as it gives it (`OnchainAsk.suggestedSatPerVbyte`, as the reference does, `Forms/OnchainPayout.tsx:48-50`; above 500 the field says « Invalid » and « Submit » stays off, as there); keep what the user typed and chose in the panel's cubit, never in a widget, so that what is shown is what is checked whenever the panel is built (review of #132); on paste (read once on the user's tap, never polled), call `check` and show what the `CheckedPayout` holds, its figures rather than ones computed from the fields: for an invoice its decoded amount (`CheckedPayoutInvoice.amount`, the amount the coordinator pays), its expiry (`expiresAt`), the trade's network, and every warning (`warnings`, `statusWarnings`) with a text of its own; for an address the address as checked (`text`), the rate and the amount it receives (`sentSats`); show each `PayoutCheckRefusal` with a text of its own (for `PayoutInvoiceAmountNotWholeSats`: ask for an invoice of exactly the whole sats shown); on confirmation give that very `CheckedPayout` to `execute`, never a text read again or edited after it was shown, and keep the confirm button disabled while one runs; on `PayoutChangedSinceShown` check the form again against the fresh read it carries, show that and ask again; never retry by itself after `PayoutOutcomeUnknown` (show the status read, and let the user give a payout again only if the status still asks for one); take a hold (`HoldTradeKeyUsecase`) while the payout panel is shown with the app in the foreground and its tab of the shell visible, and release it when the panel goes, the app is hidden or paused, or another tab is shown (built so in #91, « The payout panel (#91) » below). Never display, log, print, store, report or put in a failure the signed message, the robot's key or token, and never log the invoice, the address or a payment hash: the values print their kind only; the invoice and the address are shown only to the user who gave them. The texts of the coordinator are never shown (the refusals are kinds).
- **The encrypted chat of a trade (#92, phase 2): `CoordinatorClient.fetchChat` and `postChat` of `packages/robosats`** (its README, section « the encrypted chat of a trade »): messages are read from the API only, and each message sent is also published over Nostr (#94, below). The chat use cases of #93 call them; both take a `RobotIdentity`, so `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` allows them in `fetch_robot_usecase.dart` only, and the chat use cases of #93 must be added there deliberately. They move no money and are not on the money members' list. `fetchChat({identity, lastRead, offset})` gives a `ChatPage`: `peerConnected`, the peer's key as a `ChatPeerKey` (`ChatPeerKeyOfPeer`, `ChatPeerKeyMisnamed`, `ChatPeerKeyMissing`, `ChatPeerKeyUnreadable`) and the `ChatMessage`s above the offset in the coordinator's order (`index`, `time`, `nick`, `sender`: `self`, `peer` or `unknown`; `content`: `pgpMessage` with `armored`, or `notPgpMessage` / `tooLong` without text), with the facts `inIndexOrder`, `repeatsAnIndex`, `isContiguous`, `repeatsACiphertext`, `lastIndex`, `contiguousIndex` and `contiguousIndexOverRepeats`, each message's armor kept canonical (a message whose armor does not decode is `notPgpMessage`); a `200` page over 4 MiB is `ChatPageTooLarge`. A validly signed message, once decrypted, has `DecryptedChatMessage.signatureKey`, a `ChatSignatureKey`. `postChat({identity, lastRead, message, offset})` sends only an `OutgoingChatMessage`, which only `UnlockedRobotPgpKey.encryptChatMessageToPost` makes (at most `OutgoingChatMessage.maxTextBytes`, 3,250 bytes of text as `textBytesOf` counts them, checked before encrypting: `PgpChatTextTooLong`; at most 5,000 characters on the wire), and answers a `ChatPage`. Refusals are kinds without coordinator text: `ChatRefused` with a `ChatRefusal` (`orderIdMissing` 6000, `notParticipant` 6001, `notInChat` 6002, `invalidRequest` 6003, and before sending `offsetOutOfRange`, `messageOfAnotherRobot`, and `notParticipant`, `notInChat` with `fromLastRead`), `OrderReadElsewhere`, `RobotUnknown`, `RobotCredentialsRejected`; a post fails with `OrderActionRefused` for those (the coordinator's word that nothing was stored, or nothing sent) and `OrderActionOutcomeUnknown` for everything else. The rules for #93:
  - **The peer's key is the coordinator's word.** `ChatPeerKeyOfPeer` proves only that the key's user ID is exactly `RoboSats ID <hash id>` of the peer the coordinator names, the only form the official client writes: not that the key is the peer's. A coordinator that gives each side, from the first page, a key it made in the other's name reads, rewrites, encrypts again and signs again every message both ways, and every message verifies: the chat, payment details included, is then the coordinator's. So: pin the peer's key for the trade at its first `ChatPeerKeyOfPeer`, and treat any later page whose key is another (`PgpPublicKey` equality), misnamed, missing or unreadable as a breach (`TradeKeyChanged`, design note § 3.4, § 3.6): stop sending, say so, never encrypt to a key read again without comparing it, compare `OutgoingChatMessage.peerFingerprint` with the pinned key before posting, never take a `ChatPeerKeyMisnamed` key. And offer the user both public keys and their fingerprints to compare with the peer outside the app: own `RobotPgpKey.publicKey.armored` / `.fingerprint` (the robot read back, or `UnlockedRobotPgpKey.key.publicKey`), the peer's from the pinned `ChatPeerKeyOfPeer.key`.
  - **Decided by the owner (2026-11-15, option A): what the « Don't trust, verify » panel shows and exports.** The official client's dialog (`frontend/src/components/Dialogs/AuditPGP.tsx:107-169`) shows four fields, each with a copy button: own public key, peer public key, own encrypted private key, and the robot token labelled as the private key's passphrase; it exports « Keys » as JSON `{own_public_key, peer_public_key, encrypted_private_key, passphrase}` (`:186-195`) and « Messages » as JSON (`:222-225`); it shows no fingerprints. The security consideration: the token is the robot's whole credential on its coordinator, so whoever holds the exported file, or what was copied, can act as the robot there (confirm, cancel, open a dispute) and unlock its key. The two options: (A) as the reference, the four fields and both exports; (B) the public keys (with their fingerprints) and the messages only. The owner chose (A), as the reference: « The chat panel of a trade » below.
  - Key messages by index and keep the first one seen (the reference does, `EncryptedApiChat/index.tsx:174-178`); poll from `contiguousIndex`, never from `lastIndex` (one message at index 2,147,483,647 would hide every later one); messages beyond a gap are shown, kept by index, with « A message may be missing before this one. ». A gap that persists keeps the poll offset back, so every poll reads and parses everything beyond it again, up to 4 MiB (about 47,800 tiny messages, 366 ms to parse on a desktop, several times that on a phone), and a peer can cause one cheaply, two posts at once on the reference's unlocked numbering: once the same gap has held for three polls with the messages beyond it unchanged (the same indexes and the same canonical `armored` above `contiguousIndex`, which the use case keeps anyway), poll from `contiguousIndexOverRepeats` when it is beyond the gap, which carries the offset over the indexes the numbering skips after a repeated index and over no other gap, and keep the gap marked; any other gap is never polled past. Run the poll's parsing and the page's facts off the UI isolate: `fetchChat` decodes and parses the page synchronously; if the transport keeps it on the UI isolate, measure, and if it janks, the package has to parse in an isolate (a later change, without the robot's identity: the bytes, both nicknames, the peer's hash id and the offset are enough); and a page that is not contiguous or repeats an index is said to be so (a dropped or replayed message: the coordinator chooses which messages and in which order, and the reference's own numbering can repeat or skip an index); never take a later page's message over an index already shown.
  - **A message shown again.** A coordinator can show an old payment detail again, at a new index, after the peer corrected it, and the replay verifies. Keep, for the trade, the `ChatSignatureKey` of every validly signed message (in memory only, never logged: it holds a digest of the text, not the text): the same key at two indexes, within a page or across pages, is one message shown twice, shown as a repeated message, not as a new one. That key is what catches it whatever the coordinator did to the armor or to the packet framing; `repeatsACiphertext` (the same canonical armor at two indexes in one page) is only a first, cheap check on the page, which a coordinator passes by framing the packets again. Show each message's signature time (`DecryptedChatMessage.signedAt`) and flag a message signed earlier than an earlier-indexed message of the same sender. A message this robot sent again after a post whose outcome was unknown is a legitimate duplicate: labelled as sent twice, not as a breach.
  - Decrypt a `self` message with the robot's own key and a `peer` message with the pinned key; mark a message whose signature does not verify (« Signature not valid: this message may not come from {nick}. », § 3.6); a message of an `unknown` sender is from neither party and is never checked as the peer's; show `notPgpMessage` and `tooLong` as unreadable, without text.
  - After a post whose outcome is unknown, read again from the offset posted with and look for the message with `ChatPage.indexOf`: found, the coordinator shows it as stored; not found, show it as « not confirmed: it may still reach your peer », never « not sent » nor « failed » (a coordinator can keep it out of this robot's pages and still deliver it, give it the peer's nickname, or change one character of it); if the user sends it again, send the same `OutgoingChatMessage`, never a new encryption of the same text.
  - After a refusal with `ChatRefused.fromLastRead`, read the order again before telling the user anything: the last read may be stale.
  - `ChatPageTooLarge` (a peer can post enough to cause it, from offset 0, which is where a restart reads from): tell the user the history is too large to read from the start; never skip silently to a later offset such as the order's `chat_last_index`, which leaves out the earlier messages, the payment details among them; offer to read only the newer messages as the user's explicit choice, and say then that earlier ones are not shown. The package cannot read the history in parts: the coordinator answers every message above an offset at once, and the indexes are known only by reading them.
  - Check the text's length with `OutgoingChatMessage.textBytesOf` against `maxTextBytes` as the user types; never log, print, store or put in a report the plaintext, the armored or wire text of a message, or the peer's key (§ 4.3), with one exception: the peer's public key a trade's chat pinned, with its two fingerprints, is stored with the trade's record in `trade_chat_pins` (#139, below), not a secret since the coordinator and the peer hold it and the export hands it out; keep the chat in memory only; poll through `RobotRequestLimiter`, every 5 s while the chat is shown (§ 3.6, § 3.8); refuse to send the robot's token (the package does) and the payout text of the trade (§ 4.2).
- **The chat of a trade, its read and its send (#93, first part): `WatchChatUsecase`, `SendChatMessageUsecase`, `TradeChats`** (`lib/src/domain/usecases/trade/`; values in `lib/src/domain/trade/trade_chat.dart`). The rules of #92 above, built so:
  - `TradeChats` (a lazy singleton of the composition) keeps one `TradeChat` per trade (its robot and order) for the life of the process, in memory only: the messages by index (the first one seen kept, `ChatEntry`, whose `toString` shows its index, author and kind only), the canonical armor of each (for a peer's message read before the key, and the export), the `ChatSignatureKey` of every validly signed message, the peer's key, the offset to read from, and the messages posted whose outcome is not known. The peer's key is pinned at its first `ChatPeerKeyOfPeer` (`ChatPeerKeyPinned`) and kept when the trade screen is opened again; a misnamed key, or once pinned another key (a key forged in the peer's name included), none or an unreadable one, is a `ChatPeerKeyBreach`, which stays: nothing is sent any more in that chat (the pinned key is compared again inside the request, right before it leaves, after any wait of the limiter). So is a key that does not verify a message of the peer read with it in the page that pins it: after a restart, the whole history read again from offset 0. The rule applies only to the read that pins the key: it catches, after a restart, a forged key served in the same page as the peer's earlier messages, or after they waited for a key; it does not catch a forged key pinned on a page with no message of the peer (empty, or only this robot's) with the history served later, nor a history from which the peer's messages were dropped: those are only marked « signature not valid » (#139). Kept across a restart (#139): the pinned public key, in canonical armor (`PgpPublicKey.armored`, at most 20,000 characters, `PgpLimits.keyArmorChars`, also a CHECK; `PgpPublicKey.parse` refuses a key whose canonical armor would be longer, however few lines it was read from, so that every key read can be kept, security review of #139), with its primary and subkey fingerprints (`PgpPublicKey.fingerprint`, `subkeyFingerprint`), is stored with the trade's record, in the table `trade_chat_pins` of `p2p.sqlite` (schema 6, `StoredTradeChatPins`, `trade_chat_pins.drift`, `TradeChatPinRepositoryImpl`, its only writer: one row per trade, never deleted nor changed, a second one writing nothing, written only for an open trade); read back, the key goes through the package's bounded parser and must give the fingerprints kept, or the pin is unreadable. `WatchChatUsecase` reads it once a run before the chat takes its first page (a pin that cannot be read fails the read, so that no key is pinned without knowing whether one was kept: `TradeChat.restorePin`), and keeps the key a run pins (`TradeChat.pinToKeep`, `keepChatPin`, written after the page is taken, tried again at each read and at each send while the write fails, another key found kept a breach with that key as the one pinned before). A pin that cannot be read shows « The chat cannot be read on this device. » (`p2pChatUnavailable`), not the coordinator's name. No message is posted while the key a run pinned is not kept yet (`TradeChat.pinsKept`, checked in `SendChatMessageUsecase` before each post, « send again » included): the send first writes the pin (functional review of #139), and is refused as `ChatSendUnavailable` with a `P2pStorageFailure`, « not sent », while it cannot, so that a write that fails cannot leave a restart trusting another key (security review of #139). After a restart the same key is pinned again silently, and another key, a misnamed one, none or an unreadable one (a key with the pinned primary key and another encryption subkey included) is the same sticky breach as within one run, with the key kept as the one pinned before (`ChatPeerKeyBreach.pinned`): the peer's earlier messages stay read and checked with it, a message signed by another key is not valid, and « Export » and « Audit Chat » give it; a breach is never stored, the pin before it stays. What is stored is not secret: a public key that the coordinator and the peer already hold, and that the chat's export hands out, with its two fingerprints; never a private key, a message, or anything decrypted. The triggers protect the pins against the app's own SQL, and the pins protect against a coordinator that serves another key after a restart; they do not protect against whoever can write the file `p2p.sqlite` (a rooted device, malware with root, a restore tool): such a writer can drop the triggers and delete or replace a pin, and with a coordinator in league the next run then trusts the key served without a word (security review of #139). The parade would be a proof of the pin held by the vault (`packages/secrets`, a MAC of the row, say): not built. The chat's messages stay in memory only: the pinned key, and the trade's Nostr keys (below), are the only things of the chat that are stored. The limit of trust on first use: a trade with no pin kept (one recorded before this change included) pins at its first read, and a key forged in the peer's name then, before any message of the peer, is pinned silently (tested as such).
  - `WatchChatUsecase.read(record, seen)`: one read, for the screen's poll; `GET /api/chat/` through the robot's places, from `TradeChat.offset`, about `seen.order`; the robot's key leased from `TradeKeyring` only when the page holds a message to decrypt, and the lease closed at once (`ChatPages.take`); this robot's messages decrypted with its own public key, the peer's with the pinned key (a peer's message read before the key waits, `awaitingPeerKey`, and is read once the key is pinned), a message of neither party never checked as the peer's, a text that is not an encrypted message, too long, or not decryptable shown as unreadable; each text cleaned by `ChatEntry.displayable` (no control character but line feed and tab, and no format character, Unicode category Cf, nor variation selector: the bidirectional marks, U+061C, the zero-width characters, the zero-width joiner too, so that an emoji sequence shows as its parts, U+00AD, U+FEFF and the tag characters; U+2028 and U+2029 made line feeds; no private-use character, noncharacter, nor letter that shows nothing, U+115F, U+1160, U+2800, U+3164, U+FFA0 and U+034F; a run of combining marks cut to four), which is also what a copy gives, the panel adding a box of bounded height and a forced direction; marks: `afterGap` (« A message may be missing before this one. »), `repeatOf` (the same signed message at two indexes), `sentTwice` (this robot's own message sent again after an unknown outcome), `signedOutOfOrder`; `ChatView.incomplete` when the page is not contiguous or repeats an index. The offset is `ChatPage.contiguousIndex`, carried to `contiguousIndexOverRepeats` once the same gap held for three reads with the messages beyond it unchanged (remembered from the page that showed the repeated index, since later pages asked from before the gap no longer show it). `ChatPageTooLarge` sets `historyTooLarge` and never moves the offset; `readNewerOnly` moves it to the order's `chat_last_index` on the user's choice, and `skippedEarlier` says the earlier messages are not shown; when the order shown was read before those messages (its `chat_last_index`, 0 for a chat that was empty as the reference answers, is not past the offset) it skips nothing and says so, `ChatSkipNeedsOrderRead`: the order must be read again first.
  - `SendChatMessageUsecase.send(record, seen, text)`: the text as typed, refused before anything leaves (`refusalOf`, pure, for the screen as the user types: `ChatTextEmpty`; `ChatTextTooLong` over `OutgoingChatMessage.maxTextBytes`, 3,250 bytes, not the 4,000 characters of the design note, so that the armored message stays within the package's own bound, `OutgoingChatMessage.maxWireChars`, the 5,000 characters the coordinator's model declares (the coordinator itself enforces no limit, `chat/serializers.py:62-65`); `ChatTextHoldsGarageKey`, a `robo1` bech32 Garage Key by its shape alone, the stored key never read), then `ChatPeerKeyNotKnown` or `ChatPeerKeyChanged`, then `ChatTextHoldsToken` from the package's `PgpPlaintextContainsToken`; encrypted with a lease to the pinned key (compared with the key pinned as it is right before the post, in `send` and `sendAgain`, so that a breach read while the send waited stops it), posted once with the chat's offset; `ChatSentStored(index)` when the answer, or after an outcome unknown a read from the offset posted with, shows it (`ChatPage.indexOf`); otherwise `ChatSentNotConfirmed` with a `ChatPendingMessage`, never sent again but by `sendAgain(record, seen, id)`, the user's choice, which posts the same `OutgoingChatMessage`. A refusal (`ChatSendRefused`, with `fromLastRead` from the package) stored nothing; the limiter that sent nothing is one too.
  - Risk left as the reference leaves it, the owner's choice (2026-11-15: stay with the reference): a chat message that is only a Lightning invoice or a Bitcoin address carries a signature that converts into this robot's signed payout (a chat signature is the same text signature as a clear-signed payout, the signed bytes identical for one line). The peer alone cannot use it, since a coordinator checks a payout against the key of the robot that sends it, authenticated by its token's hash; whoever holds that hash, colluding with the peer, could submit it at status 15 of this trade or at the payout step of a later trade where this robot buys, the signature being tied to no order. A robot made in this app is shown to one coordinator only, and a pasted key's robots cannot take an order here, so that needs a leak of that coordinator's records. The reference does not guard against it, and nothing here does: such a text is sent as typed. The refusals before sending are the reference's check of the token, the Garage Key's (the same check extended to the key the tokens derive from), the empty text and the package's size bound.
  - `robot_bindings_architecture_test.dart` pins it: `fetchChat` in the two chat use cases only, `postChat` in the send's only, `TradeKeyring.lease` and `TradeKeyLease.key` in the payout's and the chat's files, `encryptChatMessageToPost` in the send's file, `chatDecryption` and `ChatDecryptionKey.decryptChatMessage` in `IsolateRobotKeyCompute` only (the decryption in its isolate, above) and `RobotKeyComputePort.decryptChat` in the read's only, no other member of `UnlockedRobotPgpKey`. The scripted coordinator of the test seam answers `GET` and `POST /api/chat/` as the reference does (no `order_id` 6000, an unknown order a server error, 9, 10, 11, 16 to read, 9 and 10 to post, a blank message or a negative offset 6003, a GET offset that is not an integer a server error, `{}` for a post without offset, the next index after the highest, `chat_last_index` 0 for an empty chat and otherwise the highest index; known gaps: the taker only, where the reference lets the maker read and post too, and messages in index order, where the reference's query has no `ORDER BY`), with a maker robot the test gives (`ScriptedCoordinator(maker:)`), its key (`peerPublicKey`), its messages (`peerSays`) and a chat the test may change (`chat`).
- **The chat over Nostr (#94): every message sent is also published to Nostr relays, as the official client does.** The official client sends each message to the coordinator and over Nostr (`onSendMessage`, `EncryptedChat/index.tsx:201-239`, commit ff1998c) and, with its default setting and a Garage Key, shows only the Nostr messages (`EncryptedNostrChat/index.tsx`): a peer on it did not read Troc's messages (owner's test, 15/11/2026; set to « API » it did). Owner's decisions (15/11/2026): D1 the `bip340` package, NIP-44 and NIP-59 written here on `pointycastle`; D2 a pool as the reference's; D3 nothing shown when Nostr fails.
  - `packages/robosats`: `OutgoingNostrChatMessage.compose` builds the two NIP-59 gift wraps of `sendToNostr` (README, « The chat over Nostr »), byte for byte as `nostr-tools` 2.25.2 (checked once against it, PR of #94; the official client locks it at RoboSats commit ff1998c and 2.26.0 at commit babf62e, whose code for these wraps, NIP-44 and the events' ids and signatures is the same (read in `nbd-wtf/nostr-tools` at `2027d15a39b7605e28ca22f54509cb767fd13751`, the first commit whose `package.json` says 2.26.0 with the dependencies the lock gives it (2.26.0 has no tag), against the tag `v2.25.2`; the repository was read, not the package the npm registry serves, which may differ; #174, README, « The chat over Nostr »); nothing is built without both Nostr keys of the order, when the key the order gives for this robot is not its own, or without a relay. `nostrRelaysFor` gives `min(3, n)` relays, the order's coordinator's first (where the reference puts the relay of the coordinator serving its page: a declared deviation), then drawn at random. Primitives in `lib/src/nostr/`, the robot's secret key kept in `NostrKeyPair`, every random byte from the caller's `Random` (`Random.secure()` in the app).
  - `TorCoordinatorTransport.publish` (`NostrRelayTransport`): a websocket `ws://<onion>/relay/` through the robot's session to that origin, with the session's own `HttpClient` and a handshake of the transport's own (security review of #158): no redirect ever followed (`followRedirects = false`, a `3xx` is a failure), no extension offered and any extension or subprotocol the relay answers with refused, so no compression can inflate a message, `Upgrade` and `Connection` checked by exact token and `Sec-WebSocket-Accept` given once and right, every failing branch closing its connection (destroyed once detached, aborted before, the session given up on a timeout or connection error so that a connection still being made is cancelled), each step within what is left of the publication's deadline, one instant computed once, with no retry and no new session once it has passed, so that no connection a publication opened outlives it (reviews of #158), then `WebSocket.fromUpgradedSocket` over a socket whose incoming bytes are counted before `dart:io` assembles a message: at most `maxRelayBytes` (64 KiB) and `maxRelayFrames` (64) messages per publication, beyond which the socket is destroyed and the publication fails; memory held after the handshake is that bound plus one read of the socket, the handshake's head being bounded by `dart:io` at 1 MiB (tested through a real SOCKS5 proxy on the loopback, not yet against a coordinator's relay); the two events sent in order, an `OK` awaited for each (to close cleanly and report only), one retry on a new session after a connection failure, within the request deadline; only a fixed word is logged (`P2P Nostr chat publication: <label>`), never the relay, robot, ids or text.
  - `SendChatMessageUsecase`: the copy starts when the transport starts writing the post's body on a connection it obtained to the coordinator (`CoordinatorRequest.onSending`, given by `CoordinatorClient.postChat`, called by `TorCoordinatorTransport` from Dio's `onSendProgress`), as the reference starts it as it posts, and never when nothing is written: a refusal before sending (a last read outside the chat, say), a transport without a place every time, no session, a connection that fails before the body, a key breach seen while the post waited (reviews of #158). Nothing of the Nostr path, synchronous or not, reaches the post, the screen or the zone: the transport catches whatever the hook throws and the use case whatever the composition or the publication throw, `Error`s included (owner's decision on the security review, an exception to rule 11 for this optional path), letting the copy go and logging a fixed word with the error's type, never its message (`P2P coordinator request hook failed (<type>)`, `P2P Nostr copy failed (<type>)`). A copy still being composed when its message is refused or never sent is not published; one whose message is stored is (second review of #158). It is composed apart (`RobotKeyComputePort.composeNostrChat`, an isolate that receives the robot's Nostr key pair, never its token) and published without being awaited, so it never delays the post; its outcome changes nothing of the message's state and nothing is shown (as the reference). When the coordinator refuses a post it received after its copy was composed, the copy is out, as in the reference; when it accepts the connection and cuts it, the copy leaves and the message shows as « not confirmed »: the screen says the coordinator did not take the message while a peer in the official client's Nostr view may read it, and a message typed again shows twice there (declared, nothing built against it). « Send again » publishes the same events again only when no relay accepted them, and composes the copy when the first send could not (`TradeChat.nostrCopyComposing`, `keepNostrCopy`, `nostrCopyToPublish`); a copy is let go once its message is stored, refused or never sent. Reading stays on the API: the official client never sends a message over Nostr alone.
  - The trade's Nostr keys are pinned (security review of #158; the official client pins nothing): the peer's and this robot's keys are pinned with the PGP pin, in memory, when the trade's first post leaves with both keys valid, synchronously, before anything is composed, so that two messages composed at once cannot go to two keys (`TradeChat.admitNostrKeys`, checked again before publishing); if a later read of the order gives another key, no Nostr copy is published for the trade any more (`P2P Nostr copy not sent: a Nostr key of the trade changed`), and the PGP path goes on as before; no screen says it. It covers a coordinator substituting a key during the trade; not one that substitutes it from the first read (the protocol ties no Nostr key to the peer's PGP identity, so the PGP pin cannot vouch for it), It is kept across a restart (part of #159): the two keys are stored with the trade's record, in the table `trade_chat_nostr_pins` of the same schema 6 (`StoredTradeChatNostrPins`, the same triggers and the same only writer as `trade_chat_pins`), before the trade's first copy is published (`TradeChat.nostrPinToKeep`, written in `SendChatMessageUsecase._publishOverNostr`: no copy leaves while they are not kept, a copy whose write failed is let go and the next one tries again, other keys found kept stop the copies), and read with the PGP pin once a run (`TradeChat.restorePin`), so that a key the order changes after a restart stops the copies as within one run. They are not secret: public keys that the coordinator gives both parties and that each gift wrap names in the clear to the relays. What the user sees when a Nostr key changes is unchanged (nothing): that part of #159 stays open.
  - Deviations from the reference, declared: the copy published only once the post leaves for the coordinator (the reference publishes before posting, whatever follows), and still published when the coordinator then refuses the post; the order's coordinator's relay first in the pool; the `OK`s awaited; nothing published when the coordinator holds another Nostr key for the robot (the peer would filter it out anyway); the same events on « Send again »; the seal and wrap dated at the current second rounded down, never in the future (`nostr-tools` rounds, up to half a second ahead); images out of scope. A phone clock more than 15 minutes ahead gets the events refused by strfry (`rejectEventsNewerThanSeconds = 900`); nothing in the app says so.

The reference's `EncryptedApiChat`, `ChatHeader` and `MessageCard` at commit ff1998c, in the wallet's look:
  - The trade screen shows it at 9 and 10 (`Chatting`, prompt `chat`) through `TradeScreen(chat: ChatPanelBuilder?)`, in the chat step's place for it (`ChatStepPanel.chat`, #95), between the prompt's text and its buttons, keyed `trade-chat-panel`; the route gives it with one `ChatCubit` per shown trade (`P2pRouter.tradePage`), closed with the page. The prompt of the chat step, its helper lines, « Beware scams » and its buttons are #95's (`ChatStepPanel`), and the panel shows none of them; its keys are `p2pEnd…`, the chat's `p2pChat…`.
  - What it shows is what the reference's chat component shows, and nothing of the prompt: « You: connected / disconnected » (a peer's key received, `connected={Boolean(peerPubKey)}`), « Peer: connected / disconnected / ...waiting », the messages in a box of the reference's height (18.42 em), scrolled to the end when one comes, each with the author's avatar, seeded by its hash id as the Garage and the order seed it, and its dot (green while connected), the nickname, the tick or the cross of its signature with the reference's tooltip (« Verified signature by … », « Cannot verify signature of … »), a copy of the text on the user's tap, the cards of the taker and of the maker in two colours of the theme (its `info` and `tertiary`, where the reference has a blue and a purple of its own); the signature is read once by a screen reader (the tooltip's words), and a card is one stop for it; the field with the reference's label « Type a message » above it (three lines) and « Send » beside it, under it at a large text size, off while the peer's key is not known with the reference's tooltip « Waiting for peer... ». No time on a card, as in the reference. A message's text is plain text in a forced left-to-right direction: no markup or Markdown, no link, URI or invoice made tappable, the reference's image metadata never read, and a copy gives the cleaned text, without hidden characters (review of #93).
  - The app's own lines, each in words that say what happened or what is awaited: the first read until one succeeds or fails (« Reading the chat from … »; the robot key's unlock it may hold is not said apart), the wait of a send or of a message sent again (« Sending the message to … »), a message whose post had no known outcome kept in its place with « Not confirmed by …. It may still reach your peer. » and « Send again », which only the user's tap sends (never a read, never a timer), a refusal before sending (too long, a Garage Key, the token in the reference's alert, which also empties the field as the reference does), a refusal by the coordinator, a read that failed (the chat as it was stays shown), a key that changed or is not the peer's, in one wording that says what to do (« This chat is no longer secure: your peer's key changed. Nothing more is sent here. Do not trust payment details received from now on. », also the greyed « Send »'s tooltip; a pointer to « Open Dispute » comes with #95's options dialog), a history too long with « Read only the newer messages » (which, on an order read before those messages, waits for a newer read of the order, « Reading the newer messages… », then skips, and only while the panel is shown), the marks of `ChatEntry` (a message may be missing before, shown again, sent twice by this robot, « This message may be older than it looks. »), « Waiting for peer... » and « This message cannot be read. » in a card's place, « Messages are not sent at this step. », « Earlier messages are not shown. », and the robot texts' « busy » line reused for a busy coordinator. The lines that say what an action gave are read out when they appear.
  - Screen capture: the panel takes `PrivacyScreen` as the Garage does and draws nothing until the protection says it is on, and its cubit reads the chat and holds the key only once it is on (a refusal leaves both off, « Retry » starts them); without it, « Screen protection could not be turned on, so the chat stays hidden. » and « Retry » (its own gate, inline, since `BullScreenPrivacyGate` builds a whole page). No new dependency.
  - `ChatCubit` is the only caller of the chat's use cases and, for the chat, of the key's hold (`trade_screen_architecture_test.dart`), given their members by the locator. While active (the panel shown, the app resumed, its tab of the shell visible: `TickerMode`) it holds the robot's key (`HoldTradeKeyUsecase`) and reads at once and every 5 s, the reference's `CHAT_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`, one read at a time; otherwise neither. `send` sends the field's text as it is at the tap, once, while « Send » is off; a text typed during the send is kept. Its state's `toString` shows no message and no text typed.
  - « Audit Chat » and « Export » are the reference's buttons of « Order options » (`Prompts/Chat.tsx`), the dialog of the chat step's « … », which is #95's: the chat module gives them as `ChatAuditButton` and `ChatExportButton`, which that dialog places first, « Audit Chat » then « Export », as the reference does (#157): the route gives the trade screen `chatOptionsOf` with the chat panel, both over the one `ChatCubit` the trade page provides, so that there is no second watch of the chat and no second decryption (once the panel has read the chat while shown, its cubit holds the robot's unlocked key, and the export asks the coordinator nothing; otherwise the key's lease reads the robot back, as every lease does), and the cubit's life is unchanged (it reads only while the chat panel is shown, and closes with the page); « Order options » opens only at the chat step, 9 and 10, the reference's only place for it (`TradeBox/index.tsx:538-545`), and without the chat (the feature holding no robot) it shows neither. They show and export what the reference's do, the owner's choice (2026-11-15: « Comme RoboSats, on invente le moins possible »): `ExportChatUsecase` (the only file of the feature but the Garage Key's fingerprint that reads a robot's token, called by `ChatCubit` only, on the user's tap, the robot's key leased for the encrypted private key and the lease closed before it returns) gives `TradeChat.export`, the reference's `{credentials: {own_public_key, peer_public_key, encrypted_private_key, passphrase}, messages}`, the passphrase being the robot's token: whoever holds it can act as the robot at its coordinator. « Export » copies it to the clipboard, indented, as the reference's mobile client does (`utils/saveFile.ts`); « Audit Chat » opens « Don't trust, verify » (`Dialogs/AuditPGP.tsx`, its PGP tab): the reference's text and its four fields with their tooltips and a copy, this robot's public key, the pinned peer's, the encrypted private key and « Your private key passphrase (keep secure!) », then « Keys » and « Messages », which copy their JSON, compact, as the reference's mobile client does.

the export's `plainTextMessage` is the cleaned text, an unreadable message is exported with a null text, and `time` is written again in ISO 8601 (the reference keeps the coordinator's string); `peer_public_key` is left out before the peer's key is known, as the reference leaves it undefined, and after the peer's key changed it is the pinned key, where the reference exports the last key the coordinator gave (`EncryptedApiChat/index.tsx:141`); an « Export » that cannot have the key, or whose copy the clipboard refuses, says so under the button, with the wait said meanwhile, and an « Export » left while it waits (« Order options » closed) copies nothing, so that the token never replaces later what the user copied meanwhile (security review of #161); the export copied is not flagged sensitive (Flutter's `Clipboard.setData` cannot set `EXTRA_IS_SENSITIVE`): from Android 13 the system shows a preview of its start at each copy, and a keyboard with a clipboard history may keep it, the gap stated for the Garage Key above; the token copied stays on the clipboard, as in the reference; the panel and that dialog protected from screen capture, drawing nothing until the protection is on; nothing of either logged; the link « Learn how to verify » not shown (the trade screens open no link); « Send » has a spoken name (« Send »), the reference's button an icon only; the French of the texts the reference leaves in English (« Waiting for peer... », the signature tooltips, « Audit Chat », « Don't trust, verify », the token's alert) is ours. Out of scope: images (the coordinator's Blossom), Nostr (#94), dispute statements, notifications.
- `packages/robosats` adds a second cryptographic dependency, recorded here as the custody policy requires, with the owner's agreement (2026-11-15, #94): `bip340` 0.3.0 (MIT, by fiatjaf, the author of Nostr; pure Dart on `pointycastle` and `crypto`, already in the workspace) for the BIP-340 signatures of Nostr events, pinned exactly, called only through `Bip340` (`lib/src/nostr/schnorr.dart`), whose tests replay the official BIP-340 vectors. Not 0.3.1, which requires `pointycastle` ^4 while `bip85_entropy` and `dart_pg` hold the workspace on ^3; the code of `lib/` is identical in both; move to 0.3.1 with `pointycastle` 4. Neither audited nor constant-time (`BigInt`), like the rest of the package's secp256k1 code.
- `packages/robosats` adds one cryptographic dependency, recorded here as the custody policy requires (2026-11-10): `pinenacl` 0.6.0 (MIT, pure-Dart TweetNaCl port) for Ed25519 and X25519. It is pure Dart, so the reproducible build is unaffected; it is neither audited nor constant-time. `dart_pg`, `sign_dart` and `sha3` were tried and removed after the security review: keep them out.
- The OpenPGP layer in `packages/robosats/lib/src/pgp/` is hand-written and provisional: a Rust library replaces it before any release. Until then, every input it reads stays bounded by `lib/src/pgp/limits.dart` and every public method returns a typed failure on hostile input. The library that replaces it must refuse, for the proof of a robot's key pair, any S2K but the iterated and salted one, as `ProtectedSecret.provesPassphraseWith` does: a simple S2K derives the hash of the passphrase alone (over SHA-256, the token hash every coordinator holds, so a coordinator could lock a key of its own under it) and a salted one a single hash of it; the tests « a key pair the coordinator locked itself » of `test/coordinator/robot_test.dart` must keep passing. A change there is validated by rerunning `packages/robosats/tool/pgp_interop.sh` against GnuPG and OpenPGP.js (`OPENPGPJS=<path to openpgp.min.js>`) and committing the fixtures it rewrites. Reasons and evidence: `docs/spikes/pgp.md`.
- **The RoboSats reference.** The protocol is reimplemented from RoboSats/robosats at one reference commit: `babf62e2238f262182d0bf638496aaa53e286853` (2026-11-15), cited as "commit babf62e", since the refresh before phase 3 (#174). It is not a release: its `version.json` says 0.8.8, but the tag `v0.8.8-alpha` (published on 2026-11-08, still the latest release on 2026-11-16) points to `e56f9cf7c`. Until #174 the reference was `ff1998caed30888b2fb60e15022c29063a60f1ba` (2026-11-06), cited as "commit ff1998c" in the code comments, tests, fixtures and reports written before: the review of #174 found no protocol change in the 27 commits from it to babf62e, and nothing changed in between under `chat/`, `robosats/`, `frontend/src/utils/` and `frontend/src/pgp/`, nor any code under `api/` (only `api/federation.json`, the federation list, whose entries are the ones embedded since `e56f9cf7c`, below). In `frontend/package-lock.json`, the official client moves from `nostr-tools` 2.25.2 to 2.26.0, whose code for the chat's gift wraps, NIP-44 and the events' ids and signatures is that of 2.25.2 (read in `nbd-wtf/nostr-tools` at `2027d15a39b7605e28ca22f54509cb767fd13751`, the first commit whose `package.json` says 2.26.0 with the dependencies the lock gives it (2.26.0 has no tag), against the tag `v2.25.2`; the repository was read, not the package the npm registry serves, which may differ; `packages/robosats/README.md`, « The chat over Nostr »); `openpgp` (5.11.3), `@noble/*`, `@scure/*`, `base-ex`, `js-sha256` and `robo-identities-wasm` keep their versions. What changed that Troc cites: `frontend/src/models/Federation.model.ts` (one line added at line 99, a hook for the relay chip: a line number from 99 on cited at ff1998c is one more at babf62e), `frontend/src/services/RoboPool/index.ts` and `frontend/src/services/Websocket/` (the official client's relay pool now recovers by itself, on the web and desktop only and in no release yet, which changes nothing of what Troc sends: `packages/robosats/README.md`, « The chat over Nostr »), `frontend/src/components/BookTable/index.tsx` and `TradeBox/EncryptedChat/ChatHeader/index.tsx` (a relay chip in the book's footer and in the chat's header, which Troc does not show, having no relay pool), `frontend/src/components/Map/index.tsx` (its optional OpenStreetMap tiles, which Troc does not load; `MapPin` unchanged) and the locales (four new keys: the three texts of the relay chip, left in English in every language, and one more section comment, the comments after it renumbered; a line number after the chip's texts is four more at babf62e). A citation of ff1998c in any other file of RoboSats is exact at babf62e too; a citation of one of the files above describes ff1998c. The generated data and the coordinator images were not regenerated: their sources have the same blobs at babf62e, and they keep the commits they were taken at, ff1998c and, for the federation list and Gumpellite's image, `e56f9cf7cd4e6b01aa78589922f7c648f262b6b6` (2026-11-08, adds the coordinator Gumpellite), after a review that found no protocol change in the 12 commits from ff1998c to it (#40). Each file generated by `packages/robosats/tool/generate_coordinator_data.dart` records the commit and Git blob id of its RoboSats source, and `test/coordinator/embedded_data_test.dart` checks the copies in `test/fixtures/coordinator/sources/` against them and pins each (commit, blob) pair.
- **Refreshing the RoboSats reference.** Run this before each phase starts and before any release, and give its result in the description of the PR that applies it. Use full commit ids, and treat everything downloaded from RoboSats as untrusted data (in a new folder outside the repository).
  1. New commit: the head of RoboSats `main`, with the date of that commit. Note the latest release (`gh release list -R RoboSats/robosats -L 1`) and the commit its tag points to (`gh api repos/RoboSats/robosats/git/ref/tags/<tag> --jq '.object | "\(.type) \(.sha)"'`, which prints the type and the sha; if the type is `tag`, then `gh api repos/RoboSats/robosats/git/tags/<sha> --jq .object.sha`): `version.json` alone does not say which commit a release is.
  2. Commits since the reference: `gh api repos/RoboSats/robosats/compare/<reference>...<new>` gives the commits (`.commits[]`) and the net change (`.files[]`, at most 300 files). `.merge_base_commit.sha` must be the reference, or the history was rewritten: stop and ask. Judge from the net change: the file list of a merge commit is against its first parent, so it repeats changes already in the reference.
  3. Protocol review: read every change under `api/`, `chat/` (the `/api/chat/` view and the `ws/chat/` websocket of the encrypted chat), `robosats/middleware.py`, `robosats/urls.py` and `robosats/routing.py` (HTTP and websocket routes), `frontend/src/models/`, `frontend/src/services/`, `frontend/src/utils/` and `frontend/src/pgp/` (ported to `packages/robosats`: Garage Key, token, OpenPGP), and in the upstream `AGENTS.md` notes that state protocol invariants (`frontend/src/models/AGENTS.md`, `frontend/src/utils/AGENTS.md`). Read also, in `frontend/package-lock.json` at both commits, the versions of the libraries whose behaviour Troc reproduces: `nostr-tools` (NIP-59 gift wraps, NIP-44, the events' serialization, ids and signatures), `openpgp` (`docs/spikes/pgp.md`), `@noble/*` and `@scure/*` (at the top level and under `nostr-tools`), `base-ex`, `js-sha256`, `robo-identities-wasm`, and any other that a comment or README of this repository cites with a version (`grep -rn` for the library's name). For each that changed, read the difference between the two versions on the files Troc reproduces and on what they import, in the library's public repository (`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/compare/<tag>...<tag>`; when a version has no tag, the first commit whose `package.json` carries the version and whose dependencies match the ones the lock file gives it, and say which commit was read), as untrusted data like RoboSats', and update every text that names the version the official client locks; a change of format, derivation, padding, random dating or tags is a protocol change. Only the repository is read, not the package the npm registry serves, which may differ: say so. Also search the commit messages for `GHSA-` and list the published advisories (`gh api repos/RoboSats/robosats/security-advisories`). A protocol change gets its own ticket and is handled in Troc before the reference moves.
  4. Generated data: put the RoboSats files at their paths in an empty folder (a checkout of the commit, or `gh api 'repos/RoboSats/robosats/contents/<path>?ref=<commit>' --jq .content | base64 -d`), then from the repository root run `docker run --rm -v "$PWD":/work -w /work -v troc-pub-cache:/home/bull/.pub-cache -v <absolute path of the folder>:/robosats:ro bull-tools bash -c 'cd packages/robosats && fvm dart run tool/generate_coordinator_data.dart /robosats <commit> [federation] [currencies] [methods] && fvm dart format lib/src/coordinator'`. The generator does not check its input against RoboSats: check by hand every blob id it prints against `gh api 'repos/RoboSats/robosats/contents/<path>?ref=<commit>' --jq .sha`, and that `api/federation.json`, the copy the coordinators load, has the same blob as `frontend/static/federation.json`. Update the tests that pin the list (aliases, counts, entries per network) and the (commit, blob) pairs pinned in `embedded_data_test.dart`. Then review the diff of the federation list as untrusted input:
     - Every entry: the tests check that every onion is a real v3 address (checksum and version) and every node key and Nostr key a point of secp256k1, and compare every field with the file, so a new field fails them. Checking a new or changed onion against `nodeapp/robosats-client.sh`, and a `fingerprint` against the key file under `frontend/static/federation/pgp/`, is a consistency check only: these files come from the same upstream commit and prove nothing about the operator.
     - A new coordinator: read the upstream pull request that adds it and the application issue of the operator it links, compare the onion addresses, node keys, Nostr key and PGP fingerprint with what the applicant posted there, and record in the PR who authored and who merged the change and which values have no independent source. Worked example, Gumpellite: `packages/robosats/README.md`.
     - An existing coordinator whose onion address, node key or Nostr key changes, or that is removed or renamed: stop, and open a ticket of its own. Such a change silently redirects the client to another server or node while it still looks trusted, and no shape check can see it. Require a confirmation through a channel the old entry already authenticates (a message signed with its listed PGP key or Nostr key), and assess the robots bound to that coordinator under the rule "one robot, one coordinator".
     - A new coordinator has no recorded responses: tests build its answers, and recording them is a separate step that contacts the live federation (`test/fixtures/coordinator/README.md`).
     - Coordinator images: a new coordinator's image (`frontend/static/federation/avatars/<alias>.webp`, at the commit of the list) goes into `features/p2p/assets/coordinators/` with its row in `features/p2p/lib/src/ui/widgets/coordinator_images.dart` and its pin in `features/p2p/test/coordinator_images_test.dart`, its blob checked against RoboSats like the generated files', and its RIFF chunks checked (size, no trailing bytes, no animation or metadata). A coordinator of the list without an image keeps its drawn mark; that is a decision to record by updating the test « every coordinator of the embedded list has its image », which fails until then. A changed image of an existing coordinator is reviewed by eye before its pin moves, and a removed coordinator's image is deleted.
  5. Record the result: update the reference commit and its date in this section and in `packages/robosats/README.md`. A comment or report that cites the reference describes code read at that commit: change it to the new commit only where that code was read again at the new commit, and otherwise say which commit each part comes from, as above. Search for the commits cited today, `grep -rn -e babf62e -e ff1998c -e e56f9cf7c` (the federation commit is cited in `federation.dart`, `packages/robosats/README.md`, the fixtures README and `embedded_data_test.dart`, and with ff1998c, the commit of the other data and images, in `coordinator_images.dart`, `coordinator_images_test.dart` and `NOTICE.md`). The onion list of `integration_test/robosats_tor_test.dart`, the phase-0 Tor probe, is frozen at `ff1998c` on purpose: do not refresh it.
- **Watch item: the robot bearer.** RoboSats introduced a token per coordinator on 2026-10-06 ("Fix GHSA-22q8-9ghc-jg3h", merged on 2026-10-07) and reverted it on 2026-10-15, and that advisory is not published (checked on 2026-11-11, and again on 2026-11-16 for #174: nothing between ff1998c and babf62e brings it back). So at the reference the token hash is the same on every coordinator, and Troc's rule stays one robot index bound to one coordinator (above, and `docs/spikes/robot-coordinator.md`). A per-coordinator token coming back upstream is a protocol change for step 3.
